\begin{abstract}
The GeoVac framework has, over Papers~0--31, accumulated a body of
structural results---the $S^3$ Fock-projection equivalence
(Paper~7~\cite{paper7}), the angular sparsity theorem
(Paper~22~\cite{paper22}), the Hopf-graph lattice-gauge structure
(Paper~25~\cite{paper25}), QED on $S^3$ (Paper~28~\cite{paper28}),
the Bargmann--Segal $S^5$ HO lattice (Paper~24~\cite{paper24}), the
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Wilson gauge (Paper~30~\cite{paper30}), and the
universal/Coulomb partition (Paper~31~\cite{paper31})---each
self-contained but together suggesting one parent structure.
\textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]} This
paper names and constructs that structure: a finite spectral triple
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ on the Fock-projected $S^3$ graph, with
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ the algebra of complex functions on graph
nodes, $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ the Camporesi--Higuchi spinor space
restricted to the truncation, and $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ the diagonal
Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac operator~\cite{camporesi_higuchi1996} on the
truncated spinor space of unit~$S^3$ (no graph form is attached to
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$; Remark~\ref{rem:D_GV_no_graph_form}).  This is not a
new derivation; it is a structural map.  Three results follow.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]}
(1)~\emph{Lineage.}~The construction sits inside the published
Marcolli--van~Suijlekom gauge-network framework~\cite{marcolli_vs2014}
(with the Perez-Sanchez correction~\cite{perez_sanchez2025} that the
continuum limit is Yang--Mills without Higgs), and inside the
Connes--van~Suijlekom spectral-truncation
program~\cite{connes_vs2021,hekkelman2022,hekkelman_mcdonald2024,vansuijlekom2024_ksystems}.  Every
structural ingredient of the GeoVac construction has published
precedent; what is not duplicated is the $\alpha$ observation
($K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ matches $\alpha^{-1}$ to $4.8 \times 10^{-7}$;\
the root of the cubic $1/\alpha = K - \alpha^2$ matches to
$8.8 \times 10^{-8}$;\ Paper~2~\cite{paper2}) from a discrete spectral
construction.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]}
(2)~\emph{Sub-sector identification.}  Paper~28's QED on~$S^3$ is the
spectral-action computation on the spectrum of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$;
Paper~31's universal/Coulomb partition is the $\mathcal{A}/D$ split of
the present triple's spectral data.  Paper~25's Hodge-1 Laplacian and
Paper~30's $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Wilson action are read as the Cartan-torus
and full non-abelian slices of an almost-commutative extension
by~$M_2(\mathbb{C})$, but through the gauge-network (edge-hopping)
Dirac of those papers, \emph{not} through $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$:\ the
diagonal $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ commutes with every $\pi(f)$ and so admits
no inner fluctuations.  The four papers are read as four projections
of one structure, at the level of spectral and algebraic data.
\textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]}
(3)~\emph{Axiom audit.}  $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is finite,
involutive, faithfully represented on $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$;
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is self-adjoint, and since $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ and
every $\pi(a)$ are diagonal in the same basis,
$[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)] = 0$ identically:\ the diagonal triple is a
\emph{spectral}, not a metric, object (the metric role belongs to the
non-diagonal multipliers of the truncated operator system on the
\emph{true} Camporesi--Higuchi window --- the span of complete Dirac
eigenspaces, Paper~38~\cite{loutey_paper38} --- whose state spaces are
proved to converge under the left-translation Lipschitz seminorm;
convergence in the Dirac-commutator seminorm is \textbf{[OPEN]}, that
seminorm being non-degenerate on the window only as measured at
$n_{\max} \le 3$).  A
real structure $J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ exists on the Dirac sector with
$J_{\mathrm{GV}}^2 = -\mathbb{1}$ (the quaternionic/pseudoreal sign
for the 2-dimensional Dirac spinor representation of
$\mathrm{Spin}(3) = \mathrm{SU}(2)$) and $J_{\mathrm{GV}} D_{\mathrm{GV}}
= +D_{\mathrm{GV}} J_{\mathrm{GV}}$, the signs
$(\epsilon, \epsilon') = (-1, +1)$ of the continuum $S^3$ factor
($\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~$3$ mod~$8$~\cite{connes1995}).  The continuum
factor is odd, and so is the true Camporesi--Higuchi window
(measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$:\ the commutant of its operator
system is the scalars), the truncation that converges; only
the chirality-doubled Fock/Weyl \emph{model} truncation is even, via the
chirality-doubling grading $\gamma_{\mathrm{GV}} = \sigma_x \otimes
\mathbb{1}$, with sign triple $(-,+,+)$ and no $\mathrm{KO}$ label
attached at finite cutoff.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} On the Fock/Weyl
\emph{model} of the operator system (the computational object of this
paper, in its Weyl and chirality-doubled forms) the order-zero and order-one conditions
fail at finite $n_{\max}$, with residuals that \emph{grow} over the
measured cutoffs (order zero at $n_{\max} = 2, 3, 4$, order one at
$n_{\max} = 2, 3$; mechanism open).  \textbf{[MEASURED]} On the true
Camporesi--Higuchi window that converges, the charge conjugation
$J\psi = (i\sigma_y)\overline{\psi}$ satisfies $J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$,
$JD = +DJ$ and $J\mathcal{O}J^{-1} = \mathcal{O}$ exactly, while order
zero and order one fail from $n_{\max} = 1$ with residuals that grow
toward the sup-norm scale of the generators;\ there every residual is
a Toeplitz boundary term of the compression, confined to the top Dirac
levels of the window.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The four-way $S^3$ coincidence---Fock projection image,
Hopf bundle total space $\sthree$ (base $S^2$), Camporesi--Higuchi spin carrier,
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ gauge manifold---is the statement that the manifold
reached by the operator-system truncations in the continuum limit, the
spinor bundle of the Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac, and the gauge group of
the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ almost-commutative extension (Papers~25/30) all
coincide on the unique rank-1 non-abelian compact simply-connected Lie
group.

\noindent\textbf{Scope.}  This paper does not derive new physics.  It
synthesizes existing GeoVac results into the axiomatic spectral-triple
language.  The synthesis lifts each result from a free-standing
observation to a sub-sector of a single named structure, and makes
explicit which axioms hold rigorously, which hold up to the standard
finite-truncation caveats, and which remain open.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]}
The real-structure question---whether the GeoVac Dirac operator $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ (the diagonal spectral form) carries a real
structure matching the signs of $S^3$ exactly at finite $n_{\max}$ or
only in the continuum limit---is resolved in favour of
finite-$n_{\max}$ exactness \emph{for the three $J$-relations}
$J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$, $JD = +DJ$, $J\mathcal{O}J^{-1} = \mathcal{O}$
only, measured on the Fock/Weyl model objects and, independently, on
the true Camporesi--Higuchi window.  \textbf{[MEASURED]}
The order-zero and order-one conditions fail at finite $n_{\max}$ with
residuals that grow with the cutoff, on the Fock/Weyl model of the
operator system and on the true window alike (on the true window as a
boundary term of the compression).  The Fock/Weyl model truncation is
even (chirality doubling; sign triple $(-,+,+)$, no $\mathrm{KO}$
label);\ the true window is odd (measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$), like
the continuum (its operator system generates all of $B(\mathcal{H})$,
so it admits no grading), and
there the pair $(\varepsilon, \varepsilon') = (-,+)$ is forced by
equivariance;\ the residual open
items are catalogued in Sec.~\ref{sec:open}.
\end{abstract}

% ===================================================================
\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:intro}
% ===================================================================

\subsection{Why a spectral triple, and why now}

Across the thirty-two papers (0--31) preceding this one, the GeoVac framework
has carried four distinct strands.  Strand~1 is the Fock projection
$p_0 = \sqrt{-2E_n}$ that maps the hydrogenic Hamiltonian onto a function of the
Laplace--Beltrami operator~$\Delta$ on the unit three-sphere,
$H = -\tfrac{Z^2}{2}(1-\Delta)^{-1}$, where $\Delta$ has continuum eigenvalues
$\lambda_n = -(n^2 - 1)$; the graph nodes carry the quantum-number
labels $(n, l, m_l)$, not that spectrum (Paper~7).  Strand~2 is the Hopf bundle
$S^1 \to S^3 \to S^2$ that organizes the discrete edge structure into
ladder operators with $U(1)$ phase content (Papers~2, 25).  Strand~3
is the Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac spectrum
$|\lambda_n^{\mathrm{D}}| = n + 3/2$ with degeneracy
$g_n^{\mathrm{D}} = 2(n+1)(n+2)$ that controls QED on $S^3$
(Paper~28).  Strand~4 is the Wilson lattice gauge structure with
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ link variables on the same graph (Paper~30).

Each strand uses the unit three-sphere in a different role:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item[(a)] $S^3$ as the \emph{conformal image} of the hydrogenic
spectrum (Paper~7);
\item[(b)] $S^3$ as the \emph{total space} of the Hopf bundle on which
Paper~2's $\alpha$ Observation is phrased;
\item[(c)] $S^3$ as the \emph{spin carrier} of the Camporesi--Higuchi
Dirac operator (Paper~28);
\item[(d)] $S^3$ as the \emph{gauge manifold} of $\mathrm{SU}(2)$
Wilson links (Paper~30).
\end{itemize}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Working hypothesis~WH4 in the project register flags this as the
strangest and most consequential coincidence in the framework: $S^3 =
\mathrm{SU}(2)$ is the unique rank-1 non-abelian compact simply
connected Lie group where (a)--(d) all coincide; in any other
dimension or any other rank, the four roles separate.  WH4 takes the
position, in its current (deflated) form, that the four strands are not
four coincidences but one Fock-projection statement plus three forced
consequences of $S^3 = \mathrm{SU}(2)$ (its parallelizability and
maximal-torus structure).

This paper makes WH4 explicit.  We give a concrete construction of a
finite spectral triple
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:construction}) --- a spectral, not
metric, object, since its diagonal $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ commutes with the
diagonal algebra, the metric role belonging to the operator-system
representative on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window (Paper~38);
audit the standard Connes axioms one by one, separating
what holds at finite cutoff (the three $J$-relations; oddness on
the true window, which the chirality-doubled model replaces by an even
grading) from what holds only in the continuum limit (order zero and
order one, which fail at finite cutoff on the Fock/Weyl
model of the operator system and on the true window) (Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit}); read
Papers~25, 28, 30, and~31 as structural sub-sectors of the resulting
structure (Sec.~\ref{sec:subsectors}); read the Paper~2 combination
formula $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ inside the spectral-action language
(Sec.~\ref{sec:K_decomposition}); and place the Coulomb/HO asymmetry
of Paper~31 in spectral-triple terms (Sec.~\ref{sec:coulomb_ho}).

\subsection{What this paper is and is not}

\paragraph{What it is.}  A synthesis paper.  The construction is
explicit, finite, and reproducible from existing GeoVac modules
(\texttt{geovac/lattice.py}, \texttt{geovac/dirac\_matrix\_elements.py},
\texttt{geovac/fock\_graph\_hodge.py}); the lineage citations are to
published mathematical work; the audit of the Connes axioms uses
standard textbook definitions~\cite{connes1995,connes_marcolli2008,%
vansuijlekom_book2015}.

\paragraph{What it is not.}  A derivation of $\alpha$.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Paper~2 stays
where it is: a structural observation with three spectral
homes of distinct type for the three pieces $B$, $F$, $\Delta$ but no first-principles
derivation of the combination rule.  This paper does not change that
status; it gives the spectral-action language in which the open
question is phrased without promoting it beyond that observation.

\paragraph{What it is not, second.}  A claim of the
Connes--Chamseddine Standard Model.  The GeoVac construction is a
\emph{rank-1} non-abelian almost-commutative extension; the SM
spectral triple of \cite{chamseddine_connes1997_2008} is rank~$\ge 3$ with
specific finite-algebra content $\mathbb{C} \oplus \mathbb{H} \oplus
M_3(\mathbb{C})$.  GeoVac is not the Standard Model.  It is a finite
spectral triple that is an \emph{instance} of Marcolli--van Suijlekom 2014~\cite{marcolli_vs2014} (with Perez-Sanchez 2025 continuum-limit correction~\cite{perez_sanchez2025}) at the data level and an \emph{extension} at the structural level (the $S^3$ label set singled out by Bertrand $\times$ Fock projection, master Mellin engine, explicit Hopf algebra $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$, Tannakian dual $U^*$), \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} with the empirically interesting
property that a combination of three invariants drawn from different
sectors of the triple, $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$, satisfies, after subtraction of the leading
Sprint-A correction~$\alpha^2$, a residual
$K - 1/\alpha - \alpha^2 \approx 1.2 \times 10^{-5}$ (a post-correction
relative residual of $8.8\times 10^{-8}$) whose absolute size matches
$\pi^3\alpha^3$ to within 0.25\% with CODATA~2018 $\alpha$ --- with
CODATA~2022 it sits $0.52\%$ below, so the agreement is not stable at
this level across editions (Remark~\ref{rem:sprint_A}) (the raw match
$|K - 1/\alpha|/(1/\alpha)$ is $4.77 \times 10^{-7}$;\ CODATA~2022 gives
$4.76 \times 10^{-7}$ raw and $8.75 \times 10^{-8}$ for the cubic's root).

\paragraph{Internal register.}  This paper makes explicit the
spectral-triple framing already operating informally in the working
hypothesis register (kept as \S 1.7 of the repository's \texttt{CLAUDE.md}; the sprint, track and door codenames used throughout this paper are chronicle anchors into that file and the repository \texttt{CHANGELOG.md}; WH1's commutative-factor leg --- state-space convergence of the round-$S^3$ truncations on the true window --- proved unconditionally as of 2026-06-10, Paper~38~\cite{loutey_paper38};\ convergence of the almost-commutative structure is \textbf{[OPEN]}).
Papers continue under the rhetoric rule of \S 1.5: dual-description
framing, no ontological priority claims, lead with computational
results.

% ===================================================================
\section{The Connes Data Recalled}
\label{sec:connes_data}
% ===================================================================

A spectral triple is a triple $(\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{H}, D)$
where~\cite{connes1995}
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item $\mathcal{A}$ is an involutive (unital) algebra,
\item $\mathcal{H}$ is a Hilbert space carrying a faithful
$*$-representation $\pi: \mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})$,
\item $D : \mathcal{H} \to \mathcal{H}$ is an unbounded self-adjoint
operator with compact resolvent, such that the commutators
$[D, \pi(a)]$ are bounded for every $a \in \mathcal{A}$.
\end{itemize}
The triple is called \emph{even} if there is a self-adjoint involution
$\gamma$ commuting with $\pi(\mathcal{A})$ and anticommuting with $D$;
otherwise \emph{odd}.  A \emph{real structure} on the triple is an
antilinear isometry $J: \mathcal{H} \to \mathcal{H}$ with
$J^2 = \epsilon\,\mathbb{1}$, $JD = \epsilon'\,DJ$,
$J\gamma = \epsilon''\,\gamma J$ (the signs $(\epsilon,\epsilon',
\epsilon'')$ are determined by the $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension
mod~$8$~\cite{connes1995}); for the canonical Riemannian spin
manifold of dimension $n$, the $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension is $n$ mod~$8$
and the signs are tabulated.

The two structural axioms of central interest below are:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(\textbf{O1})] \emph{Order one:} $[[D, \pi(a)], \pi(b)^{\circ}]
= 0$ for all $a, b \in \mathcal{A}$, where $b^{\circ} = JbJ^{-1}$ is
the right action induced by the real structure;
\item[(\textbf{R})] \emph{Reality:} $J$ exists with the signs above.
\end{itemize}

For an \emph{almost-commutative} spectral triple
$(\mathcal{A}_M \otimes \mathcal{A}_F, \mathcal{H}_M \otimes
\mathcal{H}_F, D_M \otimes \mathbb{1} + \gamma_M \otimes
D_F)$~\cite{vansuijlekom_book2015,chamseddine_connes1997_2008}, the
manifold sector $\mathcal{A}_M = C^{\infty}(M)$ is replaced in our
finite setting by the algebra of complex-valued functions on a finite
graph, with the convergence to $C^{\infty}(M)$ carried by the
true-window operator-system representative of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}
(the diagonal algebra itself is the gauge-network convention; see
Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}).  This
is exactly the move made by Marcolli and van~Suijlekom in their gauge
networks construction~\cite{marcolli_vs2014} and continued by
Perez-Sanchez~\cite{perez_sanchez2025}: replace the
manifold algebra by an algebra on a discrete vertex set, with edges
carrying connection data.  The GeoVac construction sits in this
lineage by design.

% ===================================================================
\section{The GeoVac Triple: Explicit Construction}
\label{sec:construction}
% ===================================================================

Fix a truncation $n_{\max} \ge 1$.  All objects below are finite.

\subsection{The algebra $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$}

Let $V_{\mathrm{Fock}}$ denote the set of Fock-graph nodes at
truncation $n_{\max}$, i.e. quantum-number triples $(n, l, m_l)$ with
$1 \le n \le n_{\max}$, $0 \le l \le n-1$, $-l \le m_l \le l$.  The
total node count is $N_{\mathrm{Fock}}(n_{\max}) = \sum_{n=1}^{n_{\max}}
n^2$.

\begin{definition}[Function algebra on the Fock graph]
\label{def:A_GV}
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})} := \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Fock}}}$,
the commutative $*$-algebra of complex-valued functions on
$V_{\mathrm{Fock}}$ under pointwise multiplication, with involution
$f^* (v) = \overline{f(v)}$.
\end{definition}

This is precisely the manifold algebra of the gauge networks of
\cite{marcolli_vs2014} (their $C(V)$, with $V$ the vertex set), with
the GeoVac-specific choice that $V$ is the Fock-projected $S^3$ graph
of Paper~7 rather than a generic graph.  It is finite-dimensional
($\dim_{\mathbb{C}} \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} = N_{\mathrm{Fock}}$),
unital ($1$~is the constant function~$1$), and abelian.

\begin{remark}[$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ as the diagonal representative of an operator system]
\label{rem:operator_system}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} In the parallel reading of GeoVac through the spectral-truncation
framework of Connes and van~Suijlekom~\cite{connes_vs2021},
Definition~\ref{def:A_GV} describes
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})} = \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Fock}}}$
as the diagonal (gauge-network) representative attached to the operator
system (its $C^*$-envelope is $M_N(\mathbb{C})$, since $\mathrm{prop} = 2$
gives $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}^2 = M_N(\mathbb{C})$)
\[
   \mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}} := P_{n_{\max}} \, C^\infty(S^3) \, P_{n_{\max}},
\]
where $P_{n_{\max}}$ is the index-truncation projecting onto the
span of the first $n_{\max}$ Fock shells (in the spinor-harmonic
basis of $L^2(S^3)$ adapted to the SO(4) decomposition).  This is
structurally identical to the Connes--van~Suijlekom worked example
on $S^1$ (their $P_n$ onto $\mathrm{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\{e_1, e_2,
\ldots, e_n\}$, \cite[\S 3.1]{connes_vs2021}), the spectral
projection $\chi_{[1, n]}(D)$ of the circle Dirac operator;\
$P_{n_{\max}}$ is likewise a spectral projection, onto the first
$n_{\max}$ eigenspaces of the Laplace--Beltrami operator.  The genuine truncated algebra-image
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ is $*$-closed but \emph{not} multiplicatively
closed; it carries nontrivial off-diagonal matrix elements
\[
   \langle (n,l,m_l) | \, f \, | (n', l', m_l') \rangle
   = \int_{S^3} \overline{Y_{n l m_l}}\, f \, Y_{n' l' m_l'}\, dV
\]
between distinct $n$-shells.  These overlap integrals are exactly
the Gaunt~/~Wigner-3j angular machinery and the hypergeometric
Slater radial integrals already implemented throughout the GeoVac
pipeline (Paper~22 angular sparsity; Paper~14 ERI structure).  The
off-diagonal data therefore already exists in the framework;
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ as written in Definition~\ref{def:A_GV}
is its diagonal restriction.

The two readings agree on diagonal elements and on the spectral
data of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$; they differ in what is taken as the
algebraic content surviving the truncation.  Connes and
van~Suijlekom note that taking the $C^*$-envelope of $\mathcal{O}_\Lambda$
typically collapses to a ``non-informative full matrix algebra'' --
the algebraic-structure information lives in the operator-system
data thrown away by the envelope construction.  Constructing
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ explicitly in $\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}})$
and computing its propagation number and Connes distance on
node-evaluation states is the deliverable of the WH1 round-2 sprint
(see \texttt{debug/wh1\_connes\_vs\_mapping\_memo.md}, 2026-05-03).
The present subsection retains the
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} = \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Fock}}}$ definition
because (a) it matches the Marcolli--van~Suijlekom gauge-network
convention used throughout Papers~25 and~30, and (b) it carries the
spectral data (spectrum, degeneracies, real structure).  The two
representatives are \emph{not} interchangeable for axiom-level or
metric content:\ on the diagonal representative $[D_{\mathrm{GV}},
\pi(a)] = 0$, so it carries no metric and the order-zero/one conditions
hold vacuously, whereas on $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ the commutators are
nonzero and the order-zero/one conditions fail at finite $n_{\max}$
(Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit}).

\emph{Which operator system converges.}  The
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ defined above --- the first $n_{\max}$ Fock
shells, and its Weyl and chirality-doubled spinor lifts
(\texttt{SpinorTruncatedOperatorSystem},
\texttt{FullDiracTruncatedOperatorSystem}) --- is a
\emph{computational model}, and every finite-cutoff axiom measurement
in this paper (propagation number, Connes distances, the three
$J$-relations, the order-zero/one residuals) is made on it, except the
true-window real-structure audit (item~6 of
Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit}).  The object
whose continuum limit is proved (Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence},
Paper~38~\cite{loutey_paper38}) is a different one:\ the truncation to
the \emph{true} Camporesi--Higuchi window, the span of the complete
Dirac eigenspaces with $|\lambda| \le n_{\max} + \tfrac12$, whose
operator system $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}$ has dimension
$5, 30, 91$ at $n_{\max} = 1, 2, 3$ (\texttt{tests/test\_p38\_true\_ch\_window.py}).
The doubled model has the same labels and Hilbert-space dimensions but
is not $\mathrm{SU}(2)_L$-invariant, omits the blocks coupling positive
and negative Dirac levels, and reaches only the bands $N \le 2n_{\max}-1$;
its quantities are model quantities, not window quantities (Paper~38,
\S\,Truncations).  On the true window the Dirac-commutator kernel is
exactly the scalars at $n_{\max} \le 3$ (Paper~38,
remark on the Dirac-commutator seminorm), and the real-structure audit is repeated
on it in item~6 of Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit};\ its propagation number
is not measured.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Propagation number of $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$]
\label{prop:propagation_number}
\textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} Let $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}} = P_{n_{\max}} C^\infty(S^3) P_{n_{\max}}$
be the truncated operator system of
Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}, viewed as a $*$-closed subspace of
$M_N(\mathbb{C})$ with $N = N_{\mathrm{Fock}}(n_{\max})$.  The
propagation number in the sense of Connes \& van~Suijlekom
(\cite{connes_vs2021}, Definition~2.39) satisfies
\begin{equation}
   \mathrm{prop}(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) = 2
   \qquad \text{for every } n_{\max} \in \{2, 3, 4\},
   \label{eq:prop_two}
\end{equation}
matching the Toeplitz operator system $C(S^1)^{(n)}$
(\cite{connes_vs2021}, Proposition~4.2: $\mathrm{prop}(C(S^1)^{(n)}) =
2$ independently of $n$) verbatim.  Equivalently,
$\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}^2) = N^2$ while
$\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) < N^2$ strictly:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c}
n_{\max} & N & N^2 & \dim(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) &
   \dim(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}^2) \\ \hline
2 & 5  & 25  & 14  & 25  \\
3 & 14 & 196 & 55  & 196 \\
4 & 30 & 900 & 140 & 900 \\
\end{array}
\]
The dimension fraction $\dim(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) / N^2$ falls
$0.560 \to 0.281 \to 0.156$ across the three truncations: the
operator system is a vanishing fraction of its $C^*$-envelope as
$n_{\max}$ grows, but its second power fills the envelope
completely.  (This is the scalar Fock-shell model of
Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}, not the true Camporesi--Higuchi
window $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}$ of the convergence
theorem, whose propagation number is not measured here.)
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Computational verification]
The full construction and verification is documented in
\texttt{geovac/operator\_system.py} (the \texttt{TruncatedOperatorSystem}
class) and \texttt{tests/test\_operator\_system.py} (24 tests, all
passing; CPU time ${\sim}20$~s).  In summary: a basis of
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ is built from the multiplier matrices
$M_{NLM}$ corresponding to the hyperspherical harmonics
$Y^{(3)}_{NLM}$, with selection rules $|n - n'| + 1 \le N \le n + n' -
1$ (SO(4) triangle), $|l - l'| \le L \le l + l'$ with $l + l' + L$
even (S$^2$ Gaunt parity), $M = m - m'$, and the angular S$^2$ part
evaluated symbolically via \texttt{sympy.physics.wigner.wigner\_3j}.
Linear independence of $\{M_{NLM}\}$ is verified at machine precision;
the propagation number is read off as the smallest $k$ with
$\dim(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}^k) = N^2$.  The result
$\mathrm{prop} = 2$ is robust: it is invariant under the choice of
SO(4) radial-overlap placeholder (verified across three placeholders
including a pure indicator that retains only the support pattern), so
\eqref{eq:prop_two} does not depend on the radial value
convention (it is the generic value, see the remark below).  See \texttt{debug/wh1\_round2\_propagation\_memo.md}
(2026-05-03) for the full computational record, including the explicit
witness pair $a = M_{2,1,0}$ at $n_{\max} = 2$ for which $a^2 \notin
\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ (Frobenius residual $\approx 14.9\%$), with
the deficit concentrated on the top-shell diagonal entries.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Significance of $\mathrm{prop} = 2$]
Connes \& van~Suijlekom prove $\mathrm{prop}(C(S^1)^{(n)}) = 2$ for
the Toeplitz operator system on the circle by showing that products
of two Toeplitz shift generators reach every matrix unit
$e_{ij}$~\cite{connes_vs2021}.  The companion dual operator system
$C(\mathbb{Z})^{(n)}$ has $\mathrm{prop} = \infty$ (their
Proposition~4.3) — so the value $\mathrm{prop} = 2$ is non-trivial
even at this level: it separates the Toeplitz construction from its
dual.  Eq.~\eqref{eq:prop_two} states that the Fock-projected $S^3$
truncation is in the same propagation class as the Toeplitz $S^1$ at
the level of a quantitative Connes--van~Suijlekom invariant.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The value should not be over-read:\ $\mathrm{prop}
= 2$ is the \emph{generic} value for a $*$-closed unital subspace of
$M_N(\mathbb{C})$ of the same complex dimension (random such subspaces
of dimension $14$ in $M_5$ and $55$ in $M_{14}$ all have $\mathrm{prop}
= 2$), so the invariant certifies that $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$
is not an abelian or otherwise degenerate comparator, not that it is
special among truncations.  The strongest quantitative alignment between
GeoVac and the published Connes--van~Suijlekom framework is the
convergence theorem in van~Suijlekom's own distance
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}), not this invariant.

The mechanism producing
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}} \subsetneq M_N(\mathbb{C})$ is the
``raise-then-lower at the top shell escapes the truncation'' boundary
correction: a multiplier $M_{N \ge 2, L, M}$ acting at the top shell
$n = n_{\max}$ would, in the continuum, be balanced by intermediate
sums over $n' > n_{\max}$, but the truncation $P_{n_{\max}}$ kills
these contributions.  This is structurally the same boundary effect
as the pendant-edge theorem of Paper~28~\cite{paper28},
$\Sigma_{\mathrm{graph}}(\mathrm{GS}) = 2(n_{\max} - 1)/n_{\max}$ for
the graph-native ground-state self-energy, viewed from the
operator-system side rather than the QED side.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[$\mathrm{prop} = 2$ separates $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ from abelian comparators]
\label{cor:structural_specificity}
\textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} The propagation value
$\mathrm{prop}(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) = 2$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:propagation_number} separates the
Connes--van~Suijlekom spectral truncation of $S^3$ from the abelian
(point-evaluation) discretizations of $S^3$; it does \emph{not} single
it out among non-abelian truncations, for which $\mathrm{prop} = 2$ is
The natural circulant-style comparator on $S^3$ at $N$ points
\[
   A_{\mathrm{circ}, N} := \mathrm{span}\{E_{i, i} : i = 0, \ldots, N - 1\}
   \subset M_N(\mathbb{C}),
\]
i.e., the commutative $C^*$-subalgebra of diagonal matrices in the
``point-evaluation'' basis of $\mathbb{C}^N$, satisfies
\begin{equation}
   \mathrm{prop}(A_{\mathrm{circ}, N}) = \begin{cases}
      1 & \text{(intrinsic envelope:\ $A_{\mathrm{circ}, N}$ is its own
                  $C^*$-algebra)}, \\
      \infty & \text{(ambient envelope:\ $M_N(\mathbb{C})$ is non-abelian;
                       no power of an abelian subalgebra reaches it)}.
   \end{cases}
   \label{eq:prop_circulant}
\end{equation}
Verified at $N \in \{2, 5, 14, 30, 55, 140\}$, matching both
dimension-matching conventions (envelope-match $N(\mathrm{circ}) =
N(\mathrm{GV})$ and operator-system-match $N(\mathrm{circ}) =
\dim(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}})$).  Either reading of
\eqref{eq:prop_circulant} is categorically different from the
spectral-truncation value~$2$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}[Computational verification]
The construction lives in \texttt{geovac/circulant\_s3.py} (the
\texttt{CirculantS3Truncation} class) with verification in
\texttt{tests/test\_circulant\_s3.py} (35 tests, all pass; runtime
${\sim}1.4$~s).  Algorithmically, $\mathrm{prop}(A_{\mathrm{circ}, N})$
is computed via the same vec-stack rank algorithm used in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:propagation_number}, applied to the
diagonal-matrix basis of $A_{\mathrm{circ}, N}$.  The intrinsic-envelope
reading exits at $k = 1$ since $\dim(A_{\mathrm{circ}, N}^1) = N =
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(A_{\mathrm{circ}, N})$ already saturates the
intrinsic $C^*$-envelope.  In the ambient-envelope reading, the
$M_N(\mathbb{C})$ envelope has dimension $N^2$ for $N \ge 2$ but
$\dim(A_{\mathrm{circ}, N}^k) = N$ for every $k$ (commutativity is
preserved under self-products), so no finite $k$ achieves
saturation; $\mathrm{prop} = \infty$ in this ambient-envelope
extension of Connes--van~Suijlekom's Definition~2.39 (their definition
uses the $C^*$-envelope, which here gives the intrinsic value $1$).  See
\texttt{debug/wh1\_round3\_falsification\_memo.md} (2026-05-03) for
the full record, including the verbatim Connes--van~Suijlekom Outlook~\S6
contrast (lines 2611--2618 of arXiv:2004.14115v2) between
spectral and circulant truncations on~$S^1$, of which
Eq.~\eqref{eq:prop_circulant} is the verbatim $S^3$ analog.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Connes distance on $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$: SDP framework
and structural findings]
\label{rem:connes_distance}
\textbf{[MEASURED]} The Connes distance on operator systems
(\cite{connes_vs2021}, Eq.~(2))
\[
   d_D(\phi, \psi) = \sup_{x \in \mathcal{O}} \big\{ |\phi(x) - \psi(x)|
      : \|[D, x]\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le 1 \big\}
\]
restricted to pure node-evaluation states $\phi_v(x) := \langle v | x | v
\rangle$ on the truncated operator system has been computed by
semidefinite programming on $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ at $n_{\max} = 2, 3$.
The implementation lives in \texttt{geovac/connes\_distance.py} (a
\texttt{cvxpy} SDP with the operator-norm constraint lifted to the LMI
$\bigl[\begin{smallmatrix} I & [D, x] \\ [D, x]^* & I \end{smallmatrix}\bigr]
\succeq 0$, and a gauge-fixing constraint orthogonalizing $x$ to
$\ker([D, \cdot]) \cap \mathcal{O}_h$); 17 tests in
\texttt{tests/test\_connes\_distance.py} (all pass; runtime ${\sim}9$~s).
Three structural findings emerged at the placeholder convention:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{SO(3) $m$-reflection forces zero distance.}  For every
   $|n, l, -m\rangle$ and $|n, l, +m\rangle$ pair, $d_D(\phi_{|n,l,-m\rangle},
   \phi_{|n,l,+m\rangle}) = 0$ exactly (one such pair at $n_{\max} = 2$,
   four at $n_{\max} = 3$).  This is the SO(3) $m$-reflection symmetry
   of the operator system showing through, not a pathology: the symmetry
   identifies the two states as members of the same orbit, and any
   point-discriminating function must be constant on the orbit.

% [retracted 2026-09-30: p32-connes-placeholder-findings]
\item \textbf{Rational structure at $n_{\max} = 2$ under placeholder
   multipliers (retracted as a placeholder artifact,
   Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}).}  Under the placeholder values the
   five non-zero entries of the $5 \times 5$ distance matrix at
   $n_{\max} = 2$ grouped into ratios $1 : 2 : 3$ (specifically
   $28.87 : 57.73 : 86.60$), with $86.60 \approx 50\sqrt{3} = 86.6025$.  This rational/algebraic
   structure is lost at $n_{\max} = 3$ as the SDP gains degrees of
   freedom.  Under physical values the $1{:}2{:}3$ grouping does not
   persist, though the $50\sqrt{3}$ entry does
   (Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}).

\item \textbf{Non-monotonicity in the Fock-graph distance.}  Pearson
   correlation between $d_D(\phi_v, \phi_w)$ and the combinatorial
   Fock-graph distance $d_{\mathrm{graph}}(v, w)$ (counting $\Delta n =
   \pm 1$ and $\Delta l = \pm 1$ ladder steps) is $\rho \approx -0.04$
   at $n_{\max} = 2$, growing to $+0.14$ at $n_{\max} = 3$ (Spearman
   $0.09 \to 0.18$) under placeholder values;\ under physical values the
   sign flips (Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}), and the absolute level
   is far from a discretized-geodesic metric at our reachable cutoffs.
\end{enumerate}

These findings should be read with three explicit caveats, the
first two of which are closed in Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}.

\begin{itemize}
\item The SO(4) radial overlap integral $I_{n N n'}^{l L l'}$ in the
   construction of $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$
   (Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}) is currently a placeholder rather
   than the genuine Wen--Avery $3$-$Y$ integral on $S^3$.
   Round~2's propagation-number result
   (Proposition~\ref{prop:propagation_number}) is invariant under this
   choice; the Connes distance is not.  The absolute distance values
   reported above are convention-dependent until the placeholder is
   replaced.

\item The construction is on the scalar Fock basis $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$
   only; the spinor lift to $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes
   \mathbb{C}^4$ with the native Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac in the
   $(n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j)$ basis is not yet incorporated.  The
   diagonal scalar Dirac on $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ has a large
   $\ker([D, \cdot]) \cap \mathcal{O}$ that makes the SDP unbounded on
   most state pairs; the metric values reported here use an
   off-diagonal Dirac proxy as a finite-distance regulator, which is a
   second convention choice.

\item No comparison to the round-$S^3$ continuum geodesic distance is
   attempted.  Such a comparison is the actual content of Connes--vS
   Gromov--Hausdorff convergence.  The scalar case is published:\
   Gaudillot-Estrada--van~Suijlekom~\cite{gaudillot_estrada_vs2025}
   prove spectral-truncation convergence for all compact metric
   groups, including $\mathrm{SU}(2) \cong S^3$; the spinor case ---
   on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window of complete Dirac eigenspaces,
   not the chirality-doubled Fock model used in this remark --- is the
   contribution of Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} below and Paper~38.
\end{itemize}

The SO(3) $m$-reflection forced zeros (and the $50\sqrt{3}$
fingerprint) are robust across the placeholder choice;\ the rational
grouping at $n_{\max} = 2$, the absolute distance values and the
non-monotonicity diagnostic are not (Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}).  See
\texttt{debug/wh1\_round3\_connes\_distance\_memo.md} for the full
record.  Caveats (1) and (2) above are subsequently closed in
Remark~\ref{rem:r31_r32_update}, where two of the three structural
findings (the $1{:}2{:}3$ grouping and the trend-toward-correlation
reading) are retracted as placeholder artifacts.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[R3.1+R3.2 closure of round-3 caveats]
\label{rem:r31_r32_update}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Two of the three caveats above have been closed in subsequent
sub-sprints (continuation of the same dispatch on 2026-05-03), and
two of the three structural findings (the $1{:}2{:}3$ grouping and the
trend-toward-correlation reading) have been retracted as placeholder
artifacts.

\paragraph{R3.1 (Wen--Avery integral, placeholder $\to$
physical).}  The placeholder for $I_{n N n'}^{l L l'}$ is replaced
with the closed-form Wen--Avery $3$-$Y$ integral on $S^3$,
factorized as $I_{\mathrm{rad}} \cdot G_{\mathrm{Gaunt}}$ with
$I_{\mathrm{rad}}$ the Gegenbauer triple integral on $\chi$ and
$G_{\mathrm{Gaunt}}$ the standard $S^2$ Gaunt integral.
Implemented in \texttt{geovac/so4\_three\_y\_integral.py}
(sympy-exact, \texttt{lru\_cache}), verified by hyperspherical
orthonormality at $n_{\max} \le 3$ in exact arithmetic.
$\mathrm{prop}(\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}) = 2$ holds at $n_{\max} =
2, 3, 4$ under the physical default with dim sequences $14 \to
25,\ 55 \to 196,\ 140 \to 900$ \emph{bit-identical} to the
placeholder values---confirming the round-2 invariance claim by
replacement, not by sampling.

The Connes distance recomputed under physical values retains
the $m$-reflection forced zeros and the $50\sqrt{3}$ fingerprint,
but \emph{retracts} the $1 : 2 : 3$ rational structure as a
placeholder artifact: under Avery the distinct nonzero values at
$n_{\max} = 2$ are $\{1.27, 86.60, 87.18, 174.93, 175.53,
262.11\}$ with no clean integer grouping.  The $M_{2,0,0}$
multiplier rescales by $\sim 3\times$ between placeholder and
physical values, propagating as $\sim 9\times$ on pairs that use
it twice.  More importantly, the non-monotonicity \emph{flips
sign} at $n_{\max} = 3$: Pearson goes from $+0.14$ (placeholder)
to $-0.36$ (physical), Spearman from $+0.18$ to $-0.33$.  The
optimistic ``slow trend toward correlation'' reading is
retracted; under physical values the metric is
\emph{anti-correlated} with the Fock-graph distance.  Memo:
\texttt{debug/wh1\_r31\_avery\_wen\_avery\_memo.md}.

\paragraph{R3.2 (spinor lift, scalar Fock $\to$ Weyl spinor on
$S^3$).}  The construction extends to the Weyl sector of the
Camporesi--Higuchi spinor bundle on $S^3$ via Clebsch--Gordan
decomposition $|\psi_{l, j = l + 1/2, m_j}\rangle = \alpha_+
|Y_{l, m_j - 1/2}\rangle \otimes |\!\!\uparrow\rangle + \alpha_-
|Y_{l, m_j + 1/2}\rangle \otimes |\!\!\downarrow\rangle$ with
$\alpha_\pm = \sqrt{(l \pm m_j + 1/2)/(2l + 1)}$.  Each scalar
Avery multiplier $M_{NLM}$ lifts to a CG-weighted sum of two
scalar matrix elements; the resulting
$\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{n_{\max}}$ is API-compatible with the
existing SDP machinery.  Implemented in
\texttt{geovac/spinor\_operator\_system.py}.  $\dim
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}} = \dim \mathcal{O}$ at every $n_{\max}$
(the CG decomposition introduces no new linear independencies);
identity, $\ast$-closure, and $m_j$-reflection forced zeros all
generalize naturally.

Two clean R3.2 findings.  \textbf{(i)} \emph{The truthful
Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac on the spinor bundle is too
$n$-degenerate} for the Connes distance to be well-defined on
cross-shell pure-state pairs: at $n_{\max} = 2$, $24$ of $28$
cross-pairs give $+\infty$ because the $n$-shell-diagonal
multipliers $M_{N, L = \mathrm{even}, M = 0}$ commute with $D =
\mathrm{diag}(n_{\mathrm{Fock}} + 1/2)$, making the SDP
unbounded.  This is the spinor analog of the round-3
\texttt{shell\_scalar} diagnostic.  It is a property of the Fock/Weyl
spinor \emph{model}, not of the truncated Camporesi--Higuchi triple:\ on
the true window (complete Dirac eigenspaces) the Dirac-commutator
kernel is exactly the scalars at $n_{\max} \le 3$ (Paper~38,
remark on the Dirac-commutator seminorm; \texttt{tests/test\_p38\_true\_ch\_window.py}).
\textbf{(ii)} \emph{Under a CH+offdiag perturbation that lifts
the $n$-degeneracy, the metric remains anti-correlated} with the
Fock-graph distance: Pearson nz $-0.36$ at $n_{\max} = 2$,
$-0.26$ at $n_{\max} = 3$.  The non-monotonicity is therefore
robust under both the placeholder $\to$ Avery upgrade (R3.1)
\emph{and} the scalar $\to$ spinor lift (R3.2).  Memo:
\texttt{debug/wh1\_r32\_spinor\_lift\_memo.md}.

\paragraph{Disambiguation settled.}  The round-3 \S 5.4
two-reading question (``placeholder artifact'' vs
``pure-state-distance pathology'') lands on the latter.  R3.1
rules out the placeholder reading (anti-correlation
\emph{strengthens} under physical values).  R3.2 rules out the
scalar-Dirac-proxy reading (anti-correlation persists under the
spinor lift).  The Connes distance on $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ or
its spinor lift, with pure node-evaluation states, is structurally
not a discretization of the round-$S^3$ geodesic distance at any
cutoff we can reach.  The third caveat above (no GH comparison
on the full state space) remains the natural next move; whether
the metric becomes monotone in the GH limit via a Kantorovich--
Wasserstein lift to mixed states requires Peter--Weyl on
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ (not addressed by Connes \& van~Suijlekom 2021, who
defer Gromov--Hausdorff questions generically:\ ``will be studied
elsewhere'', their \S 3).  The $S^3$ GH
convergence itself is no longer unpublished:\ the scalar case is
closed by
Gaudillot-Estrada--van~Suijlekom~\cite{gaudillot_estrada_vs2025}
for all compact metric groups including $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, and the
spinor case, on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window (not the
chirality-doubled Fock/Weyl model of this remark), by
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} and Paper~38; the
Kantorovich--Wasserstein monotonicity question remains open.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Two-sided structural alignment]
\label{rem:two_sided_alignment}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Connes \& van~Suijlekom's worked $S^1$ example exhibits the
spectral-vs-circulant distinction along two axes simultaneously:
the Toeplitz operator system $C(S^1)^{(n)}$ has $\mathrm{prop} = 2$
(their Proposition~4.2), while the circulant matrix algebra
$C(C_m)$ has $\mathrm{prop} = 1$ trivially (it is its own
$C^*$-algebra).  Eqs.~\eqref{eq:prop_two}
(Proposition~\ref{prop:propagation_number}) and~\eqref{eq:prop_circulant}
(Corollary~\ref{cor:structural_specificity}) reproduce both axes
verbatim on $S^3$:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 & \text{spectral truncation} & \text{circulant analog} \\ \hline
S^1 \text{ (Connes--vS)} & C(S^1)^{(n)},\ \mathrm{prop} = 2 &
   C(C_m),\ \mathrm{prop} = 1 \\
S^3 \text{ (this work)}  & \mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}},\ \mathrm{prop} = 2 &
   A_{\mathrm{circ}, N},\ \mathrm{prop} = 1 \text{ or } \infty \\
\end{array}
\]
The two-sided match separates spectral truncation from abelian
discretization on both manifolds; it does not foreclose the generic
non-abelian value (Remark above).  Together with the
SO(4)-invariance of the truncation $P_{n_{\max}}$ (whole Fock shells;\
the isometry-preservation property Connes \& van~Suijlekom themselves
identify as distinguishing spectral truncation from circulant
discretization),
it is a consistency check on the construction; the load-bearing
alignment is Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$}

Let $V_{\mathrm{Dirac}}$ denote the set of Dirac-graph nodes at the
matching truncation, labeled by Camporesi--Higuchi triples
$(n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j)$ with $0 \le n_{\mathrm{D}} \le
n_{\max} - 1$ (the standard convention $n_{\mathrm{Fock}} = n_{\mathrm{CH}}
+ 1$ from \texttt{geovac/dirac\_matrix\_elements.py}), Dirac--Coulomb
relativistic angular momentum $\kappa \in \{-(l+1), +(l+1)\}$ for each
$0 \le l \le n_{\mathrm{D}}$ --- equivalently the fixed
$j = l + \tfrac12$ carrying a $\pm 1$ chirality label --- and total angular
momentum projection $m_j \in \{-j, \ldots, j\}$ where $j = |\kappa| - 1/2$.
The total
node count is
\[
   N_{\mathrm{Dirac}}(n_{\max}) = \sum_{n=0}^{n_{\max}-1}
     g_n^{\mathrm{D}} = \sum_{n=0}^{n_{\max}-1} 2(n+1)(n+2)
   = \tfrac{2}{3} n_{\max}(n_{\max}+1)(n_{\max}+2).
\]

\begin{definition}[Spinor space on the Dirac graph]
\label{def:H_GV}
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})} := \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Dirac}}}$,
with the standard Hermitian inner product on $\mathbb{C}^{N_{\mathrm{Dirac}}}$.
\end{definition}

The faithful $*$-representation of $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ on
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is by the natural pullback: for
$f \in \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ and $\psi \in \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$,
\begin{equation}
   (\pi(f) \psi)(n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j)
   := f(n_{\mathrm{Fock}}(n_{\mathrm{D}}), l(\kappa), m_l(\kappa, m_j))
   \cdot \psi(n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j),
   \label{eq:representation}
\end{equation}
where the maps $n_{\mathrm{Fock}}, l, m_l$ are the standard Dirac-to-Fock
quantum-number bridges:
$n_{\mathrm{Fock}}(n_{\mathrm{D}}) = n_{\mathrm{D}} + 1$,
$l(\kappa) = |\kappa| - 1$ for both signs, and
$m_l = m_j - \tfrac12$ clipped into $[-l, l]$ (a fixed choice of Fock
node:\ the Clebsch--Gordan decomposition of $|j, m_j\rangle$ superposes
$m_l = m_j \mp \tfrac12$ and defines no map).
Each $\pi(f)$ acts diagonally; in the spinor basis it is the diagonal
matrix $\mathrm{diag}(f(v_{\mathrm{Fock}}(\,\cdot\,)))$.  The
representation is faithful: $\pi(f) = 0 \Leftrightarrow f \equiv 0$
(every Fock node has at least one Dirac node above it for $n_{\max}
\ge 1$).

\subsection{The Dirac operator $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$}

The continuum Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac operator on the unit
three-sphere has spectrum $|\lambda_n^{\mathrm{D}}| = n + 3/2$ with
degeneracy $g_n^{\mathrm{D}} = 2(n+1)(n+2)$~\cite{camporesi_higuchi1996}.
We promote this to a finite operator on $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$
by truncation of its spectral decomposition.

\begin{definition}[$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$]
\label{def:D_GV_spectral}
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is the diagonal operator on
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ with eigenvalues
$\lambda_n^{\mathrm{D},\pm} = \pm (n_{\mathrm{D}} + 3/2)$
on the eigenstate $|n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j\rangle$ with sign
$+$ for $\kappa < 0$ and $-$ for $\kappa > 0$ (a GeoVac
Dirac--Coulomb labelling convention for the two signs of the
Camporesi--Higuchi spectrum; Camporesi--Higuchi~\cite{camporesi_higuchi1996}
supply the spinor bundle and the spectrum, not a $\kappa$ labelling).
\end{definition}

This is the operator used everywhere in the paper.  (The relocated
Q$5'$ cochain record, \S VIII, used the hopping operator $D = \Lambda +
\kappa A_{\mathrm{graph}}$;\ on $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ every degree-$\ge 1$ JLO
cochain vanishes, since the algebra's idempotents commute with it.)
Every axiom verification below, the real structure $J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:reality}, and every finite-truncation spectral-action
computation are carried out on this diagonal $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$
(\texttt{geovac/full\_dirac\_operator\_system.py},
\texttt{geovac/real\_structure.py}; backing tests
\texttt{tests/test\_full\_dirac\_operator\_system.py},
\texttt{tests/test\_real\_structure.py}).  The $\pi$-free certificate
of the Dirac sector (Paper~28~\S graph\_native\_qed.6) is a statement
about this spectrum:\ every eigenvalue is a half-integer rational,
every degeneracy is a positive integer.

\begin{remark}[No separate ``graph form'' of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$]
\label{rem:D_GV_no_graph_form}
The paper carries one Dirac operator for its theorems, the diagonal
one above.  A hopping operator with weights chosen to reproduce its
spectrum would be unitarily equivalent to it by the spectral theorem
alone;\ \texttt{geovac/dirac\_matrix\_elements.py} holds closed-form
angular (Szmytkowski) and radial (hydrogenic) matrix elements in the
$(\kappa, m_j)$ basis, not a hopping operator, and the hopping operator
of the relocated Q5$'$ record is a separate computational object.  The
Dirac-sector \emph{graph} that appears elsewhere in the corpus
(\texttt{geovac/dirac\_lattice.py}, the E1-dipole adjacency of
Paper~28's graph-native QED) is a different operator, $H = D + W$,
whose adjacency encodes the photon-vertex selection rules rather than
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ itself.  The module contents
asserted here are pinned by
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_dirac\_module\_contents.py}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Bounded commutators}

\begin{proposition}[Bounded commutators]
\label{prop:bounded_commutators}
For every $a \in \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$, the commutator
$[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)]$ is a bounded operator on
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ has finite-dimensional domain
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ at every $n_{\max}$, hence bounded.
$\pi(a)$ is a diagonal multiplication operator, also bounded.  Any
commutator of bounded operators is bounded.  In fact more is true:
$D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ and $\pi(a)$ are both diagonal in the basis
$|n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j\rangle$, so
$[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)] = 0$ identically.
\end{proof}

\textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} The check is therefore trivial in a strong
sense:\ the commutator seminorm $a \mapsto \|[D_{\mathrm{GV}},
\pi(a)]\|$ vanishes on all of $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$, the Connes
distance it would induce is degenerate, the first-order differential
forms $\Omega^1_{D_{\mathrm{GV}}}$ are zero, and $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$
admits no inner fluctuations.  The diagonal triple
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ is a \emph{spectral}, not a metric, object:\ it
carries the Camporesi--Higuchi spectrum, the degeneracies and the real
structure, but no Lipschitz geometry.  The metric role belongs
instead to the \emph{non-diagonal} multipliers of the truncated
operator system on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window
(Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}; Paper~38~\cite{loutey_paper38}),
whose state spaces converge under the left-translation Lipschitz
seminorm (Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence});\ the theorem uses no Dirac
commutator, and convergence in the Dirac-commutator seminorm is
\textbf{[OPEN]} (that seminorm is non-degenerate on the window as
measured at $n_{\max} \le 3$, \texttt{tests/test\_p38\_true\_ch\_window.py}).
Both halves of the contrast ---
$[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(f)] = 0$ identically on the diagonal
representative, and nonzero commutators on the non-diagonal multipliers
of the chirality-doubled model $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ --- are
regression-pinned in \texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_diagonal\_triple.py}.

\subsection{The triple, summarized}

Putting Definitions~\ref{def:A_GV},~\ref{def:H_GV},~\ref{def:D_GV_spectral}
and Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators} together:

\begin{theorem}[The GeoVac spectral triple]
\label{thm:GV_triple}
For every $n_{\max} \ge 1$, the data
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})},
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})},
D_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})})$ is a finite spectral triple in the
sense of~\cite{connes1995}: $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is unital and
involutive, $\pi: \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} \to
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}})$ is a faithful
$*$-representation, $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is self-adjoint with bounded
commutators.  \textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]} As
$n_{\max} \to \infty$ the state spaces converge to that of the canonical
spectral triple of the unit Riemannian spin three-sphere $S^3$ with the
Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac operator \emph{for the operator-system
representative on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window}
$\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}} = (\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}, \mathcal{H}_{n_{\max}}, D_{n_{\max}})$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} (van~Suijlekom's state-space
Gromov--Hausdorff distance, translation-seminorm metrization, limit
identified up to isometry;\ the target's spectral-triple properties are
inputs, not outputs, of the convergence); neither the Fock/Weyl model
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ of the axiom audit nor the diagonal
(gauge-network) representative
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} = \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Fock}}}$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}, used for the axiom audit above, is
the object that converges (Corollary~\ref{cor:structural_specificity}
places the diagonal representative on the comparator side of the Connes--van~Suijlekom distinction).
\end{theorem}

The continuum-limit clause is van~Suijlekom's state-space
Gromov--Hausdorff convergence of spectral
truncations~\cite{vansuijlekom2021_fejer} (in the operator-system
framework of~\cite{connes_vs2021}), proved for the true window
$\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}$ in Paper~38; no
Cauchy-sequence statement for the
diagonal algebra is made.

% ===================================================================
\section{Axiom Audit}
\label{sec:axiom_audit}
% ===================================================================

We check each Connes axiom in turn, distinguishing what holds
rigorously at finite $n_{\max}$ from what holds only in the continuum
limit.

\subsection{Order zero (involutive faithful representation)}

\textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} Holds by construction from
Definitions~\ref{def:A_GV}--\ref{def:H_GV} and
Eq.~\eqref{eq:representation}:\ $\pi(f)$ acts diagonally in the
$(n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j)$ basis, so $\pi$ is multiplicative and
$\pi(f^*) = \pi(f)^\dagger$, and it is faithful because the
quantum-number bridges of Eq.~\eqref{eq:representation} map
$V_{\mathrm{Dirac}}$ onto $V_{\mathrm{Fock}}$.  No numerical
certificate is involved.

\subsection{Bounded commutators (regularity)}

Holds rigorously at every $n_{\max}$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators}.

\subsection{Compact resolvent / finite summability}

In the finite-$n_{\max}$ truncation $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is
finite-dimensional so this is automatic.  The continuum statement is
that $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ has compact resolvent and is $3^+$-summable
(Dixmier-trace summable in dimension $3+\epsilon$); this is the
Camporesi--Higuchi summability of the continuum operator on $S^3$ and
is a standard result~\cite{camporesi_higuchi1996,connes1995}.

\subsection{Order one}

\begin{proposition}[Order one, diagonal representative]
\label{prop:order_one}
For every $a, b \in \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ (the diagonal
representative), $[[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)], \pi(b)^{\circ}] = 0$
holds trivially, because already $[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)] = 0$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators}).
\end{proposition}

This is vacuous rather than informative:\ the diagonal triple has no
nonzero commutators to constrain.  It is \emph{not} the statement for
the Fock/Weyl model $\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ of the operator-system
representative, whose
multipliers do not commute with $D$ and for which the order-zero and
order-one conditions (with the real structure) \emph{fail} at finite
$n_{\max} \ge 2$ (item~5 of the finite-$n_{\max}$ audit below;
Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit} labels the two representatives
separately).  Order-one becomes
non-trivial only when one extends to an almost-commutative triple
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes M_n(\mathbb{C})$ with non-abelian
fiber, at which point it constrains the form of fluctuations of $D$.
This is exactly the setting where Papers~25 ($n=1$, $U(1)$ fiber) and
30 ($n=2$, $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ fiber) live; we treat them in
Sec.~\ref{sec:subsectors}.

\subsection{Real structure}

$S^3$ is odd-dimensional; in dimension~$n$ the canonical
$\mathrm{KO}$-dimension is~$n$ mod~$8$ for a Riemannian spin manifold,
and the sign table~\cite{connes1995,vansuijlekom_book2015} for $n=3$
gives
\[
   \epsilon = -1, \quad \epsilon' = +1, \quad \epsilon'' = ~\text{(none)}~
   ~~\text{(no $\gamma$ in odd dimension).}
\]
The sign $\epsilon = -1$ reflects the fact that the 2-dimensional
spinor representation of $\mathrm{Spin}(3) = \mathrm{SU}(2)$ is
quaternionic (pseudoreal): the standard charge-conjugation operator
on this representation squares to $-\mathbb{1}$, equivalently
$\mathbb{H} \otimes_{\mathbb{R}} \mathbb{C} \cong M_2(\mathbb{C})$
acts on a complex 2-dimensional space with antiunitary $J$ satisfying
$J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$.  This is the same sign that appears in the
canonical Lorentzian Dirac formalism in $3+1$ dimensions for the
charge-conjugation matrix~$C$.

\begin{proposition}[Real structure on $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$]
\label{prop:reality}
There exists an antilinear isometry $J_{\mathrm{GV}}:
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}} \to \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ with
$J_{\mathrm{GV}}^2 = -\mathbb{1}$ and $J_{\mathrm{GV}} D_{\mathrm{GV}}
= +D_{\mathrm{GV}} J_{\mathrm{GV}}$, namely the charge-conjugation
operator on the Camporesi--Higuchi spinor bundle restricted to the
truncation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Construction.]
The continuum charge conjugation $J_{S^3}$ on the spinor bundle of
$S^3$ satisfies the listed signs by construction~\cite{connes1995}
(the KO-dimension-3 row;\ Camporesi--Higuchi~\cite{camporesi_higuchi1996}
supply the spinor bundle and spectrum, not a charge conjugation);\ the
finite-cutoff signs are verified directly by
\texttt{tests/test\_real\_structure.py}.  Restriction to
the truncation $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})}$ commutes with
the spectral truncation because $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ acts diagonally in
the spectral basis and $J_{S^3}$ permutes the spectral basis (sending
$|n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, m_j\rangle \mapsto
|n_{\mathrm{D}}, \kappa, -m_j\rangle$ up to a phase --- \emph{$m_j$ only,
with the chirality label held fixed} --- whose antilinear-squaring gives
$-\mathbb{1}$ on the quaternionic 2-spinor representation;\ a
chirality flip would give $\varepsilon' = -1$, since $D_{\mathrm{GV}} =
\chi\,(n + \tfrac12)$).  The implemented $J$
(\texttt{geovac/real\_structure.py}) maps $m_j$ alone, as the
real-structure tests verify.  The restriction therefore inherits the signs.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Finite-$n_{\max}$ audit (Sprint WH1-Connes Step~2,
2026-05).}  The qualitative argument above is now backed by a
quantitative computational audit at $n_{\max} \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ on
both the Weyl sector
(\texttt{geovac/spinor\_operator\_system.py}, $\dim_{\mathcal{H}} = 2,
8, 20$) and the full-Dirac sector
(\texttt{geovac/full\_dirac\_operator\_system.py},
$\dim_{\mathcal{H}} = 4, 16, 40$), with
$J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ realized as an antilinear permutation-phase operator
$J = U \mathcal{K}$ (with $\mathcal{K}$ complex conjugation) and phase
convention $\sigma(l, m_j) = i^{2 m_j} (-1)^l$ giving
$\sigma_a \overline{\sigma_{J(a)}} = -1$ on every basis label.
Implementation in \texttt{geovac/real\_structure.py};
verification in \texttt{tests/test\_real\_structure.py}
(44 tests passing); raw audit data
\texttt{debug/data/wh1\_real\_structure\_audit.json}.
The audit verdict for the four Connes-axiom conditions involving $J$
at finite $n_{\max}$ on the truthful Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac is as
follows;\ items~1--5 are measured on the Fock/Weyl and chirality-doubled
\emph{model} objects of Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}, and item~6
repeats the audit on the true window whose continuum limit
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} proves:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{$J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$ HOLDS EXACTLY} at every
$n_{\max} \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ on both sectors (bit-exact zero
residual).  The phase formula
$\sigma_a \overline{\sigma_{\pi(a)}} = -1$ is verified pair-by-pair
across the spinor and full-Dirac bases.
\item \textbf{$J_{\mathrm{GV}} D_{\mathrm{GV}}^{\mathrm{truthful}}
= +D_{\mathrm{GV}}^{\mathrm{truthful}} J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ HOLDS
EXACTLY} at every $n_{\max}$ (bit-exact zero residual).  Mechanism:
the truthful CH Dirac is diagonal in
$(n_{\mathrm{Fock}}, l, m_j, \mathrm{chirality})$ with eigenvalue
$\mathrm{chi} \cdot (n_{\mathrm{Fock}} + 1/2)$ depending only on $n$
and~$\mathrm{chi}$; $J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ permutes only $m_j$, leaving
$n$ and $\mathrm{chi}$ fixed, so the matrix relation
$U \overline{D} = D U$ is diagonal-by-diagonal.
\item \textbf{$J_{\mathrm{GV}} \mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}
J_{\mathrm{GV}}^{-1} = \mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$ HOLDS EXACTLY} at
every $n_{\max}$ (bit-exact zero residual on each generator $a$, with
$0$ of $1, 14, 55$ generators failing the $\mathcal{O}$-membership
test).  Mechanism: each multiplier matrix
$\widetilde{M}_{NLM}$ has $J$-conjugate
$\widetilde{M}_{N L (-M)}$ via the $m_l \to -m_l$ symmetry of the
Wigner-3j coupling coefficients (Edmonds Eq.~3.7.5 / Wen--Avery
3-Y integral); the operator system is closed under this involution.
\item \textbf{$J_{\mathrm{GV}} D_{\mathrm{GV}}^{\mathrm{offdiag}}
= +D_{\mathrm{GV}}^{\mathrm{offdiag}} J_{\mathrm{GV}}$ FAILS}
structurally (max residual $2 \times 10^0$ at $n_{\max} \in \{2, 3\}$;
$10^{-2}$ at $n_{\max} = 1$ from the m-linear lifter alone).  This is
the natural counter-example: the round-3 \texttt{offdiag} Dirac is a
hand-crafted Hermitian perturbation with E1-type hopping
$D[a, b] = \alpha$ on the rule
$|\Delta n| = |\Delta l| = 1, |\Delta m_j| \le 1$ (value $\alpha$
within a chirality, the chirality coupling across).  Since this real
$D$ is invariant under $m_j \to -m_j$, $U\overline{D} = DU$ requires
$\sigma$ to be equal on the $J$-images of every hopping pair, while
$J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$ forces $\sigma_{J(a)} = -\sigma_a$;\ the rule
links both partners $m_j = \pm 1/2$ of an $(n, l)$ shell to the same
neighbour, so no phase choice of this permutation form commutes with
$D$, and each failing hop contributes $2|D_{ab}|$ (residual
$2\max(\alpha, \text{coupling})$).  The $(-1)^l$ factor in $\sigma$
only selects which hops fail ($\Delta m_j = 0$ with it, $|\Delta m_j|
= 1$ without).  The genuine 4-component Camporesi--Higuchi spinor
Dirac (which couples large/small components via the proper
$\mathrm{SU}(2)_L \times \mathrm{SU}(2)_R$ angular gradient with
Clifford-algebra $\gamma$ matrices) would resolve this, but lies
outside the present construction; the offdiag CH is best read as an
SDP-bounding device (round-3 R3.5 Connes distance), not a
spectral-action-foundational object.
\item \textbf{Order-zero $[a, J b J^{-1}] = 0$ and order-one
$[[D, a], J b J^{-1}] = 0$ FAIL at finite $n_{\max} \ge 2$} on the Weyl-sector model
$\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{n_{\max}}$ of the operator system (not the
true window of Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}), with
residuals of order $0.05$--$0.2$ (largest matrix entry in absolute
value, not operator norm).  At $n_{\max} = 2$:
$124$ of $196$ pairs fail order-zero (max residual $5.5\times 10^{-2}$);
$43$ of $196$ pairs fail order-one (max residual $1.0 \times 10^{-1}$);
at $n_{\max} = 3$: $2{,}594$ of $3{,}025$ and $1{,}337$ of $3{,}025$
respectively (max residuals $7.9 \times 10^{-2}$ and
$2.0 \times 10^{-1}$).  The conditions hold trivially at
$n_{\max} = 1$ (single-generator operator system).
\textbf{[MEASURED]} The order-zero failure \emph{grows} with the
cutoff (Weyl sector): max residual
$5.5 \times 10^{-2} \to 7.9 \times 10^{-2} \to 9.6 \times 10^{-2}$
and failing fraction $63\% \to 86\% \to 92\%$ ($17{,}970$ of
$19{,}600$ pairs) at $n_{\max} = 2, 3, 4$;\ on a \emph{fixed} set of
generators (the degree-1 harmonics, $N = 2$) the operator-norm
residual runs $0.051, 0.068, 0.076, 0.081$ at $n_{\max} = 2$--$5$,
and its ratio to the (growing) generator norm runs $0.225, 0.212,
0.209, 0.208$ --- still drifting down, so whether it tends to a
nonzero limit is not established (measured through $n_{\max} = 5$).
The order-zero counts/residuals, the model order-one counts at
$n_{\max} = 2, 3$ and the fixed-generator growth are
regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_order\_condition\_growth.py}.
\item \textbf{True Camporesi--Higuchi window.}
\textbf{[MEASURED]} The audit repeated on the object of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}:\ spinors
$L^2(\mathrm{SU}(2)) \otimes \mathbb{C}^2$ in the left-invariant frame,
basis $e^j_{mm's} = \sqrt{2j+1}\,D^j_{mm'} \otimes e_s$,
$D = 2\sigma\cdot J_R + \tfrac32$, window $|\lambda| \le n_{\max} +
\tfrac12$, $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}} = P\,C(S^3)\,P$ of
dimension $5, 30, 91, 204$ at $n_{\max} = 1$--$4$, spanned by the
compressed matrix coefficients $D^J_{MM'}$ of bands $N = 2J + 1 \le
2n_{\max}$ (sup norm $1$).  The charge conjugation is $J = UK$,
$U\,e^j_{mm's} = (-1)^{m-m'}\,(i\sigma_y)_{s's}\,e^j_{-m,-m',s'}$:\
pointwise complex conjugation of the spinor
($\overline{D^j_{mm'}} = (-1)^{m-m'} D^j_{-m,-m'}$) twisted by
$i\sigma_y$, which reverses $J_R$ and $\sigma$ and so fixes
$\sigma\cdot J_R$.  It commutes with $D$, hence preserves the window,
and $J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$, $JD = +DJ$ hold exactly (test-pinned at $n_{\max} = 1$--$4$).
The signs are not a phase convention:\ the antiunitaries intertwining
$D$ and the isometry generators ($J_L$, $J_R + \sigma/2$) form a
family of one phase per irreducible block (dimension $2n_{\max}$),
every member has $J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$, and no equivariant antiunitary
anticommutes with $D$.  $J\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}J^{-1} =
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}$ holds exactly;\ indeed
$J(PfP)^*J^{-1} = PfP$.  Order zero $[a, Jb^*J^{-1}]$ and order one
$[[D,a], Jb^*J^{-1}]$ \emph{fail}, already at $n_{\max} = 1$ (where
the model holds trivially), over all basis pairs:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{c|cc|ccc}
n_{\max} & \text{order 0 failing} & \text{max} & \text{order 1 failing}
  & \text{max entry} & \text{op.\ norm} \\ \hline
1 & 12/25\ (48\%) & 1/2 & 4/25\ (16\%) & 3/4 & 0.750 \\
2 & 572/900\ (64\%) & 5/9 & 513/900\ (57\%) & 5/3 & 1.863 \\
3 & 5842/8281\ (71\%) & 11/16 & 5856/8281\ (71\%) & 21/8 & 2.902 \\
4 & 30462/41616\ (73\%) & 19/25 & 31021/41616\ (75\%) & 18/5 & 3.923 \\
\end{array}
\]
(max entry in the Peter--Weyl basis;\ at order zero it equals the
operator norm).  On the fixed degree-1 generators the order-zero
operator-norm residual runs $1/2, 5/9, 11/16, 19/25, 29/36, 41/49$ and
the order-one residual $3/4, 7/9, 13/16, 21/25, 31/36, 43/49$ at
$n_{\max} = 1$--$6$, increasing toward $1$, the product of the
generators' sup norms (the circle analogue:\ on
$\mathrm{span}\{e^{ik\theta}\}_{|k| \le n}$ the commutator of the
compressions of $e^{\pm i\theta}$ is a difference of two rank-one
projections, of norm $1$ at every $n$).  Relative to the generator norm
the true-window residual ($0.72$ at $n_{\max} = 2$, $0.86$ at
$n_{\max} = 5$) is \emph{larger} than the model's (${\approx}\,0.21$).
\emph{Mechanism (exact).}  Since $fg = gf$,
$[PfP, PgP] = Pg(1-P)fP - Pf(1-P)gP$;\ at order one $[D, f] = c(df)$
commutes with $g$ pointwise, so the same identity holds with $f$
replaced by $c(df)$.  A band-$N$ multiplier moves the Dirac level by
at most $N - 1$ (Paper~38's shell-difference rule), so every residual
vanishes on all levels more than $\max(N_a, N_b) - 1$ below the top
(measured:\ largest entry outside that strip $\le 3 \times 10^{-15}$,
$n_{\max} = 2$--$5$).  The degree-1 residual lives on the top Dirac
level alone, whose share of the window is $3/(n_{\max} + 2) \to 0$, and
its normalised Hilbert--Schmidt norm $\|C\|_2/\sqrt{\dim\mathcal{H}}$
falls $0.30, 0.29, 0.28, 0.26$ at $n_{\max} = 2$--$5$.  The failure is
a boundary term of the compression --- full operator norm, shrinking
weight --- not a bulk non-commutativity.  Two independent constructions
(Clebsch--Gordan multipliers with the closed-form $J$;\ sampled Wigner-$D$
functions with $J$ obtained from pointwise conjugation of the sampled
basis) agree pair by pair to $10^{-10}$ at $n_{\max} = 1, 2$
(\texttt{tests/test\_true\_window\_real\_structure.py}).  Test-pinned
there:\ the $J$-relations (with $\dim\mathcal{O} = 5, 30, 91, 204$) at
$n_{\max} = 1$--$4$, the order tables at $n_{\max} = 1$--$3$,
the degree-1 residuals at $n_{\max} = 1$--$4$, and the boundary strip at
$n_{\max} = 2, 3$;\ the $n_{\max} = 4$ order-zero/one table row,
the degree-1 values at $n_{\max} = 5, 6$, the Hilbert--Schmidt weights
and the strip check at $n_{\max} = 4, 5$ are measured in the audit
driver, not test-pinned.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Reading of the order-condition failures.}  The
order-zero condition $[a, J b J^{-1}] = 0$ is the
abelian-classical statement \emph{multiplications by classical
functions commute}, automatic for $f, g \in C^\infty(S^3)$ in the
continuum.  At finite $n_{\max}$, the truncated objects
$P_{n_{\max}} f P_{n_{\max}}$ and
$J P_{n_{\max}} g P_{n_{\max}} J^{-1}$ are no longer multiplications
by classical functions; they are operator-system elements
(\cite[Sec.~2]{connes_vs2021}), and their commutator is
generically nonzero.  That explains why the residual is nonzero; it
does \emph{not} explain its size or its trend.  The residuals do not
decay at the cutoffs reached:\ they grow, and on fixed low-degree
generators they do not decay through $n_{\max} = 5$.  The same growth is measured on
the true window that converges (item~6), where it is identified
exactly as a Toeplitz boundary term of the compression.  No
contradiction with Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} arises:\ GH
convergence of state spaces does not by itself control the commutator
$[a, JbJ^{-1}]$ in norm, and on the true window the residual, though of
full operator norm, is confined to a boundary strip of vanishing
weight.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} status, regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_order\_condition\_growth.py} (model) and
\texttt{tests/test\_true\_window\_real\_structure.py} (true window);
the mechanism on the model (the boundary-term identity of item~6 is
the plausible reading there too), and whether the continuum identity
is recovered in any weaker (e.g.\ weak-operator or state-space) sense,
are open.

\paragraph{Net status of the real structure at finite
$n_{\max}$.}  The introductory abstract flagged as open
``whether the GeoVac Dirac operator $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ (the diagonal spectral form) carries a real structure
matching the signs of $S^3$ exactly at finite $n_{\max}$ or only in
the continuum limit.''
The Sprint WH1-Connes Step~2 audit closes this question
\emph{positively for the three $J$-relations on the truthful CH
Dirac}: $J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$, $JD = +DJ$, and
$J \mathcal{O} J^{-1} = \mathcal{O}$ all hold with bit-exact zero
residual at every finite $n_{\max} \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ on both the Weyl
and full-Dirac (chirality-doubled) model sectors, and on the true
Camporesi--Higuchi window (test-pinned at $n_{\max} = 1$--$4$), where the
signs are forced by equivariance (item~6).  It does \emph{not} close it
for the order-zero and order-one conditions (involving the
abelian-multiplication identity that defines the classical limit of the
algebra):\ on the model those fail at finite $n_{\max} \ge 2$ with
residuals that grow over the measured cutoffs (item~5 above;\
\textbf{[MEASURED]}, regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_order\_condition\_growth.py}, mechanism
open), and on the true window they fail from $n_{\max} = 1$ with
residuals growing toward the generators' sup-norm scale, as a Toeplitz
boundary term of the compression (item~6;\ \textbf{[MEASURED]},
\texttt{tests/test\_true\_window\_real\_structure.py}).
The offdiag CH Dirac fails $JD = +DJ$ structurally and is therefore
not a spectral-action-foundational object; the truthful CH is the
natural domain for the present audit.  (On the diagonal representative
$\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ the truthful CH Dirac has no nonzero
commutators and so no inner fluctuations,
Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators};\ an inner-fluctuation
calculus for the Track~3 almost-commutative extension needs the
operator-system representative or a non-diagonal Dirac,
Sec.~\ref{sec:K_decomposition} discussion.)

\subsection{$\mathbb{Z}_2$-grading}

\begin{proposition}[Odd continuum; even model truncation; odd true window]
\label{prop:odd}
(a)~The continuum spectral triple of the unit Riemannian spin $S^3$
with the Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac operator is \emph{odd}: it carries no
$\mathbb{Z}_2$-grading commuting with $C^\infty(S^3)$ and
anticommuting with the Dirac operator.
(b)~The \emph{chirality-doubled model} truncation
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ is \emph{even}:\ the chirality-doubling operator
$\gamma_{\mathrm{GV}} = \sigma_x \otimes \mathbb{1}$ (exchanging the
two chirality copies of each $(n, l, m_j)$ label) is a self-adjoint
involution that commutes with $\pi(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}})$ and
anticommutes with $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$.
(c)~The \emph{true} Camporesi--Higuchi window of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} (complete Dirac eigenspaces, the object
that converges) is \emph{odd} (measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$), like the continuum:\ at
$n_{\max} = 1, 2, 3$ the commutant of its operator system
$\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}$ is the scalars, so no
$\mathbb{Z}_2$-grading commutes with $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}$ and
anticommutes with $D$.  The evenness in~(b) is a property of the
chirality-doubled model, not of the converging truncation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~$S^3$ is odd-dimensional; this is the standard statement for odd
Riemannian spin manifolds~\cite{connes1995}.
(b)~$\pi(f)$ does not depend on the chirality label
(Eq.~\eqref{eq:representation}), so it commutes with any operator
acting on the chirality index alone, in particular with $\sigma_x
\otimes \mathbb{1}$;\ $D_{\mathrm{GV}} = \chi\,(n + \tfrac12)$ is
$\sigma_z \otimes (n + \tfrac12)$ in the chirality index, and
$\sigma_x$ anticommutes with $\sigma_z$.  Measured bit-exactly at
$n_{\max} \le 3$ together with $J\gamma = +\gamma J$
(\texttt{geovac/chirality\_grading.py};
\texttt{tests/test\_almost\_commutative.py},
\S\ref{sec:higgs_h1}; \texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_diagonal\_triple.py},
which also contrasts the diagonal $\sigma_z \otimes \mathbb{1}$
candidate, which commutes with $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ instead of
anticommuting).  \textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF + MEASURED]}
(c)~\textbf{[MEASURED]} At $n_{\max} = 1, 2, 3$
($\dim \mathcal{H} = 4, 16, 40$) the commutant of
$\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}}$ (the null space of
$X \mapsto [X, a]$ over a basis of $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}$) has
dimension $1$ (Peter--Weyl construction,
\texttt{tests/test\_true\_window\_real\_structure.py}).  Two further
routes in the same test file corroborate it at $n_{\max} = 1, 2$:\ the
sampled-function construction of $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}$, whose
commutant is again the scalars and whose generated unital algebra is
all of $B(\mathcal{H})$ (dimension $16, 256$, so by Burnside's theorem
its commutant is the scalars;\ dimension $1600$ at $n_{\max} = 3$ is
computed outside the test), and an independent polynomial construction
on $S^3 \subset \mathbb{R}^4$ (no Wigner-$D$, Clebsch--Gordan or
sampling code).  At $n_{\max} = 3$ the oddness rests on the Peter--Weyl
route.  A scalar cannot anticommute with
the invertible $D$, so the grading space is $\{0\}$.  On the true
window only the pair $(\varepsilon, \varepsilon') = (-,+)$ is defined,
and it is forced by equivariance (audit item~6).
\end{proof}

The chirality-doubled model triple of~(b) therefore carries the sign
triple $(\varepsilon, \varepsilon', \varepsilon'') = (-, +, +)$, and,
per the rule stated in \S\ref{sec:higgs_h1}, \emph{no} KO-dimension
label is attached at finite cutoff:\ the continuum factor is KO-3
(odd), the model's grading reads as the KO-4 column, and any single
label at finite cutoff is a convention.  The grading is a doubling
artefact of the chirality-labelled model basis, not a chirality of
$S^3$; it is absent on the true window~(c) (measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$) and in the continuum limit,
where the odd description is the correct one.  Odd-dimensional spectral triples construct their
spectral action from $|D|$ rather than from $D^2$ projected onto
chirality eigenspaces, which is the structure that Paper~28's
QED-on-$S^3$ computations use.
