\begin{theorem}[State-space limit identification]
\label{thm:axiom_complete}
The state spaces of the true-window truncations
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}) converge, in van~Suijlekom's
state-space Gromov--Hausdorff distance under the translation seminorm,
to the state space $(P(S^3), W_d)$, with $d$ the dual-Coxeter geodesic
distance (a homothety of the unit-sphere metric), of the canonical
spectral triple of the round Riemannian spin three-sphere with the
Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac operator, up to isometry (Paper~38,
limit-identification proposition).
That target triple is real, odd, and of $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~$3$;\
these are classical properties of the target, not outputs of the
convergence.
\end{theorem}

\textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]} This is a structural theorem; no fitting
parameters or empirical
inputs.  It is a statement about the continuum limit only:\ at finite
cutoff the true-window truncation is odd like its limit
(measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$;\ Proposition~\ref{prop:odd}(c)), only the chirality-doubled model being
even (Proposition~\ref{prop:odd}(b)), and the
Fock/Weyl model of the operator system fails order zero and order one,
as does the true window, by a boundary term of the compression
(Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit}, audit items~5--6).  The audit in
Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit} establishes that the spectral-triple
framing is not metaphorical at the level of spectral data and the
three $J$-relations.

% ===================================================================
\section{Sub-sector Identification: Papers 25, 28, 30, 31}
\label{sec:subsectors}
% ===================================================================

We now read the major framework papers as projections of the GeoVac
triple.

\subsection{Paper 28 (QED on $S^3$): the spectral action}

Paper~28~\cite{paper28} computes Tr~$f(D^2/\Lambda^2)$ and related
spectral sums on the Dirac operator $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ in the continuum
limit, with $\mathrm{SO}(4)$ vertex selection rules from the
Hopf-bundle decomposition.  In the language of Sec.~\ref{sec:connes_data}:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item Theorem~T9 (Paper~28 Sec.~T9): the spectral zeta of $D^2$ is a
two-term polynomial in $\pi^2$ with rational coefficients at every
integer $s$.  In spectral-triple language: the heat-kernel
coefficients $a_k(D^2)$ on $S^3$ saturate at $k=2$ (Paper~28's
``SD two-term exactness theorem'').
\item Self-energy structural zero (Paper~28 Theorem~4):
$\Sigma(n_{\mathrm{ext}}=0) = 0$ on the continuum spectral sum,
identically by vertex parity.  This is the standard heat-kernel
bosonic sector cancellation specialized to the Camporesi--Higuchi
spectrum.
\item Vector-photon QED on $S^3$ (Paper~28 \S vector\_photon\_qed):
adding photon $(q, m_q)$ labels with Wigner 3$j$ vertex coupling
recovers 7/8 selection rules for scalar electrons and 8/8 for Dirac
electrons.  The per-loop calibration is the $S^2$ Weyl constant
1/(4$\pi$) (M1 volume slot).
\end{itemize}

In the spectral-triple language Paper~28 reads as the spectral-action
computation $\mathrm{Tr}\,f(D_{\mathrm{GV}}^2/\Lambda^2)$ at one loop;
the Camporesi--Higuchi spectrum is the input data of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$;
the SO(4) selection rules are the harmonic decomposition of the spinor
bundle.  Read this way, Paper~28 is not a free-standing observation
about $S^3$ but the spectral action of the present triple (a reading,
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]}).

\subsection{Paper 25 (Hopf graph as lattice gauge): the Cartan torus}

Paper~25~\cite{paper25} introduces the signed node-edge incidence
matrix $B$ of the Fock graph, with $L_0 = BB^{\top}$ the node
Laplacian (spectrally inert on $S^3$ and $S^5$) and
$L_1 = B^{\top}B$ the discrete Hodge-1 Laplacian.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]}
The spectral-triple reading of $L_1$ is \emph{not} an inner fluctuation
of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$:\ $[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(f)] = 0$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators}), so
$\Omega^1_{D_{\mathrm{GV}}} = 0$ and $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ has no
fluctuations at all.  The reading goes through the gauge-network
(edge-hopping) Dirac of Papers~25/30 --- the Marcolli--van~Suijlekom
setting of Sec.~\ref{sec:lineage} --- whose $U(1)$ link variables
fluctuate the edge data and whose quadratic kinetic term is $L_1$.  In
that setting $L_1$ is the Cartan-subalgebra (maximal-torus) sector, and
its assignment as ``photon propagator'' in Paper~25 is the
gauge-network spectral-action reading.  A statement of the same
identification on the non-diagonal multipliers of
$\mathcal{O}_{n_{\max}}$, with a backing test, is open.

\subsection{Paper 30 (SU(2) Wilson gauge): the full non-abelian sector}

Paper~30~\cite{paper30} builds the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Wilson lattice
gauge theory on the same graph and proves: (a) the maximal-torus
reduction reproduces Paper~25's $U(1)$ action exactly; (b) the
weak-coupling kinetic term equals $L_1$ acting on
$\mathfrak{su}(2)$-valued 1-cochains.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} In the
spectral-triple language the natural setting is the
almost-commutative algebra $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes
M_2(\mathbb{C})$ (a rank-1 non-abelian $M_2$ fiber).  With the
diagonal $D_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes \mathbb{1}$ this extension still has
no inner fluctuations ($[D_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes \mathbb{1}, a \otimes
m] = [D_{\mathrm{GV}}, a] \otimes m = 0$);\ the identification of
Paper~30's $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-valued link variables as the
fluctuations, and of the Wilson plaquette action as the lattice-gauge
implementation of the spectral-action curvature term, is made in the
Marcolli--van~Suijlekom gauge-network setting with its edge-hopping
Dirac (Sec.~\ref{sec:lineage}), where the order-one condition is
non-trivial.

Read that way, Paper~30 is the rank-1 non-abelian almost-commutative
gauge-network extension over the GeoVac label set; Paper~25 is the
abelian projection of Paper~30; together they exhibit the standard
Cartan torus / full group structure of that gauge sector.  They are
not fluctuations of the diagonal $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$.

\subsection{Paper 31 (universal/Coulomb partition): the
$\mathcal{A}/D$ split}

Paper~31~\cite{paper31} partitions GeoVac results into a universal
sector that depends only on the algebra $\mathcal{A}$ (angular
sparsity, composed scaling, selection rules) and a potential-specific
sector that depends on the Dirac operator $D$ (spectrum, calibration
$\kappa$, transcendental content, rigidity, $\alpha$ observation).  In
the spectral-triple language this is exactly the $\mathcal{A}/D$ split:
properties of $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ alone (rank, dimension,
representation content) transfer to any spectral triple with the same
algebra; properties of $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ specifically depend on the
choice of Dirac operator.  The $D$-dependent side is a statement about
\emph{spectral} data (spectrum, degeneracies, the transcendentals of
spectral sums):\ on the diagonal representative $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$
commutes with $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators}), so the split carries no
metric content of its own.

Paper~31 is the structural reading of Paper~22 (angular sparsity
theorem, an $\mathcal{A}$-statement) and Paper~23 (nuclear shell
model, a different $D$ on the harmonic-oscillator label set, hence a
different $\mathcal{A}$ sharing the angular $(l, m)$ factor): when one
keeps $\mathcal{A}$ fixed and varies $D$, the universal results
transfer; when one varies $\mathcal{A}$ (say, replace $S^3$ with
$S^5$, Paper~24), the entire structural picture reorganizes.

\paragraph{Track NI as an explicit composed real-space spectral triple.}
The ``Different $\mathcal{A}$ and $D$'' row of
Table~\ref{tab:subsectors} (Paper~23~\cite{paper23}, nuclear shell
model) covers a second class of construction in Paper~23~\S VI: the
composed nuclear--electronic deuterium Hamiltonian, a 26-qubit
Hilbert-space tensor product of a 16-qubit deuteron register
(harmonic-oscillator basis) with a 10-qubit hydrogenic electronic
register (Fock-projected $S^3$ lattice), coupled through a
cross-register hyperfine $\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{S}$ operator.  This
is structurally distinct from both the SM almost-commutative geometry
(which couples a Riemannian factor with a finite \emph{internal}
factor) and from the standard spectral-truncation convergence
machinery (which handles single triples or one-infinite-plus-one-finite
products).  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} It is read as a Connes-style
real-space multi-particle spectral triple;\ no axiom audit of the
composed triple is carried out.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} Its
Hamiltonian reproduces the singlet--triplet hyperfine pattern
$(-3A/4, +A/4)$ for the input coupling $A_{\rm hf}$ set to the $21\,$cm
value ($1.62 \times 10^{-7}$ Ha for the $3A/4$ shift) --- a structural
check of the Clebsch--Gordan pattern, not a measurement against an
external reference, since the coupling is the input.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The
positioning observation (that the canonical NCG / spectral-triple
literature does not contain a directly comparable construction) is
formalized in the standalone Track NI Zenodo memo
(\verb|papers/group4_quantum_computing/track_ni_spectral_triple_zenodo.md|).
The W2b cross-manifold structural blocker of
Sec.~\ref{sec:frontier_framing} is exactly the convergence-theorem
gap that surrounds this construction.

\paragraph{Paper 39 treats the same-manifold tensor-product case at the
state-space GH level.}
The same-manifold sub-case of W2b --- the tensor product
$\Tcal_{\sthree}^{\lambda_a} \otimes \Tcal_{\sthree}^{\lambda_b}$ of
two truncated Camporesi--Higuchi spectral triples on $\sthree$ at
distinct focal lengths $\lambda_a \neq \lambda_b$ --- is treated in a
companion standalone paper (Paper~39~\cite{paper39}) at the level of
van~Suijlekom's state-space Gromov--Hausdorff convergence;\ the
Latr\'emoli\`ere-propinquity question that defines W2b stays open. The result
extends Paper~38's convergence to the KO-dim-$6$ graded tensor product
via the \emph{lifted-state} construction (leading constant $1$):\ Paper~38's
unconditional-theorem facts, lifted factor-wise and metrized by the
translation seminorm, with the limit the graded (Clifford-doubled) tensor
product of the two odd Camporesi--Higuchi real spectral triples --- the
naive $\gamma_a \otimes D_b$ being unrealisable ($\gamma_a = 0$ on
$\sthree$).  This is
the mathematical foundation for the GeoVac multi-focal-composition
program, where two electrons in distinct hydrogenic shells live on
$\sthree$ at distinct focal lengths $\lambda = n/Z$;\ the convergence
theorem of Paper~39 says that the cross-register joint
operator system on $\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{\nmaxa} \otimes \mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{\nmaxb}$ (the true Camporesi--Higuchi windows, not the Fock/Weyl label-set systems) converges
qualitatively to the continuum tensor algebra $C^\infty(\sthree
\times \sthree)$ in van~Suijlekom's state-space Gromov--Hausdorff
distance (the Latr\'emoli\`ere propinquity strengthening is the same
open gap as for the single $\sthree$ factor; Remark~\ref{rem:gh_status_caveat}). The two
remaining cross-manifold variants
($\Tcal_{\sthree} \otimes \Tcal_{S^5}^{\mathrm{Bargmann}}$ and
$\Tcal_{\sthree} \otimes \Tcal_{S^5}^{\mathrm{Riem}}$) remain the W2b
content of Sec.~\ref{sec:frontier_framing}.

\begin{remark}[Multi-center composition: the reducible--irreducible transition]
\label{rem:multicenter_composition}
The multi-focal program above concerns two electrons at distinct focal
lengths on a single $\sthree$; the complementary \emph{multi-center}
composition concerns the orthogonal projections $P_A$ onto the
single-center orbital subspaces of a polyatomic molecule in the shared
Hilbert space with overlap metric $G$.  The natural setting is the
\emph{$*$-representation} theory of orthogonal projections.  For a
\emph{diatomic} the pair $\{P_A, P_B\}$ is \emph{tame} (Type~I): by the
two-projection theorem (Halmos~\cite{halmos1969}, with the principal-angle
norm and block calculus catalogued in B\"ottcher--Spitkovsky~\cite{bottcher_spitkovsky2010})
the generated $*$-algebra decomposes
into blocks of dimension $\le 2$, completely classified by the principal
angles between the two subspaces---\textbf{[MEASURED]} for LiH at $R_{\mathrm{eq}}$ the three
angles $7.6^\circ / 44.7^\circ / 67.3^\circ$, the middle one within
$0.3^\circ$ of $45^\circ$, so that $\lVert [P_A, P_B] \rVert = 0.49997$
sits essentially at the ceiling $\tfrac12$ (the ceiling itself, and the
reducibility of any pair, are forced by the two-projection theorem
\textbf{[SYMBOLIC]}; the molecular data supply only the angles).  The
$*$-algebra generated by
\emph{three or more} projections is, by contrast, $*$-wild in general;
the passage from two to three centers is precisely this tame-to-wild
threshold.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} What the molecular data adds is that the three-center
configuration lands on an \emph{irreducible} (Schurian) point of that
wild family: for linear $\mathrm{BeH}_2$ (the $\sigma$ pair
$\{1s, 2p_0\}$ per center) the three center-projections generate the
\emph{full} matrix algebra $M_6$ and act irreducibly (commutant
$= \mathbb{C}\,\mathbb{1}$), welding the whole space into a single
indecomposable block that admits no decomposition; every pair remains
reducible, as it must by the two-projection theorem.

Tameness is not composability, and conflating the two misreads the
obstruction.  The diatomic pair is completely classified, but it
emphatically does not commute---$\lVert [P_A, P_B] \rVert = 0.49997$ is
essentially the maximal possible value $\tfrac12$---and it is that \emph{two}-projection
non-commutativity, not the three-projection wildness, which constitutes
the multi-focal composition wall recorded above.  The obstruction
accordingly has two distinct rungs.  At two centers the projections fail
to commute while remaining completely classified by their principal
angles: the obstruction is quantitative, and the cost of removing it is
known in closed form.  At three centers the classification itself fails,
and no complete list of invariants exists.  Forcing commutation at the
first rung---L\"owdin orthogonalization---is the framework's
sparsity-destroying move; at the second rung there is no corresponding
normal form available to force.

The wildness is specific to the subspace configuration and is \emph{not}
a generic consequence of non-commutativity.  The framework's other
non-commutative angular structure---$SO(3)/SU(2)$ coupling, the Gaunt and
$6j$ machinery---is the representation theory of a \emph{compact} group,
hence type~I by Peter--Weyl, hence tame however non-abelian it may be.
\textbf{[MEASURED]} The contrast is quantitative: on the menu $l = 0, 1, 2$ the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$
generators satisfy $\lVert [J_a, J_b] \rVert = 2$ yet their commutant is
three-dimensional (one dimension per irreducible constituent, and
$\sum_\lambda m_\lambda^2$ in general---four for $l\!=\!1$ at multiplicity
two, five for $l\!=\!1 \oplus l\!=\!2 \oplus l\!=\!2$), whereas the three
center-projections, at the \emph{smaller} non-commutativity
$\lVert [P_A, P_B] \rVert \approx 0.49$, have commutant
$\mathbb{C}\mathbb{1}$.  Non-abelianness and wildness are independent
properties.  This is the structural reason the framework's angular
sparsity is graded, finite and potential-independent (Paper~22), and the
reason no Gaunt-style graded reduction should be expected to rescue
multi-center composition: arbitrary configurations of subspaces are not
group representations and escape Peter--Weyl.

Two invariants must be kept separate.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} (i)~The \emph{categorical}
statement---pairs reducible, triple irreducible---is robust: it survives
enriching the per-center $\sigma$ menu
$\{1s, 2p_0\} \!\to\! \{1s, 2s, 2p_0\}$ ($M_6 \!\to\! M_9$, irreducible
at the physical $Z=2$ charge, regression-pinned;\ the structural $Z=1$
case is likewise regression-pinned,
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition\_pins.py}) and holds at real
molecular geometries.  It is, however, a statement about center
\emph{count}: three coupled projections in general position are
irreducible at essentially any bonding-relevant separation, the
commutant reverting to reducible only once residual overlaps fall below
resolution.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} (ii)~The bonding-dependent content is instead the
\emph{magnitude} of the irreducible three-body coupling, measured by the
nested commutator $\lVert [[P_A, P_B], P_C] \rVert$ (which cannot even
be formed for a diatomic): $0.32$ at bonding (regression-pinned,
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition\_pins.py}), decaying smoothly
to zero as the centers separate.

\textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} Geometry sharpens the picture.  At \emph{exactly} linear geometry the
in-plane basis $\{s, p_x, p_z\}$ per center decouples by axial symmetry
into a $\sigma$ and a $\pi$ sector and the nine-dimensional algebra is
reducible; \emph{any} bend mixes the sectors and welds the full space
into one irreducible $M_9$ block (regression-pinned at the
$\mathrm{H_2O}$ geometry $104.5^\circ$;\ the sweep across
$160^\circ \!\to\! 90^\circ$ and apex charges $Z = 1, 2, 4$ is likewise
regression-pinned, \texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition\_pins.py})---the operator-algebra
face of the Renner--Teller mechanism.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} As an internal observation the
configuration operator $F = \sum_i P_i$ carries, over molecular
geometry, a lattice of conical intersections each with a $\pi$
($\mathbb{Z}_2$) Berry phase and, under bending, a structural
Renner--Teller promotion; this is the \emph{generic} topology of a
symmetric-triatomic degeneracy, distinct from the molecule's physical
electronic conical intersection, not an identity with it.  (Computed and
regression-pinned in \texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition.py} on
validated two-center overlaps, with the commutant routine self-validated
on cases of known answer; chronicle in CHANGELOG v5.1.0.)

\textbf{[SYMBOLIC + MEASURED]} The Hamiltonian face of the same welding is
the Renner effect~\cite{renner1934}, and its bookkeeping splits along the
forced/free seam.  Take one electron, an apex charge $Z_c$ and two unit
charges at distance $R$, bent by $s$ (the sine of half the deviation from
linear).  A $|m| = \Lambda$ pair then opens at order $s^{2\Lambda}$:\ the
bend changes $m$ by one unit per power of $s$, and $+\Lambda$ reaches
$-\Lambda$ in $2\Lambda$ steps.  On a single shell the angular prices are
the exact rationals $6/5$ ($\Lambda = 1$) and $5/6$ ($\Lambda = 2$)
(\texttt{tests/test\_renner\_teller\_m\_splitting.py}).  These are forced.
The coefficient itself is free.  In closed-form bases its radial weights
carry exponentials in $R$ and no $\pi$
(\texttt{tests/test\_renner\_teller\_rung2.py}).  At $Z_c = 1$, $R = 2$ the
converged one-centre referee gives $0.44941$ per $s^2$ for
$E_{\rm in} - E_{\rm out}$.  That referee uses a complete finite-element
radial basis and partial waves to $l = 55$, with exact second-order
perturbation theory checked against finite bends
(\texttt{tests/test\_renner\_teller\_rung4.py}).

Two structural facts survive the numbers.  First, the $|m| = 2$ relay
shifts both $\pi$ components equally and cancels exactly from the
splitting.  Second, one $\sigma$ partner carries most of the $\sigma$
relay, and which side of the $\pi$ level it sits on fixes the sign.  The
partner changes with $R$:\ it is the protons' symmetric $1s$ combination
for $R = 2$--$6$, and the apex's $n = 2$, $l = 0$ level ($\pi$'s former
$l$-sibling) for $R \ge 8$.  It crosses $\pi$ at $R = 1.667$, where the
coefficient diverges and changes sign.

The same machinery with parameter-free screened atomic potentials
(Fermi--Amaldi self-interaction factor) reproduces Walsh's AH$_2$
diagram~\cite{walsh1953} in sign and order
(\texttt{tests/test\_walsh\_ah2.py}):\ $3a_1$ falls, $1b_2$ rises, $1b_1$ is
flat.  The doubly occupied orbital sum bends H$_2$O robustly.  For BeH$_2$
it nearly cancels, so in this model the orbital-sum rule alone does not
decide BeH$_2$.  None of this is new chemistry.  What the framework adds is
the sort:\ label survival, opening order and filling count are forced;
slopes and partner positions are free.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The configuration operator's Berry curvature: a flat $\mathbb{Z}_2$ bundle over nuclear geometry]
\label{rem:config_berry_curvature}
The configuration operator $F = \sum_i P_i$ of the previous remark varies
over the nuclear-geometry manifold $\mathcal{M}$, and its geometric-phase
structure is the finished form of the ``lattice of conical intersections''
noted there.  \textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} Because the $\sigma$ orbitals are real, $F$ is real-symmetric
at every geometry, so its Berry connection is \emph{flat} with only a
$\mathbb{Z}_2$ ($\pm$) holonomy: the curvature is not a smooth field but a
sum of $\pi$-flux Dirac deltas at the conical intersections,
\[
  \Omega \;=\; \sum_{\text{CI}} \pi\,\delta^2(m - m_{\text{CI}}),
\]
the Longuet--Higgins sign-change / molecular Aharonov--Bohm structure
(Longuet-Higgins; Berry; Mead--Truhlar) realized here for $F$ rather than the
electronic Hamiltonian.  \textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} On the linear-stretch plane $(d_1, d_2)$ the
Fukui--Hatsugai--Suzuki lattice flux is exactly $0$ or $\pi$ on every
plaquette (forced by the reality of $F$;\ the machine-exact regression
check is a consistency check, not independent evidence).
\textbf{[MEASURED]} There are three $\pi$-sources---the central
intersection at $(2.45, 2.45)$ and an antisymmetric-stretch mirror pair---
carried by the two crossing bands and absent on the other four (the
central source and the mirror pair are both regression-pinned,
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition\_pins.py}).  The holonomy
is the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ representation of $\pi_1(\mathcal{M} \setminus
\mathrm{CI})$ sending a loop to $\pi \cdot (\#\,\text{enclosed} \bmod 2)$,
cross-checked by direct parallel transport ($\pi$ around one intersection and
around all three).

\textbf{[MEASURED]} Each intersection is the real (equatorial) section of a charge-$\tfrac12$
Berry monopole: in a fixed real frame the local $2\times2$ model is
$F_0 + a_z \sigma_z + a_x \sigma_x$ with the $\sigma_y$ coefficient
\emph{identically zero} (\textbf{[SYMBOLIC]}:\ forced by $F$ real-symmetric)---the real family lives on the sphere's equator---and
branching vectors $g = \nabla a_z$ along the symmetric stretch (the tuning
mode) and $h = \nabla a_x$ along the antisymmetric stretch (the coupling
mode), with nonzero winding.  \textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ (rather than $U(1)$)
character is \emph{forced by time-reversal symmetry}---the reality of the
Coulomb overlaps---not by planarity: real orbitals give real overlaps in any
geometry, so a non-coplanar real center leaves $F$ real-symmetric and does not
lift the phase.  Breaking time reversal does.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} A magnetic (Peierls) phase
$\phi$ threading the $\mathrm{H}$--$\mathrm{Be}$--$\mathrm{H}$ loop makes $F$
complex-Hermitian, opens the empty $\sigma_y$ axis, and turns each intersection
into a genuine $U(1)$ Berry monopole of integer charge: the first Chern number
over a small sphere enclosing $(d^*, d^*, 0)$ in $(d_1, d_2, \phi)$ is $\pm1$
(opposite on the two crossing bands), and the real $\phi = 0$ plane cuts the
monopole at its equator, recovering the $\pi$ holonomy.
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} This is an internal
observation about the geometry of the composition, distinct from the
molecule's physical electronic conical intersection.  (Regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_berry\_curvature.py}; chronicle in CHANGELOG
v5.1.0.)
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Four centers: a continuous four-body modulus (the $\tilde D_4$ analog)]
\label{rem:four_center_modulus}
The three-center geometric phase of Remark~\ref{rem:config_berry_curvature}
is discrete ($\mathbb{Z}_2$ conical intersections).  A \emph{fourth} center
adds a genuinely new, \emph{continuous} four-body invariant.  With
$S_{ij}=Q_i^{\!\top}Q_j$ the metric-orthonormal overlaps between center
subspaces, the loop holonomy of this overlap connection around the
$4$-cycle,
$W=\operatorname{Tr}(S_{12}S_{23}S_{34}S_{41})=\operatorname{Tr}(P_1P_2P_3P_4)$,
is invariant under the per-center gauge $Q_i\mapsto Q_iO_i$ ($O_i\in O(r)$)
and is therefore a physical configuration invariant.  Two facts make it the
four-center analogue of an elliptic modulus.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} First, a \emph{threshold}: for
four rank-one (single-orbital) subspaces $W$ is a function of the pairwise
and triple traces---the $4\times4$ Gram is the entire configuration, so
there is no four-body degree of freedom---whereas for rank $\ge2$ it is not
(a local Jacobian-nullspace test gives residual $\gtrsim0.3$).  A genuine
four-body invariant appears exactly when each center carries a
$\sigma$--$\pi$ pair, mirroring the rank-one$\to$rank-two step in the tame
four-subspace ($\tilde D_4$) problem.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} Second, over molecular shape $W$ is a
\emph{smooth continuous modulus}: for a planar rhombus with $\{1s,2p\}$ per
center it runs monotonically (from $\approx 0.8$ to $\approx 0.04$ over
the sprint's diagonal-ratio range;\ the monotonicity and the sprint-range
endpoints ($0.79 \to 0.042$) are both regression-pinned,
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_composition\_pins.py}) as the diagonal ratio varies
---a one-parameter family of inequivalent four-center configurations, the
continuous freedom the three-center case lacks.  For real orbitals $W$ is
real (its phase is $\mathbb{Z}_2$); a magnetic (Peierls) flux through the
$4$-cycle makes $\arg W$ grow continuously from zero---the four-body $U(1)$
lift.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The object is the $*$-representation (non-abelian holonomy) analogue
of the $\tilde D_4$ cross-ratio: four \emph{orthogonal} projections are
$*$-wild rather than tame, so the modulus is a matrix loop holonomy, not a
single scalar cross-ratio.  It is a geometry-axis invariant---a function of
nuclear shape---distinct from the density-axis cross-ratio of the
three-center integral in Paper~59~\cite{loutey_paper59}.  (Regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_four\_center\_modulus.py}; chronicle in
CHANGELOG v5.1.0.)
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The configuration operator is the composed basis's overlap metric]
\label{rem:config_operator_is_metric}
The three preceding remarks treat $F = \sum_i P_i$ as an abstract
configuration operator.  It is not abstract.  Let $G$ be the Gram matrix
of the union of the per-center bases, with blocks
$G_{ij} = \langle \chi_i | \chi_j \rangle$, and suppose each
\emph{intra}-center block is the identity---automatic when each center's
own functions are orthonormal, and exact for the Shibuya--Wulfman metric
of Paper~60~\cite{paper60}.  Put $X = \operatorname{chol}(G)^{\mathsf T}$,
so that $X^{\mathsf T} X = G$ and the columns of $X$ are the basis vectors
expressed in an orthonormal ambient frame; let $W_k$ collect the columns
belonging to center $k$.  Then $W_k^{\mathsf T} W_k = G_{kk} = I$, so
$W_k$ has orthonormal columns, the pseudoinverse in $P_k = W_k W_k^{+}$
collapses to $W_k^{\mathsf T}$, and
\[
   F \;=\; \sum_k W_k W_k^{\mathsf T} \;=\; X X^{\mathsf T}
     \;=\; X\,(X^{\mathsf T} X)\,X^{-1} \;=\; X\,G\,X^{-1}.
\]
\textbf{[SYMBOLIC PROOF]} Hence $F \simeq G$---\emph{similar}, not merely cospectral.  Every
spectral statement in the preceding remarks (the conical intersections,
the band gaps, the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ holonomy of the eigenvectors) is
therefore a statement about the \emph{overlap metric of the composed
basis} over nuclear geometry.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} Verified across the whole construction:
linear $\mathrm{BeH_2}$, bent $\mathrm{BeH_2}$ (including the
$\mathrm{H_2O}$ angle $104.5^\circ$), and the four-center configuration,
with $\max|\operatorname{spec} F - \operatorname{spec} G| \le
3\times10^{-15}$ and $\lVert FX - XG \rVert_{\max} \le 9\times10^{-16}$.
The intra-center hypothesis is load-bearing rather than decorative:
perturbing one intra-center block off the identity by $0.3$ breaks the
identity outright (spectral discrepancy $6.7\times10^{-2}$).  The
four-center holonomy of Remark~\ref{rem:four_center_modulus} is the same
statement read blockwise,
$\operatorname{Tr}(P_1P_2P_3P_4) =
\operatorname{Tr}(G_{12}G_{23}G_{34}G_{41})$ (residual
$3\times10^{-16}$).

Two consequences follow, one positive and one negative.  \textbf{[MEASURED]} \emph{(i)~An
orthogonalization criterion.}  Since the intersections are degeneracies
of the metric, the eigenvectors of $G$ are double-valued around one:
parallel transport of the near-degenerate eigenvector around a small loop
enclosing the central intersection returns it with overlap $-1.0000$,
against $+1.0000$ on a CI-free control.  Any orthogonalization that
\emph{selects} metric eigenvectors---canonical orthogonalization with a
drop threshold, the standard remedy for near-linear-dependence---is
therefore path-dependent along a geometry scan through such a point,
whereas symmetric (L\"owdin) orthogonalization $G^{-1/2}$, being a
function of the matrix rather than of its eigenvectors, returns
bit-exactly (\textbf{[SYMBOLIC]}:\ single-valuedness of any matrix
function; the numerical return is a consistency check).  \textbf{[MEASURED]} \emph{(ii)~It is not a conditioning statement.}  The
intersections are \emph{mid}-spectrum degeneracies (crossing eigenvalue
$\approx 0.71$ against $\lambda_{\min} \approx 0.13$), and
$\operatorname{cond}(G)$ passes through $d^*$ monotonically
($19.9 \to 16.2 \to 15.0$); the metric-conditioning growth of Paper~60 is
a different functional of the same object, and no kink at $d^*$ should be
expected or claimed.  The distinction drawn in the preceding remarks
between $F$ and the molecule's \emph{physical electronic} conical
intersection is unchanged; what changes is that $F$ is an invariant of the
framework's own encoding rather than a generic operator introduced for the
analysis.  (Regression-pinned in
\texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_config\_operator\_metric.py}; chronicle in
CHANGELOG v5.1.2.)
\end{remark}

% ===================================================================
\section{The $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ Formula in Spectral-Action
Language}
\label{sec:K_decomposition}
% ===================================================================

Paper~2~\cite{paper2} observed the numerical coincidence
$K = \pi(B + F - \Delta) \approx 1/\alpha$ at $4.77 \times 10^{-7}$ (raw;\ the $8.8 \times 10^{-8}$ figure is the match of the root of Paper~2's cubic $1/\alpha = K - \alpha^2$, not of $K$ itself), with
\[
   B = \gvq{b_casimir}{42}, \quad F = \gvq{f_fock_dirichlet}{\tfrac{\pi^2}{6}}, \quad \Delta = \gvq{delta_dirac}{\tfrac{1}{40}},
\]
and the Phase~4B--4I sprint series identified spectral homes of
distinct type for the three pieces (project development-frontier log):
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{0pt}
\item $B$ is the finite Casimir trace at $m=3$:
$B = \sum_{n=1}^{3} (2l+1)l(l+1)|_{n,l} = 42$ (Paper~2 Eq.~17).
\item $F = D_{n^2}(d_{\max} = 4) = \zeta_R(2)$, the Fock-degeneracy
Dirichlet series at the packing exponent (Phase~4F $\alpha$-J).
\item $\Delta^{-1} = g_3^{\mathrm{D}} = 2(3+1)(3+2) = 40$, the total
(both-sign) Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac degeneracy at level $k = 3$,
$|\lambda| = k + 3/2 = 9/2$ (Paper~7's $g_3^{\mathrm{Dirac}}$; Phase~4H SM-D).
\end{itemize}

In spectral-triple language, $B$, $F$, $\Delta$ live in three different
sectors of the same triple:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item $B$ is a \emph{finite scalar Casimir trace} on the truncated
algebra: in the language of Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit}, it is
$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}}(C \cdot \mathbb{1})$ at
$n_{\max} = 3$, either for $C = L^2$ the $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ Casimir
$l(l+1)$ (the sum printed above) or for $C = (n^2 - 1)/2$, half the
$\mathrm{SO}(4)$ Casimir $L^2 + A^2 = n^2 - 1$;\ with the unhalved
$\mathrm{SO}(4)$ Casimir the same trace is $84 = 2B$ (both routes'
agreement at $42$, and the un-halved trace at $84$, are regression-pinned
in \texttt{tests/test\_paper32\_B\_normalisation.py}).
\item $F$ is an \emph{infinite scalar spectral zeta}: it equals
$\zeta_{1-\Delta_{S^3}}(2) = \sum_{n \ge 1} n^2 \cdot n^{-4} = \zeta(2)$
in the continuum limit, where $\Delta_{S^3}$ is the scalar
Laplace--Beltrami operator on $S^3$ and $1 - \Delta_{S^3}$ has
eigenvalue $n^2$ with the Fock degeneracy $n^2$.  It is \emph{not}
$\zeta_{-\Delta_{S^3}}(2) = \sum_{n \ge 2} n^2/(n^2-1)^2 \approx 0.885$.
\item $\Delta^{-1}$ is a \emph{single-level Dirac degeneracy}: it
equals $g_3^{\mathrm{D}}$, the total multiplicity (both signs) of the
Camporesi--Higuchi level $k = 3$, $|\lambda_3^{\mathrm{D}}| = 9/2$.
\end{itemize}

These three are categorically different objects: a finite
combinatorial trace, an infinite analytic Dirichlet series, and a
single-level multiplicity.  They sit in different sectors of the
spectral triple (scalar/spinor; finite/infinite; trace/zeta/dimension)
and \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Phases 4B--4I made eight unsuccessful attempts to find a common spectral
generator that produces all three simultaneously, without success
(twelve counting Sprint~A and Sprint~K-CC; see the E6
no-single-mechanism observation, Observation~\ref{obs:no_single_mechanism_K}).

\begin{observation}[Three-sector composition]
\label{obs:three_sector}
The Paper~2 combination rule $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ is a sum across
three structurally distinct sectors of the GeoVac spectral triple.
Each piece has been individually identified as a clean spectral
invariant; the combination rule itself remains a numerical observation.
\end{observation}

This rephrasing is what working hypothesis WH5 in the project register
is built on: $\alpha$ is the conversion factor between three projection
regimes that do not share a generator.  The spectral-triple framing
does not derive $\alpha$; it makes precise where the obstruction is.

\begin{remark}[Sprint A, $\pi^3 \alpha^3$ residual]
\label{rem:sprint_A}
\textbf{[MEASURED]} Sprint~A (April~2026, working-hypothesis register) verified at 80~dps that the
post-cubic residual $K - 1/\alpha - \alpha^2 = 1.2079 \times 10^{-5}$
matches $\pi^3 \alpha^3 = 1.2049 \times 10^{-5}$ to within 0.25\%
(CODATA~2018 $\alpha$;\ with the CODATA~2022 value the residual is
$1.1986 \times 10^{-5}$, $0.52\%$ \emph{below} $\pi^3\alpha^3$, so the
agreement is not stable at this level across editions).
The structural reading is $\pi^3 = \mathrm{Vol}(S^5)$, hinting at a
contribution from the Bargmann--Segal $S^5$ HO sector
(Paper~24~\cite{paper24}) at higher order.  This is a structural
hint, not a derivation; the cross-check (Paper~24's HO triple is a
distinct spectral triple, not a sub-sector of the GeoVac triple)
shows that the $\pi^3 \alpha^3$ identification, if real, is a
\emph{cross-triple} relation and not internal to the GeoVac
spectral-action expansion.
\end{remark}

\section{The Coulomb/HO Asymmetry in Spectral-Triple Language}
\label{sec:coulomb_ho}
% ===================================================================

Paper~31~\cite{paper31} formalized a layered asymmetry (Paper~24
\S 5 catalogues seven layers, six standing;\ the table below
tabulates structural rows, including the spectrum-computing role of
$L_0$, withdrawn and marked; the
CM Hodge structure is Paper~24 \S 5) between the
Coulomb spectral triple of the present paper and the harmonic
oscillator spectral triple of Paper~24~\cite{paper24}, summarized
here:

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\toprule
Layer & Coulomb (this paper) & Harmonic oscillator (Paper~24) \\
\midrule
Manifold algebra $\mathcal{A}$ &
  Functions on $S^3$ Fock graph &
  Functions on $S^5$ holomorphic Hardy graph \\
Projection order &
  Second-order (scalar Laplace--Beltrami)$^{*}$ &
  First-order (complex Euler) \\
Spectrum &
  Quadratic $n^2 - 1$ (scalar LB)$^{*}$ &
  Linear $E_N = N + 3/2$ \\
Calibration constant &
  $\pi$ (volume slot $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4$) &
  None ($\pi$-free in exact arithmetic) \\
Wilson + natural matter &
  $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, matter rep $=$ link rep (argued) &
  Pure gauge only; matter needs CG \\
$L_0$ spectrum (withdrawn) &
  No ($L_0 \not\to \Delta_{S^3}$) &
  No (spectrum diagonal) \\ % [retracted 2026-09-26: coulomb-ho-layer1-and-three-discriminators]
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The Coulomb/HO asymmetry from Paper~31, in spectral-triple
language.  The two systems are distinct spectral triples sharing only
the algebra type (functions on a graph) and the trace structure of the
spectral action.  $^{*}$The rows describe the Fock projection's scalar
sector; this paper's Dirac operator $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ is first order
with spectrum $\pm(n + 3/2)$, and the order contrast of the row is
between the two \emph{projections} (conformal vs.\ holomorphic).}
\label{tab:coulomb_ho}
\end{table}

\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} In spectral-triple language the asymmetry is structural: the Coulomb
case is (in its continuum limit) a Riemannian-spin spectral triple of $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~3,
the HO case is a spectral triple of complex Euler type on the
holomorphic Hardy space of $S^5$.  These are different objects even
though both are ``almost-commutative'' in the broad sense.  Calibration
$\pi$ enters in the Coulomb case through the $k = 0$ volume slot
$\mathrm{Vol}(S^2) / 4$ in the spectral-action coefficients; it does
not enter the HO case because the HO spectral action is built from a
linear (first-order) operator whose heat kernel does not produce $\pi$
at the rational level of the truncation~\cite{paper24}.

\begin{observation}[Coulomb-specificity of $\pi$]
\label{obs:pi_coulomb}
The presence of $\pi$ in the spectral-action coefficients of QED is
specific to the Coulomb-projected $S^3$ triple of this paper, not a
universal feature of GeoVac-style discretizations.  The HO triple is
$\pi$-free at the discrete level.  On this reading QED's $\pi$
enters through the Coulomb-projection $S^3$ triple:\ through the
$k = 0$ volume slot $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4$ (M1) and the spectral
action's $\pi^{2k}$ (M2).
\end{observation}

\begin{theorem}[G3 closure: Bertrand-rigidity category obstruction]%
\label{thm:g3_closure}
\textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT; scoped to published constructions]}
Within standard Connes spectral triples and Berezin--Toeplitz
quantization, there is no tensor-product spectral triple
$\Tcal_{\sthree} \otimes \Tcal^{\mathrm{BL}}_{S^5}$
that simultaneously preserves:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] the Riemannian-Dirac character of $\Tcal_{\sthree}$
  (second-order conformal projection and spectral action built from
  $D^2$, spinor bundle, first-order Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac with
  spectrum $|\lambda_n| = n + 3/2$); and
\item[(b)] the Bargmann character of $\Tcal^{\mathrm{BL}}_{S^5}$
  ($\pi$-free certificate, Hardy-sector tower structure,
  first-order Euler spectrum $E_N = N + 3/2$, Paper~24).
\end{enumerate}
The obstruction is categorical and rests on three premises:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Bertrand's theorem (1873):}  the only central
  potentials with all bounded orbits closed are $V = -Z/r$
  (Coulomb) and $V = \omega^2 r^2$ (harmonic oscillator).
\item \emph{Fock rigidity (Paper~23~\cite{paper23}, Thm.~4):}
  the $\sthree$ conformal projection is unique to $-Z/r$;\ the
  projection is second-order (stereographic / conformal), yielding
  a Riemannian spectral triple with the Laplace--Beltrami
  operator.
\item \emph{HO rigidity (Paper~24~\cite{paper24}, Thm.~3):}
  the $S^5$ Bargmann--Segal projection is unique to $\omega^2 r^2$;\
  the projection is first-order (complex-analytic / holomorphic),
  yielding a Berezin--Toeplitz construction with the Euler number
  operator on the Hardy space.
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
\emph{Step 1 (category identification).}
Fock rigidity and HO rigidity place the two potentials in different
mathematical categories: Coulomb $\to$ second-order conformal
$\to$ Riemannian spectral triple; HO $\to$ first-order
holomorphic $\to$ Berezin--Toeplitz quantization.

\emph{Step 2 (exhaustion of tensor-product options).}
Three constructions are available:
(i)~the standard graded tensor product with the round-$S^5$
Riemannian Dirac---this preserves~(a) but violates~(b)
($\pi$ re-enters via the Seeley--DeWitt coefficients on $S^5$);
(ii)~the tensor product with the Bargmann--Euler operator---this
preserves~(b) but violates~(a)
($E_{\mathrm{BL}}$ is not a Dirac-type operator on $S^5$,
being the radial part of the Laplacian restricted to the
holomorphic sector, not an elliptic differential operator
on the total space);
(iii)~a hypothetical framework-extending construction beyond
standard Connes spectral triples and Berezin--Toeplitz
quantization---we know of no such framework in the published
mathematics (a literature-absence premise, which is why the theorem is
scoped to published constructions).

\emph{Step 3 (irreducibility).}
Bertrand exhausts the potentials;\ the two rigidity theorems fix
the projection type for each.  The category mismatch is a
structural feature of central-force quantum mechanics, not an
artifact of the GeoVac construction.  The asymmetry layers of
Paper~24~\S~V (seven catalogued, six standing;\ five are listed here,
the remaining two, including the
CM Hodge structure, are in Paper~24~\S~V) --- spectrum-computing role of $L_0$ (\emph{withdrawn}:\
$L_0$ does not converge to $\Delta_{S^3}$ and is
spectrally inert on both spheres); calibration $\pi$; Wilson gauge
with natural matter coupling (a representation-theoretic layer, not an
$L_0$ one); modular-Hamiltonian Pythagorean orthogonality;
spectral-action gravity termination --- are, the withdrawn one
excepted, read here as downstream of this single categorical
obstruction;\ the proof sketch argues the obstruction itself, not each
layer's derivation from it.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[G3 scope]%
\label{rem:g3_scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:g3_closure} does not assert that $S^3$ and $S^5$
are unrelated --- they are the two Bertrand geometries, deeply connected
through the angular sparsity theorem (Paper~22).  It asserts that their
\emph{spectral-triple-type structures} live in different mathematical
categories, and no published framework bridges the gap while preserving
both structural contents.  Cross-manifold unification, if achievable,
requires framework-extending mathematics beyond the scope of this paper
and the published NCG literature.
\end{remark}

% ===================================================================
\section{The $\pi$-Source Case-Exhaustion Theorem}
\label{sec:pi_source_theorem}
% ===================================================================

\textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} The empirical principle of Paper~35~\cite{paper35} (Prediction~1,
graduated to load-bearing status on a cumulative 208/208 confirmation
across sprints KG and TX-B, May~2026) states that a GeoVac observable
contains $\pi$ if and only if its evaluation includes a continuous
integration over a temporal or spectral parameter promoted from the
discrete label/Dirac spectrum.  Read in the spectral-triple language of
\S\ref{sec:construction}--\S\ref{sec:axiom_audit}, this principle has
a structural underpinning: in chains of the first fifteen Paper~34
projections, the places where such continuous integrations occur are
anchored to three named mechanisms of the present triple.  \textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} We state the
case-exhaustion below and give its proof sketch;\ the
(i)$\Leftrightarrow$(ii) leg rests on the empirical Paper~35
verification rather than on a proof, and the converse
(iii)$\Rightarrow$(i) is not established:\ no mechanism is
known to put $\pi$ into every one of its outputs (see the proof sketch).

\begin{theorem}[$\pi$-Source Case-Exhaustion]
\label{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}
Let $\mathcal{C} = (P_{i_k} \circ \cdots \circ P_{i_1})$ be any finite
composition of projections drawn from the Paper~34 list~\cite{paper34}
\S\,III.1--\S\,III.15, applied to a Layer-1 datum on
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ or its Bargmann--Segal sibling triple.  The
following hold:\ (i)$\Leftrightarrow$(ii) (the empirical leg) and
(ii)$\Rightarrow$(iii).  The converse (iii)$\Rightarrow$(i) is not
established (see the proof sketch):
\begin{enumerate}
\setlength\itemsep{0pt}
\item[(i)] The converged value $\mathcal{C}(\mathrm{datum})$ contains a
transcendental of the form $\pi$, $\sqrt{\pi}$, $\pi^k$ for $k \in
\mathbb{Q}$, or $\pi$ in a denominator.
\item[(ii)] The chain $\mathcal{C}$ contains at least one projection
$P_{i_j}$ whose evaluation pipeline includes a continuous integration
over a parameter promoted from the discrete label/Dirac spectrum
(heat-kernel time, Matsubara frequency, Mellin contour, $S^2$
angular (volume-slot) measure, $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Haar measure, or
analytic-continuation parameter).
\item[(iii)] The chain $\mathcal{C}$ engages at least one of three
spectral-triple mechanisms:
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0pt}
\item[\textbf{M1}] (the $k = 0$ volume slot):\ introduces $\pi =
\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4$ via the Hopf fibration $S^1 \to S^3 \to S^2$,
or its higher-rank analogues~\cite{paper25,paper30}.
\item[\textbf{M2}] (Seeley--DeWitt heat-kernel coefficient):\
introduces $\sqrt{\pi}$ via heat-kernel coefficients on the
Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac spectrum on unit~$S^3$ (Paper~28
Theorem~T9~\cite{paper28}: $\zeta_{D^2}(s)$ is a two-term polynomial
in $\pi^2$ with rational coefficients at every integer $s \ge 1$;\ at
half-integer $s \ge 5/2$ it carries odd zeta values instead).
The sub-mechanism for the spinor sector is the half-integer Hurwitz
$\zeta(s, 3/2)$.
\item[\textbf{M3}] (vertex-topology Dirichlet $L$-content):\
introduces Catalan~$G = \beta(2)$ and Dirichlet $\beta(s)$ via
quarter-integer Hurwitz shifts $\zeta(s, 3/4)$, $\zeta(s, 5/4)$ at
two-loop sunset vertices (Paper~28 Theorem~3:\
$D_{\mathrm{even}}(s) - D_{\mathrm{odd}}(s) = 2^{s-1}(\beta(s) -
\beta(s-2))$).
\end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i)~$\Leftrightarrow$~(ii) is Paper~35 Prediction~1, verified across
208 individual checks (sprints KG, TX-B, May 2026~\cite{paper35}).

(ii)~$\Rightarrow$~(iii):\ the proof body that follows enumerates the
fifteen Paper~34 projections in §III.1--§III.15 (the corpus state at
this theorem's drafting; Paper~34 has since grown to twenty-eight
projections~\cite{paper34}, and the theorem's claim is here restricted
to those first fifteen pending an extension of the case-by-case
enumeration to projections~16--28).  Each of the fifteen Paper~34
projections is
either $\pi$-free at the projection step (projections~1,~3,~4,~5,~8,
9,~12,~13,~14: Fock conformal, Bargmann--Segal, Stereographic, Sturmian,
Wigner~$3j$, Wigner~$D$, Molecule-frame, Drake--Swainson, Rest-mass) or
$\pi$-bearing through a continuous integration that reduces to one of
M1,~M2,~M3 by direct identification (Hopf~bundle, Spectral~action,
Camporesi--Higuchi spinor lift, Wilson plaquette, Vector-photon promotion,
Observation/temporal-window).  The nine $\pi$-free projections live in
either Paper~31's universal sector (algebra-only, transfers to any
spherical-fermion system) or Paper~22's potential-independent angular
sparsity tier; neither sector contains $\pi$.  The six $\pi$-bearing
projections are tagged, one by one, to the mechanism of their
$\pi$-bearing integration:\ Hopf bundle, Wilson plaquette (Haar volume)
and vector-photon promotion to M1 (Paper~25);\ spectral action (also
M1), Camporesi--Higuchi spinor lift (half-integer Hurwitz) and
observation/temporal window (Matsubara $\zeta_R(2k)$) to M2 (Paper~28
T9);\ M3 arises only in the composition of the spinor lift with a
vertex-parity rule (Paper~28 Theorem~3).  This leg is a tagging, made
case by case and recorded in the sprint chronicle (CHANGELOG);\ it has
no regression test, which is one reason the theorem is
\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]}.

The converse (iii)~$\Rightarrow$~(i) is not established:\
engaging a mechanism is not known to force $\pi$ into the converged
value.
Paper~55~\cite{loutey_paper55} tags the rational Casimir trace $B = 42$ as M1's
``rational core'' (a tagging convention, not a proof that $B$ is the
converged value of a chain engaging M1);\ the spinor spectral
zeta of M2's sub-mechanism at half-integer $s$ carries no known $\pi$,
e.g.\ $\zeta_{D^2}(5/2) = 14\,\zeta(3) - \tfrac{31}{2}\,\zeta(5)$
(Paper~28 T9;\ this lies outside the pure-Tate M2 ring, which is
scoped to Seeley--DeWitt coefficients and integer $s$);\ M3's
convergent closed form at $s = 4$ gives
$2^{3}(\beta(4) - \beta(2)) = 8(\beta(4) - G)$, with no known $\pi$
(none of $\zeta(3)$, $\zeta(5)$, $G$, $\beta(4)$ is known to lie in
$\mathbb{Q}[\pi]$, nor proved not to).  What remains
closed-form is the forward identification per mechanism:\ M1's
$\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4 = \pi$ and M2's heat-kernel $a_0(S^3) = \sqrt{\pi}$
carry $\pi$ where those specific quantities enter.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[M1 sub-mechanism lies in the localised pure-Tate
sub-ring generated by $\pi$]
\label{cor:m1_pure_tate}
The M1 sub-mechanism (the $k = 0$ volume slot of the master Mellin engine,
carrying the factor $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4 = \pi$ of Paper~25's
$S^3 \to S^2$ bundle;\ Paper~38's $4/\pi$ is \emph{twice} the
unit-radius sphere-volume quotient $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/\mathrm{Vol}(S^3) =
2/\pi$ and not a base-to-total ratio)
produces period values in the localised pure-Tate sub-ring
$\mathbb{Q}[\pi, \pi^{-1}]$ of mixed Tate periods over $\mathbb{Q}$,
at depth $0$ and Tate weight $\in \mathbb{Z}$.  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} Every closed form
placed in the M1 slot across the corpus (the Fej\'er-constant entries are placed
there as a reading, not a derivation;\ Paper~38) (Paper 25 unit-sphere volume slot
$\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4 = \pi$; Paper 38 §L2 Fej\'er-moment constant
$4/\pi = \mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/\pi^2$, a kernel constant and not the convergence
rate; Paper 40~\cite{loutey_paper40} Thm 1 universal Fej\'er-moment constant
$4/\pi$ across compact connected simple Lie groups (derived at
rank~1, with an analytical universality mechanism at proof-sketch tier
and numerical support at rank~2 plus a noisier rank-3 datapoint; a
circle factor carries $2/\pi$);\ and the M1 factor $\pi^{-2}$ of the $\zeta(3)$ term $-3\zeta(3)/(16\pi^2)$ of the free-scalar F-value in Paper~50~\cite{loutey_paper50} §3,
an M1$\times$M3 cross-product whose $\zeta(3)$ is M3 content --- a continuum Klebanov--Pufu--Safdi value re-derived
there, not a finite-cutoff output) is a $\mathbb{Q}$-linear combination
of $\pi^n$ for $n \in \mathbb{Z}$, with $|n| \le 2$ on the empirical
witnesses logged so far.  Higher integer powers of $\pi$ enter only
through M2 (Seeley--DeWitt) or M3 (vertex-parity sums).  The M1 ring
does not engage M2's $\sqrt\pi$ vs $\pi^2$ volume-normalisation choice,
because the volume slot is itself a volume integral and does not engage
the heat-kernel asymptotic;\ it is \emph{not} free of the
Killing-form normalisation, however:\ under Kac's basic form the
Paper~38 constant reads $2\sqrt2/\pi$
(discussion of Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}; Paper~38), which lies in
$\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt2)[\pi, \pi^{-1}]$ rather than
$\mathbb{Q}[\pi, \pi^{-1}]$, so the ring statement holds in the
corpus's dual-Coxeter convention.
Sprint M1 Pure-Tate (June~2026, memo
\texttt{debug/sprint\_m1\_pure\_tate\_memo.md}).
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[M2 sub-mechanism inherits Fathizadeh--Marcolli
mixed-Tate classification, with pure-Tate refinement]
\label{cor:m2_mixed_tate}
At the standard volume-normalized Connes--Chamseddine
convention, the M2 sub-mechanism Seeley--DeWitt coefficients on
$\sthree$ are mixed-Tate periods over $\mathbb{Q}$ by direct
inheritance from
Fathizadeh--Marcolli~\cite{fathizadeh_marcolli2016} (the static-R--W
sub-case $a(t) \equiv 1$ on $\mathbb{R} \times \sthree$). \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]} The GeoVac
$\sthree$ M2 sector is in fact the strictly smaller pure-Tate
sub-ring
$\bigoplus_k \pi^{2k}\cdot\mathbb{Q}$
of even-weight Tate periods, with no $\zeta(3), \zeta(5)$, or MZV
content appearing at the SD level. This refines F--M's continuum
classification in two structural ways:\ (i) the Dirac SD expansion
is two-term exact ($a_k^{D^2} = 0$ for $k \ge 2$,
Paper~51~\cite{paper51} Cor.~2.1), not generically infinite; (ii)
the scalar Laplacian SD coefficients have the closed-form
geometric-series collapse $a_k^\Delta = 2\pi^2/k!$. The M3
sub-mechanism (vertex-parity Hurwitz, Catalan $G$, $\beta(s)$)
lives in cyclotomic mixed Tate at level $4$ (mixed Tate over
$\mathbb{Z}[i]$, not over $\mathbb{Q}$) and does \emph{not}
contaminate the SD coefficients, by the operator-order partition
of Paper~18~\cite{paper18} \S~III.7 ($k=2$ for SD vs $k=1$ for
vertex parity). Sprint Mixed-Tate Test (June~2026, memo
\texttt{debug/sprint\_mixed\_tate\_test\_memo.md}).
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[M3 sub-mechanism inherits Deligne--Glanois
cyclotomic mixed-Tate classification at level $\le 4$]
\label{cor:m3_cyclotomic_mixed_tate}
The M3 sub-mechanism (vertex-parity Hurwitz / Dirichlet $L$ /
depth-$k$ loop tower) on the discrete Camporesi--Higuchi $\sthree$
spectral triple lies in the cyclotomic mixed-Tate period ring at
level $\le 4$ over $\mathcal{O}_4[1/4] = \mathbb{Z}[i, 1/2]$ at depth
at most the loop order, by direct application of
Deligne~\cite{deligne2010} (proven for $N \in \{2,3,4,6,8\}$) and the
explicit basis of Glanois~\cite{glanois2015}.  Three sub-rings are
realised:
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z})
\;\subset\;\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z}[1/2])
\;\subset\;\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z}[i, 1/2]),
\end{equation*}
selected by (i) un-restricted vs vertex-restricted summation, and
(ii) loop order bounding the motivic depth.  Specifically:
\begin{itemize}
\item Un-restricted Dirac Dirichlet $D(s)$ at integer $s \ge 2$
      (Paper~28~\cite{paper28} Thm~2) lies in
      $\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z})$ at depth $1$;
\item Vertex-restricted $D_{\mathrm{even}} - D_{\mathrm{odd}} =
      2^{s-1}(\beta(s) - \beta(s-2))$ at integer $s \ge 2$
      (Paper~28~\cite{paper28} Thm~3) lies in
      $\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z}[i, 1/2])$ at level $4$, depth $1$, via
      Hurwitz at quarter-integer shifts;\ Catalan $G = \beta(2)$ and
      $\beta(4)$ are level-$4$ Dirichlet $L$-value periods in this
      ring;
\item The two-loop $S_{\min}$ (Paper~28 §$S_{\min}$;\ resolved in
      closed form in $\mathbb{Q}[\pi^2, \ln 2, \zeta(3), \zeta(5)]$) lies in
      $\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z}[1/2])$;\ the depth-$k$ tower (Paper~28
      Prop.~depth\_k) lies in $\mathcal{MT}$ at level $\le 4$ at depth at
      most the loop order (realized depths can drop, Paper~55).
\end{itemize}
The vertex-parity-as-$\chi_{-4}$ Dirichlet character mechanism is read as
an instance of the Deligne--Glanois Galois descent from level $4$ to level $2$
(the $N' = 2 \mid N = 4$ instance of Glanois~\cite{glanois2015} Cor.~1.2) realised on the framework's
natural QED observables.  Combined with
Corollary~\ref{cor:m2_mixed_tate}, the master Mellin engine of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion} now has a
period-theoretic stratification over the cases it treats (the theorem is
\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} and scoped to the first fifteen projections):\ M1 in $\mathbb{Q}[\pi, 1/\pi]$, M2
(Seeley--DeWitt coefficients and integer-$s$ values) in the pure-Tate
sub-ring $\bigoplus_k \pi^{2k}\cdot\mathbb{Q}$, and
M3 in $\mathcal{MT}(\mathbb{Z}[i, 1/2])$ at level $\le 4$.  Specific
motivic identification of constants at depth $\ge 3$ within the
Deligne--Glanois basis is an open question at each depth ($S_{\min}$ is
resolved in closed form, above).  Sprint M3 Cyclotomic Mixed-Tate
(June~2026, memo
\texttt{debug/sprint\_m3\_cyclotomic\_mixed\_tate\_memo.md}).
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Joint engagement]
M1, M2, M3 are not set-theoretically disjoint as constructions:\ the
spectral action invokes both M1 (the $k = 0$ volume slot on the spinor
bundle) and M2 (Seeley--DeWitt coefficients) simultaneously.  The theorem requires
only that \emph{at least one} of the three be engaged; multiple
engagement is the typical case at higher loop order.  The mechanisms
are role-disjoint in the Paper~18~\S\,III.7 Mellin-engine sense
(M1~$=$ packing-tier $\omega_d$, M2~$=$ heat-kernel via Mellin/Gaussian,
M3~$=$ vertex-parity quarter-integer Hurwitz character mechanism).
The Track~TS-E3 sprint (May~2026) reads M1, M2, M3 as sub-cases of
a single master Mellin-engine mechanism $\mathrm{Tr}(D^k \cdot e^{-tD^2})$ on a compact Riemannian
manifold, with $k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ selecting the sub-mechanism.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Status of $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$]
\label{rem:K_under_theorem}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Paper~2's combination $K = \pi(B+F-\Delta) \approx 1/\alpha$ matches
its target at $4.77\times10^{-7}$ (raw;\ $8.8 \times 10^{-8}$ through the cubic's root after the $\alpha^2$ correction).  Under
Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}, the chains of $K$'s
ingredients (Fock~$\circ$~Hopf$\circ$~spinor) engage M1 (the explicit prefactor $\pi$,
identified by Sprint~A as $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/4$~\cite{paper2}) and M2
(in $F = \pi^2/6 = D_{n^2}(d_{\max})$ via the Mellin transform of
the scalar Fock-degeneracy).  The $\pi$-content is therefore consistent
with case-exhaustion.  The remaining anomaly is at the
\emph{depth-prediction} layer of Paper~34~\S\,VIII.3 (depth predicts
$\sim 3\%$ residual at three projections; $K$ matches at
$4.8\times10^{-7}$, the cubic's root at $8.8\times10^{-8}$): a quantitative-error puzzle, distinct from the
qualitative case-exhaustion of the present theorem, and
unaffected by it.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Falsification target]
\textbf{[OPEN]} A counter-example to Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}
would be a finite chain of the first fifteen projections introducing
$\pi$ via a fourth mechanism not in \{M1, M2, M3\};\ a sixteenth
projection doing so would fall outside the theorem's scope and would
instead block its extension.  Three natural candidates were investigated in
Track~TS-E3 (May~2026) and each REDUCED to combinations of the
three existing mechanisms:\ continuum-gravity coupling (M1+M2),
anomaly-class projections (M1+M2+M3 via the eta-invariant's
first-order Mellin), and non-trivial principal-bundle projections
(M1 normalisations against integer integrands).  The strongest
remaining computational falsification target is the discrete
first-Chern-class invariant on the Hopf $S^3$ graph at $n_{\max}=3$,
expected $\pi$-free by the case-exhaustion reading; if a $\pi$ appears
unexpectedly, a fourth mechanism M4 would need to be named.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mechanism-as-domain reading (Sprint MR-A/B/C, May~2026)]
\label{rem:master_mellin_domain}
A three-track follow-up sprint (MR-A: Dirac state-space GH rate;
MR-B: spectral-action modular residual; MR-C: L2 next-order
constant) tested whether the master Mellin engine of
Paper~18~\S~III.7 is also read (a reading, not a derivation) as
organizing convergence rates, not only tagging $\pi$-sources. The
combined verdict is a mechanism-as-domain \emph{reading} alongside the
case-exhaustion theorem.
\begin{itemize}
\item M1 ($k = 0$) is the slot of volume-slot normalizations;\ the
first-moment constant of the SU(2) scalar central Fej{\'e}r kernel,
$4/\pi$ (dual-Coxeter normalisation; $2/\pi$ on the unit $S^3$), shares
its value (a reading, not a derivation, Paper~38).  It is a kernel
constant, not the state-space GH rate:\ Paper~38 measures $O(1/n_{\max})$
convergence with a tapered amplitude.  The constant is the sphere-volume
quotient $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/\mathrm{Vol}(S^3) = 2/\pi$ for unit-radius
spheres, twice that in the dual-Coxeter normalisation;\ it is not the Hopf
fibration's base-to-total ratio, whose base carries half the radius
(Paper~38, volume-ratio note).
\item M2 ($k = 2$) is the natural domain of heat-kernel and
spectral-action convergence. Sprint MR-B derived a closed form for
the Camporesi--Higuchi modular residual:
$$
\varepsilon(t) \;=\; \sum_{m \ge 1} (-1)^m \sqrt{\pi}\,
e^{-m^2 \pi^2 / t}\left[t^{-3/2} - 2m^2 \pi^2\, t^{-5/2}
   - \tfrac{1}{2}\, t^{-1/2}\right].
$$
Verified at $> 100$ digits at every tested $t$; every coefficient is
$\sqrt{\pi}$ times an element of $\bigoplus_k \pi^{2k}\,\mathbb{Q}$; modular exponent
exactly $\pi^2$ via Jacobi $\vartheta_2$.
\item M3 ($k = 1$) is the natural domain of vertex-restricted
parity-character observables (Paper~28~\S\,QED-vertex:
Catalan~$G$, $\beta(4)$). Sprint MR-A demonstrated that asking
for M3 from a half-integer-only Peter--Weyl truncation (the
state-space GH machinery of Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}) is a
\emph{category error}: the half-integer-spin truncation cannot span
the constant function $\chi_0$ on $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, so the kernel
does not localize and the moment is identically $\gamma_n^{\rm Dirac}
= \pi$ for every $n_{\max} \ge 1$. The scalar Fej\'er moment's
$(4/\pi)\log(n)/n$ decay (dual-Coxeter normalisation; $2/\pi$ on the unit $S^3$) is an integer$\times$half-integer
interference effect; removing the integer sector kills it. This is
consistent with the M1 reading when the sub-bundle lacks the constant
function.
\end{itemize}
The reading is therefore: the index $k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ is read as
indexing \emph{both} the sub-mechanism and the natural domain of observable
from which its transcendental signature can be extracted. The
case-exhaustion theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}
(\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]}, first fifteen projections) tags \emph{which}
transcendentals appear in the cases it covers; the domain partition
refines to \emph{which observables} can produce them. Sprint MR-C
extracted the L2 next-order constant $c = 4.10932146748987562042\ldots$
to $22$ verified decimal digits but
found no PSLQ identification in $M_1 \cup M_2 \cup M_3 \cup \{\gamma_E, \log 2,
G, \zeta(3)\}$ at coefficient ceiling $10^4$. This is consistent
with $c$ being a downstream Stein--Weiss IBP derivative of the
M1-read Fej\'er constant rather than a direct Mellin output, and is a partial
negative against extending the engine's classification to all
asymptotic-expansion coefficients along subsequent analytical
pipelines. Within each domain the engine organizes the observed
values (for $k = 0$ the domain is the Fej\'er kernel's moment, read
as M1, not the GH convergence rate;\ Paper~38).
Source: \texttt{debug/mr\_a\_dirac\_propinquity\_*},
\texttt{debug/mr\_b\_spectral\_action\_rate\_*},
\texttt{debug/mr\_c\_l2\_subleading\_*}.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{The Q$5'$ cochain-level record.}  The Stage-1/Stage-2 Q$5'$
sprint remarks (JLO and Connes--Moscovici cochains, substrate
candidates, bridge identities, path counts and the pro-system and
Tannakian follow-ons) are kept verbatim in
\texttt{docs/chronicles/paper\_32\_chronicle.tex}.  Their degree-$\ge 1$
content was computed on the hopping operator $D = \Lambda + \kappa
A_{\mathrm{graph}}$, not on $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$, on which every degree-$\ge 1$
JLO cochain vanishes;\ their degree-$0$ content, the continuum
Mellin-residue identification of $M_1$, $M_2$, $M_3$, is owned by
Paper~18~\S III.7, and the Tannakian programme by Paper~56.

\begin{remark}[Rational-residue extension of the case-exhaustion theorem:
the Bernoulli ladder and the functional-equation reading of the two-layer
split (Sprint GB, 2026-05-29)]
\label{rem:bernoulli_ladder}
The case-exhaustion theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion} classifies
(conditionally, for chains of the first fifteen Paper~34 projections)
the $\pi$-content as
$\mathcal{M}[\mathrm{Tr}(D^k\, e^{-tD^2})]$. The companion question is
where the framework's \emph{rational} coefficients come from. A
diagnostic on the gravity sector's small rationals places them on a single
Bernoulli ladder, with each rung $B_{2n}$ glued across two windows by the
Riemann functional equation:
$$
\zeta(2n) = (-1)^{n+1}\frac{B_{2n}(2\pi)^{2n}}{2(2n)!}
   \in \pi^{2n}\,\mathbb{Q}
\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad
\zeta(1-2n) = -\frac{B_{2n}}{2n} \in \mathbb{Q}.
$$
The left (positive-even) window is the M2 transcendental ring of
Layer~2 observables; the right (negative-integer) window is the
$\pi$-free rational residue carried by Layer~1 skeleton coefficients.
Verified placements (all exact in \texttt{sympy}):
\begin{itemize}
\item Conical-defect tip coefficient $\tfrac{1}{12} = -\zeta(-1) =
B_2/2$ (\S4.15); per-$t$ UV target $\tfrac{1}{24} = -\zeta(-1)/2 =
B_2/4$ (the string $c/24$); scalar $S^3$ Casimir $\tfrac{1}{240} =
+\zeta(-3)/2$ ($B_4$ rung); the $\alpha$-observation's
$F = \pi^2/6 = \pi^2 B_2 = \zeta(2)$ (Paper~2; the M2 window of the
same $B_2$ whose skeleton window is the conical tip).
\item \emph{Non-trivial internal check}: the replica-entropy
derivative is forced, not free ---
$\frac{d}{d\alpha}\big[\tfrac{1}{12}(\tfrac{1}{\alpha}-\alpha)\big]
\big|_{\alpha=1} = -\tfrac{1}{6} = -B_2 = 2\zeta(-1)$, the
$\alpha$-derivative of the tip's $B_2/2$.
\item \emph{Controls behave}: $\kappa = -1/16$ (numerically
coincident with $1/\Omega^4(0) = 1/16$, the fourth power of the inverse Fock conformal factor; an Observation,
Paper~0~\cite{paper0}) has no clean $\zeta$ form; the Dirac Casimir
$17/480$ misses the scalar $B_4$ rung by exactly $1/32$ and requires
the half-integer Hurwitz shift $\zeta(-3,\tfrac{1}{2})$, i.e.\ the
scalar/spinor distinction is the integer/half-integer Hurwitz split
(the half-integer shift is M2's spinor sub-mechanism).
\end{itemize}
The reading is the rational-residue extension of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}: the rational coefficients
are the negative-integer analytic continuation of the \emph{same}
Mellin object whose positive-even values are the Layer-2
transcendentals, and the empirical two-layer split (Paper~34/35) is the
Riemann functional-equation reflection $\zeta(s)\leftrightarrow
\zeta(1-s)$ instantiated per Bernoulli order.

\emph{Honest scope.} This is a structural correspondence at the same
epistemic level as Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}, not a
forcing proof; it concerns $\zeta$ at \emph{integer} arguments
(special values), not the non-trivial zeros, so it makes no contact
with the critical-line structure (cf.\ WH6 and the three classical-RH
walls RH-N/O/M, which remain in force). The $B_2$, $B_4$, \emph{and}
$B_6$ rungs are now populated (Sprint GB-3): the $B_6$ rung's
falsifiable target was met by the conformal-scalar Casimir on $S^5$
(a GeoVac manifold via the Bargmann--Segal lattice, Paper~24), which
evaluates to $-31/60480 = \tfrac{1}{24}(\zeta(-5) - \zeta(-3))$ --- the
$B_6$ rung $\zeta(-5) = -1/252$ enters with clean coefficient
$+\tfrac{1}{24}$, alongside $B_4$ at $-\tfrac{1}{24}$, with $B_2$
\emph{absent}. The $\zeta(-5) - \zeta(-3)$ spreading (rather than a
pure $\zeta(-5)$) is the \emph{same} quadratic-multiplicity mechanism
that obstructs the functional equation in Paper~28's
functional-equation obstruction remark (the $S^5$ harmonic degeneracy is quartic),
now seen on the Casimir side from an independent observable; the method
reproduces the $S^3$ control $1/240 = \tfrac{1}{2}\zeta(-3)$ (Paper~35).
The integer/half-integer Hurwitz split recurs: the conformal scalar
gives the clean ladder value, the half-integer HO/Bargmann $S^5$
Casimir gives $-17/3840$ (the ``$17$'' echoing the Dirac-$S^3$ $17/480$).
Next rung: $S^7$ conformal Casimir is predicted to spread across
$\zeta(-7), \zeta(-5), \zeta(-3)$. The ladder is thus supported across
three rungs and two observable types, but remains a structural
correspondence, not a forcing theorem.
Source: \texttt{debug/sprint\_gb\_bernoulli\_ladder\_memo.md},
\texttt{debug/sprint\_gb3\_b6\_rung\_memo.md}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Bisognano--Wichmann reading of the M1 sub-mechanism on
temporal compactifications (Track D, May 2026)]
\label{rem:bisognano_wichmann_reading}
The M1 sub-mechanism of the master Mellin engine,
$\mathcal{M}[\mathrm{Tr}(D^0\, e^{-tD^2})]$, produces a
$\mathrm{Vol}/\mathrm{Mellin\text{-}measure}$ factor on the spatial
$\sthree$ ($4/\pi = 2\,\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/\mathrm{Vol}(\sthree)$ at unit radius,
numerically $\mathrm{Vol}(S^2)/\pi^2$, the L2 Fej\'er-moment constant in
the dual-Coxeter normalisation, read as this factor (a reading, not a
derivation;\ Paper~38) --- \emph{not} a quantity on the Hopf base,
whose radius is half the total space's;\ Remark~\ref{rem:master_mellin_domain})
and on a temporal $S^1_\beta$ factor (the bare circumference $\beta$
itself; Sprint TD Track 1, $T_{S^3}\otimes T_{S^1_\beta}$).  Sprint TD
Track 4 (\texttt{debug/sprint\_td\_track4\_memo.md}, May~2026) extends
the temporal-side instantiation to the Euclidean Schwarzschild cigar,
where smoothness at $r=2M$ forces the $\tau$-circle period
$\beta_{\mathrm{cigar}} = 8\pi M$, consistent with the Hawking
temperature $T_H = 1/(8\pi M)$ (sympy residual zero):\ the M1 volume
slot re-expresses the continuum value, it does not derive it---$M$ is
external (it comes from the Einstein equations) and $\beta$ is posited
at finite cutoff.  Track~D
(\texttt{debug/bisognano\_wichmann\_track\_d\_memo.md}, May~2026) makes
explicit the dual physical reading of M1 on temporal compactifications:
under the standard Wick-rotation chain
(Hartle--Hawking~\cite{hartle_hawking1976};
 Sewell~\cite{sewell1982};
 Bisognano--Wichmann~\cite{bisognano_wichmann1975,bisognano_wichmann1976};
 reviewed in~\cite{witten2018_rmp}),
the framework's M1 $2\pi$ on $S^1_\tau$ matches the period of the
modular-flow / Lorentz-boost orbit of a stationary observer outside
the Schwarzschild horizon.  Equivalently, the M1 sub-mechanism on
temporal compactifications corresponds, by analogy, to the Wick-rotated
Bisognano--Wichmann modular-flow mechanism;\ identifying the framework's
generator with a boost is open (Paper~42's O0).  This is a
\emph{structural correspondence} (an analogy), not a literal identification: two
mathematical objects (a volume factor on the Riemannian
side; a modular-automorphism period on the Lorentzian side) are
matched by the published Wick-rotation prescription
(Bisognano--Wichmann~1976; Sewell~1982; Hartle--Hawking~1976), not by
an operator-level proof inside the truncated spectral triple
$\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}}$.  The natural follow-up that would turn the
correspondence into an operator-level identification --- compute the modular
Hamiltonian $K$ on $\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}}$ for a wedge-restricted
Camporesi--Higuchi state and verify $\sigma_{i\cdot 2\pi} = $ identity
at finite $n_{\max}$, then take the GH limit using the lemmas of
Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} --- is named as the principal falsifier
in Track~D \S 3.1; estimated beyond sprint scale; priority MEDIUM-HIGH.
The case-exhaustion theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion} itself
is unchanged: M1's $2\pi$ carries \emph{two readings of one number}
(the $k = 0$ volume slot, the framework's own reading;\ and the
boost-orbit period via Bisognano--Wichmann, an analogy under Wick
rotation), not two independent constraints on the
master Mellin engine partition.
The corollary on Rindler wedges (the Unruh temperature
$T_U = a/(2\pi)$, with $\beta_{\mathrm{Rindler}} = 2\pi/a$ posited and
re-expressed through the same M1 volume slot --- a consistency check,
not a derivation) is carried out in the Unruh paragraph below.

\textbf{Unruh pendant (May 2026).}\ The Track~D~\S 5.4 follow-up was
executed as a pendant
sprint~(\texttt{debug/unruh\_pendant\_memo.md}, May 2026).  Wick-rotating
Rindler coordinates $(\eta, \rho)$ for an observer at proper acceleration
$a$ produces Euclidean polar $(\eta_E, \rho)$ with conical regularity at
$\rho = 0$ forcing $\eta_E$-period $2\pi$, hence proper-time period
$\beta_U = 2\pi / a$ and Unruh $T_U = a/(2\pi)$
(Unruh~\cite{unruh1976}).  Sprint~TD Track~1's
\verb|matsubara_spectrum()| applied verbatim with $\beta = 2\pi/a$
reproduces the Unruh-thermal Matsubara spectrum bit-identically (sympy
residual zero on $T_U = 1/\beta_U$, lowest bosonic mode $= a$, lowest
fermionic mode $= a/2 = \pi T_U$).  The single $2\pi$ in $\beta_U$ is
the same M1 ($k = 0$ volume slot) / $\mathrm{Vol}(S^1)$ signature that drove
the cigar correspondence, instantiated on the Rindler $\eta_E$-circle
instead of the cigar $\tau$-circle.  The reading is identical:\
under the Wick-rotation chain, the framework's M1 $2\pi$ on
$S^1_{\eta_E}$ matches the period of the modular-flow / boost-orbit of the
accelerated observer (an analogy;\ identifying the framework's generator
with a boost is open, Paper~42's O0).  Hawking, Bisognano--Wichmann, and Unruh are
three faces of one continuum Wick-rotation theorem, whose shared $2\pi$
the framework's M1 ($k = 0$) volume slot matches by analogy (it
re-expresses the continuum value and does not derive it);
the published Wick-rotation prescription supplies the bridge to the
Lorentzian-side modular-flow vocabulary in each case.  The
\emph{operator-level falsifier} (modular Hamiltonian on
$\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}}$ for a wedge-restricted state has analytic
period $2\pi$) named in Track~D~\S 3.1 covers all three faces
simultaneously, since the bridge runs through the same KMS-Tomita-Takesaki
algebra in every case; lifting the period-closure check on any one face
lifts the structural correspondence on Hawking, Bisognano--Wichmann, and
Unruh together.  Same scope (structural correspondence, not an operator-level
identification); same falsifier; same MEDIUM-HIGH priority and scale.

\textbf{The finite-cutoff period closure (Sprints L1, L1-tighten).}\
\textbf{[SYMBOLIC (trivial) + MEASURED]} Taking the rotation generator
$K = 2J_z$ as modular generator on $\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}}$ gives
$\sigma_{2\pi} = \mathrm{id}$ exactly, but for a reason that carries no
Bisognano--Wichmann content:\ $K$ has integer spectrum, so any operator
is returned after period $2\pi$, and any other integer-spectrum generator
passes the same check
(\texttt{tests/test\_modular\_period\_closure\_automatic.py}, which also
shows a non-integer-spectrum generator fails it).  $K$ anticommutes with
the reflection $R_{\mathrm{polar}}$ that defines the wedge projector, so
$P_W K P_W = 0$, and the wedge operator is a separately defined diagonal
operator, not a restriction of $K$
(\texttt{tests/test\_p32\_polar\_generator\_wedge.py}).  The
Tomita--Takesaki cross-check of Sprint~L1-tighten is circular, since its
state is built as $e^{-K}/Z$.  The falsifier named above --- \emph{derive}
$K$ as the modular Hamiltonian of a wedge-restricted Camporesi--Higuchi
state, rather than choose it, and check its period --- remains
\textbf{OPEN}.  The sprint record is kept verbatim in
\texttt{docs/chronicles/paper\_32\_chronicle.tex}.

\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Gromov--Hausdorff convergence on $S^3$]
\label{thm:gh_convergence}
Let $\mathcal{T}_{S^3} = (C^\infty(S^3), L^2(S^3, \Sigma), D_{\mathrm{CH}})$
be the round-$S^3$ metric spectral triple with the Camporesi--Higuchi
Dirac, and let $\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}} = (\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}},
\mathcal{H}_{n_{\max}}, D_{n_{\max}})$ be the Connes--van Suijlekom
truncation at cutoff $n_{\max}$ on the \emph{true} Camporesi--Higuchi
window:\ $\mathcal{H}_{n_{\max}}$ is the span of the complete Dirac
eigenspaces with $|\lambda| \le n_{\max} + \tfrac12$,
$\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{CH}}_{n_{\max}} = P_{n_{\max}} C^\infty(S^3) P_{n_{\max}}$
($\dim = 5, 30, 91$ at $n_{\max} = 1, 2, 3$), and the state spaces are
metrized by the left-translation Lipschitz seminorm (Paper~38;\ this is
\emph{not} the Fock-shell operator system of sprint~R2.1, a model object,
Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}).  Then the truncated triples converge to $\mathcal{T}_{S^3}$
in van~Suijlekom's state-space Gromov--Hausdorff distance (the
strengthening to the Latr{\'e}moli{\`e}re quantum propinquity remains an
open named gap; see Remark~\ref{rem:gh_status_caveat}):
$$
   d_{\mathrm{GH}}(\mathcal{T}_{n_{\max}}, \mathcal{T}_{S^3})
   \;\le\; \gamma_{n_{\max}}
   \;\xrightarrow[n_{\max}\to\infty]{}\; 0,
$$
where
$\gamma_{n_{\max}}$ is the L2 mass-concentration moment of the central
spectral Fej{\'e}r kernel on SU(2).  Its asymptotics
$\gamma_{n_{\max}} = (4/\pi + o(1))\log n_{\max}/n_{\max}$ (its $O(1/n_{\max})$ remainder is the measured part) (dual-Coxeter normalisation, $\mathrm{Cas}(\mathrm{ad}) = h^\vee$;\ equivalently $2/\pi$ on the unit $S^3$ this paper declares, and $2\sqrt2/\pi$ under the field-standard Kac form --- the constant scales with the metric)
is \textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM + MEASURED]} in
Paper~38~\cite{loutey_paper38};\ the constant is the Fej\'er kernel's,
and with a tapered amplitude Paper~38 measures $d_{\mathrm{GH}} =
O(1/n_{\max})$ \textbf{[MEASURED]}.  What is proved
unconditionally is the bound $d_{\mathrm{GH}} \le \gamma_{n_{\max}}$ and
$\gamma_{n_{\max}} \to 0$, while the uniform bound $\gamma_{n_{\max}} \le
6\log n_{\max}/n_{\max}$ ($n_{\max} \ge 2$) is \textbf{[MEASURED]} (a finite-range check, not proved for all $n_{\max}$);
the small-$n$ closed forms are consistent with it.  Convergence in the
Dirac-commutator seminorm, and of the almost-commutative
(gauge-network, $\mathcal{A} \otimes M_n$) structure, is
\textbf{[OPEN]}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The theorem is established by Paper~38's~\cite{loutey_paper38}
lifted-state unconditional route in the van~Suijlekom state-space
Gromov--Hausdorff distance (\emph{not} a Latr{\'e}moli{\`e}re
propinquity --- that strengthening is the open named gap of
Remark~\ref{rem:gh_status_caveat}). The five-lemma UCP-pair
assembly of WH1 sprint~R2.5, whose lemma~L5 fails
($B_{n_{\max}}$ is finite-band, so
$\mathrm{height}_B \equiv 1$ at every cutoff;\ Paper~38), and the module
panel that measured it (\texttt{geovac/gh\_convergence.py}) are kept
verbatim in \texttt{docs/chronicles/paper\_32\_chronicle.tex}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Status and named gaps]
\label{rem:gh_status_caveat}
One named gap stands for Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence}, item~(iii)
below;\ items (i), (ii) and (iv) concern the relocated UCP-pair assembly
(its lemma L5 fails) and do not affect the theorem.
(i)~The L2 cb-norm statement conflated the kernel's Plancherel
\emph{mass distribution} with its convolution-\emph{multiplier symbol}:
the smoothing map is unital completely positive with cb-norm exactly
$1$, and the quantity $2/(n_{\max}+1)$ is the mass-distribution maximum,
not a cb-norm; the moment $\gamma_{n_{\max}}$, its closed forms, and
its $4/\pi$ asymptote are unaffected.  (ii)~The $\mathrm{reach}_P$ dual estimate in L5 is a
\emph{named gap}: a forward cb-norm controls no partial inverse; the
correct mechanism is an antiderivative-transference lemma in the style
of Leimbach--van~Suijlekom~\cite{leimbach_vs2024};
for scalar sectors on compact metric groups the conclusion is covered by
Gaudillot-Estrada--van~Suijlekom~\cite{gaudillot_estrada_vs2025}.
(iii)~The distance proved is van~Suijlekom's state-space
Gromov--Hausdorff distance~\cite{vansuijlekom2021_fejer}; the
Latr{\'e}moli{\`e}re propinquity label is an open named gap --- the
Latr{\'e}moli{\`e}re framework~\cite{latremoliere2021dual} builds tunnels from quantum
isometries, not UCP pairs.
(iv)~The kernel condition $L(a) = 0 \iff a \in \mathbb{C}\mathbf{1}$
for the Dirac-commutator seminorm.  The counts $10/14$ ($n_{\max} = 2$)
and $26/55$ ($n_{\max} = 3$) for the chirality-diagonal CH Dirac, and
$1/14$, $1/55$ for the engineered offdiag Dirac, are counts on the
\emph{doubled Fock model} of Remark~\ref{rem:operator_system}, not on the
truncated Camporesi--Higuchi triple:\ on the true window the
Dirac-commutator kernel is exactly the scalars at $n_{\max} \le 3$
(\textbf{[MEASURED]}, Paper~38, remark on the Dirac-commutator seminorm;\
\texttt{tests/test\_p38\_true\_ch\_window.py}).  Item (ii) is bypassed
by the translation-seminorm reframing of the companion
$S^3$ paper, whose lifted-state route uses no dual reach;\ item (iii)
stands (the propinquity label remains a named gap).  The reframing was
adopted because its compression and lifted-state
