Connes order-zero (T5), order-one (T6), and second-order (T7)
conditions, bosonic spectral-action gauge-sector coefficient (T8),
Higgs vacuum manifold (T9), cross-rung Yukawa coupling (T10),
extensibility to rung $n \ge 4$ ($S^7$ is an H-space but not a Lie group;\ $S^{2n-1}$ for $n \ge 5$ is not even an H-space~\cite{adams1958}) (T11), and consistency
with the Bertrand $\times$ Hopf-tower reading of Door~4b (T12). \emph{All twelve} return either
SILENT (Upgrade B unconstrained on the target), COMPATIBLE (the
pre-existing constraint passes for $\mathbb{H}$, the mechanism
unaffected), IDENTICAL to CCM (same observable from either
$\mathbb{H}$ or $M_2(\mathbb{C})$ post-unimodularity), or a CLEAN
extensibility/strengthening rule (T11, T12). Zero sub-tests introduce
a new constraint; zero contradict any existing GeoVac result. Upgrade B
is therefore a \emph{clean reformulation} of the CCM $\mathbb{H}$-selection
with no new predictive content.

\textbf{Honest axiom-savings audit.} The naive comparison in the
preceding paragraph (CCM 3 axioms vs DAS 1 axiom) over-counts. Sub-test
T7 (Connes second-order condition) shows that the second-order
condition is \emph{still} required in the Upgrade-B formulation,
because it constrains the off-diagonal Yukawa structure of $D_F$
(which $\mathbb{H}$-selection alone does not determine).
Upgrade B therefore replaces \emph{two} of CCM's three
$\mathbb{H}$-selection axioms --- the complex chiral fermion
representation requirement and the $2N^2 = 32$ dimension count ---
with the single sphere-Lie-group axiom; the second-order condition
persists in both formulations. Net axiom savings: \emph{one}. The
Door~4 series is structurally complete at \textbf{partial-door (final)}.
The remaining open questions are paper-level (whether to adopt
Upgrade B's axiom-minimality advantage as the working construction
principle) and the deep wall (force $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ and the inner
KO-dim from a packing principle; no known handle; deferred). Source:
\texttt{debug/door4f\_falsifier\_A\_double\_prime\_memo.md}.

\paragraph{The deep wall is a relative non-emission statement, not a gap
  (Direction~2 scoping, 2026-06-03).} The ``deep wall'' named above --- forcing
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ and the inner KO-dimension from a packing principle ---
is sharper than an open gap:\ relative to the packing construction of
Paper~0 as presently formulated, the construction emits no such datum. One
established fact carries this, beside one Observation-level reading. The
reading first, the Door~4 seam:\ the inner
Yukawa/real-structure data lives in a ring (and a set of
$(\mathcal{H}_F, D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$ data) sharing no generator with
any forced outer ring (an Observation-level reading, \S\ref{sec:g4}). The fact, Paper~0~\S VII:\ the packing construction is
\emph{kinematic} --- it emits the label set $(n, \ell, m, s)$ and the
graph topology, and emits no operator, no real structure, no coupling,
and no Hilbert-space multiplicity. But $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ is exactly a
Hilbert-space multiplicity (the factor in $\mathcal{H}_F =
\mathbb{C}^{N_{\mathrm{gen}}} \otimes \mathcal{H}_F^{(1)}$, invisible to
every algebra- and automorphism-level mechanism), and the inner
KO-dimension is exactly a $(J_F, \gamma_F)$ datum --- both of the type
the packing construction does not produce. The packing axiom therefore
cannot constrain either:\ the residue is packing-inaccessible by the
kinematic fact alone (the seam reading is consistent with it). The single logically-open escape is a
\emph{packing-native multiplicity} --- a datum the construction emits on
the outer labels that would map to a factor-$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ tripling
of $\mathcal{H}_F$. It is empty:\ Paper~0's entire multiplicity inventory
is the $2\ell+1$ angular slots (odd, and \emph{growing} with $\ell$,
hence not a generation-universal multiplicity) together with a single
$\mathbb{Z}_2$ already consumed by spin; no constant factor of three
appears anywhere. This sharpens the structural-skeleton scope for the
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$/inner-KO-dimension residue into a \emph{relative
non-emission statement} \textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT]}:\ the current
packing construction emits no such multiplicity. It offers one
structural reading of why the H1, W3, and Koide selection attempts
failed:\ all probed inside the almost-commutative category, on the free
side of the seam (an Observation-level partition;\ the period rings on
its two sides share no generator beyond $\mathbb{Q}$, which records the
partition rather than forbidding selection), where the packing axiom has no reach. The qualifier ``relative'' is load-bearing:\ the statement
forecloses the residue for the \emph{current} packing construction, not
for every conceivable discrete one. Lifting it would require a
\emph{sibling axiom} that emits fiber multiplicities --- a new primitive
Paper~0 does not contain --- recorded as the open (long-range) direction
with no present handle. The inner \emph{algebra shape} $\mathbb{C}
\oplus \mathbb{H} \oplus M_3(\mathbb{C})$ is reached only relative to an
organizing reading:\ under the Bertrand $\times$ complex-Hopf-tower
reading plus the adopted Upgrade~B axiom (Door~4b/4d, via the Hopf tower
and Hurwitz's classification) the factor count and the $\mathbb{C}$ and
$M_3(\mathbb{C})$ summands are fixed, while $\mathbb{H}$ at $n = 2$ is
admitted, not forced;\ outside that reading the residue is precisely the
multiplicity- and real-structure-valued data. Source:\
\texttt{debug/seam\_packing\_scoping\_memo.md}.

\paragraph{The Forced-Count Theorem (synthesis, 2026-06-03).}
The eight preceding paragraphs (Sprint H1 through Door 4f and the
Direction 2 scoping) compose into a single Bertrand-shaped statement
of the structural content those sub-sprints established (Door~4 itself
is partial-door).  \textbf{[SYMBOLIC + MEASURED]} Stated as a theorem it makes explicit what the
distributed prose shows in pieces:\ given the admitted $\mathcal{A}_F$
(an organizing reading plus the adopted Upgrade~B axiom) and the imported
CCM representation, the standard axioms fix the \emph{dimension} of the
$D_F$ moduli space, but do not (and at the current packing primitive
cannot) fix the points within it.

\begin{theorem}[Forced $D_F$ moduli dimension]
\label{thm:forced_count}
Let $\mathcal{T} = (\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes \mathcal{A}_F,\;
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes \mathcal{H}_F,\;
D_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes 1 + \gamma_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes D_F,\; J,\; \gamma)$ be the
GeoVac almost-commutative spectral triple of
Sec.~\ref{sec:construction} (the graded form is displayed for
reference; the modules build the ungraded sum
$D_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes 1 + s_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes D_F$, \S\ref{sec:higgs_h1};
the count below concerns $D_F$ alone and is the same for both),
assuming:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The Bertrand-with-complex-Hopf-tower reading $n \le 3$
  (Door~4b; an organizing reading, not an argument of
  Sec.~\ref{sec:unified_gauge}), with the Upgrade B sphere-Lie-group
  axiom of Door~4e (an adopted axiom), selecting $\mathcal{A}_F = \mathbb{C} \oplus
  \mathbb{H} \oplus M_3(\mathbb{C})$.
\item The canonical Chamseddine--Connes--Marcolli SM representation of
  $\mathcal{A}_F$ on $\mathcal{H}_F$, with generation multiplicity
  $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ imported as an external input (Direction 2:\ not
  packing-derived).
\item The standard real-spectral-triple axioms:\ Hermiticity,
  KO-dim-6 chirality grading $\{\gamma_F, D_F\} = 0$,
  $J$-reality $[J_F, D_F] = 0$, and Connes order-one.
\end{enumerate}
Then the space of admissible finite Dirac operators $D_F$ has real
dimension forced by these inputs alone, with the full-axiom reduction
chain at $N_{\mathrm{gen}} = 1$ (on the full $32$-dimensional finite
geometry):
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:forced_count_chain}
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\, \mathcal{M}(D_F)
:\; 2048 \xrightarrow{\textnormal{Herm.}} 1024
     \xrightarrow{\{\gamma_F, D_F\}=0} 512
     \xrightarrow{[J_F, D_F]=0} 272
     \xrightarrow{\text{order-one}} 32 .
\end{equation}
\textbf{[SYMBOLIC + MEASURED]} The endpoint $32$ is computed with a linear
$*$-representation of $\mathcal{A}_F$ on $\mathbb{C}^{32}$ (particles
colour-blind, $M_3(\mathbb{C})$ acting on antiquarks; the CCM algebra
basis and ten random generic algebra elements give the same null space
through one null-space routine, stable across tolerances, singular-value
gap $12.4$ vs $10^{-13}$;
\texttt{tests/test\_trunk\_qa\_forced\_count\_moduli.py}).  Its projection
onto the $16$-dimensional matter sector has rank $16$ --- the \emph{full}
lepton $2\times2$ and quark-flavour $2\times2 \otimes \mathbb{1}_3$ blocks, of
which the four colour-diagonal Yukawas $y_\nu, y_e, y_u, y_d$ with their
$L \leftrightarrow R$ placements are $4$ complex, i.e.\ \emph{half},
and onto the matter--antimatter (Majorana) block rank $16$.
The physical-slice dimension under
restriction to diagonal Yukawa
$Y = \mathrm{diag}(y_\nu, y_e, y_u, y_d)$ is $8$ per generation.  The
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$-generation count with full generation-mixing Yukawa
matrices is not re-derived here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The full-axiom chain in eq.~\eqref{eq:forced_count_chain} is the
direct parameter-count of the constraint solution space
(\texttt{tests/test\_trunk\_qa\_forced\_count\_moduli.py}) on the full
$32$-dimensional finite geometry with
$N_{\mathrm{gen}} = 1$:\ Hermiticity halves the free parameters;
$\{\gamma_F, D_F\} = 0$ kills the chirality-diagonal blocks;
$J$-reality identifies the matter and antimatter sectors
($512 \to 272$); the order-one condition, imposed with a linear
$*$-representation of $\mathcal{A}_F$, removes $240$ further
parameters ($272 \to 32$): with $M_3(\mathbb{C})$ acting on the
antiquark colour index, $[[D_F, a], J b J^{-1}] = 0$ forces the quark
Yukawa blocks to be colour-diagonal and colour-independent, and strongly
constrains the matter$\to$antimatter block $P$ (it does \emph{not} annihilate
it --- the rank-$16$ real Majorana slice survives, as the next paragraph details).

\emph{What survives, measured.}  The support pattern of the solution
space is the theorem's actual content.  The matter block (rank $16$ real $=8$ complex) is
\emph{full} on the lepton $2\times2$ --- including $\nu_L \to e_R$ and
$e_L \to \nu_R$ --- and full on the quark flavour $2\times2 \otimes \mathbb{1}_3$,
including $u_L^c \leftrightarrow d_R^c$;\ the four named Yukawas are $4$
complex, i.e.\ \emph{half} of it.  The Majorana block (rank $16$ real) carries
$2$ complex $\nu_R$ entries and \emph{six} complex
$\text{lepton}_R \leftrightarrow \text{antiquark-}u_R$ entries --- so twelve of
its sixteen real moduli are lepton--quark operators.  Each constructed-triple
axiom-consistency check passes at
$n_{\max} \in \{2, 3\}$ in module
\texttt{geovac/standard\_model\_triple.py} (backed by
\texttt{tests/test\_standard\_model\_triple.py}, 47 tests);\ those
tests verify the axiom consistency of one constructed $D_F$, \emph{not}
the moduli-dimension counts of the chain, which are the direct
enumeration of \texttt{tests/test\_trunk\_qa\_forced\_count\_moduli.py}.  (No
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ scaling is claimed:\ as the statement says, the
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$-generation count is not re-derived.)
The status of the inputs:\ (1) rests on the Door~4b Hopf-tower reading
(which fixes the factor count) plus the Upgrade~B sphere-Lie-group
axiom of Door~4e, which selects $\mathbb{H}$ at $n = 2$ as an
\emph{adopted axiom}, not a derivation (the Door~4 record is
partial-door; Door~4c found $\mathbb{H}$ admitted but not forced); (3)
is the standard real-spectral-triple axiom set; (2) is the one external
input beyond the adopted axiom in (1), recorded by the Direction 2 paragraph as packing-unreachable in
the current Paper 0 construction.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Bertrand analogy]
\label{cor:bertrand_analogy}
Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count} is the inner-factor analogue of
Bertrand's theorem on closed-orbit central forces.  Bertrand forces
the \emph{form} of the central potential ($-k/r$ or $k r^2$) without
forcing the coupling $k$; Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count} forces the
\emph{dimension} of the $D_F$ moduli space without forcing the
$D_F$-entries themselves.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The two seams are
analogous:\ given its inputs, the construction fixes what the moduli space
\emph{is}, not where in it the realised system sits.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Paired with the Door 4 negative half]
\label{rem:forced_count_with_seam}
The positive content of Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count} sits beside two
negative companions already recorded above:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{Door 4 seam reading (Yukawa-value wall):}\ the
  $\eta$-trivialization plus tensor factorization plus the G3
  commuting-$\mathbb{Z}_2$ result place the $D_F$-entries inside
  $\mathcal{M}(D_F)$ in a Dirichlet ring sharing no generator with any
  forced outer ring, so the framework does not derive them from the
  forced rings (an Observation-level reading).  \textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]}
  Sprint Yukawa-PSLQ (2026-06-03) added empirical confirmation:\ a
  $162$-cell sweep of the nine measured Yukawa values against the
  inner-factor pure-Tate basis $\mathbb{Q}[\pi, \pi^{-1}]$ suggested by
  the M1/M2 slot restriction
  (basis sharpened by the $\eta$-trivialization audit ruling out
  $M_3$/Dirichlet $L$ on the inner factor) returned zero hits at
  $M \le 10^3$, at both MS-bar $M_Z$ and MS-bar
  $\mu = 2 \times 10^{16}$ GeV, across three transforms
  ($y_f,\, y_f^2,\, \log y_f$).  Source:\
  \texttt{debug/sprint\_yukawa\_pslq\_memo.md}.
\item \textbf{Direction 2 packing-reach (non-emission) statement:}\
  $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ and the inner KO-dimension are
  packing-unreachable in the current Paper 0 construction
  (preceding paragraph, source
  \texttt{debug/seam\_packing\_scoping\_memo.md}).
\end{itemize}
\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count} thus crystallises the \emph{full}
forced/free seam at the level of $D_F$:\ the forced data is the
moduli space and its dimension; the free data is (i)~the point in
the moduli space (Yukawa values, Door 4 wall), (ii)~the
multiplicity factor ($N_{\mathrm{gen}}$, Direction 2), and (iii)~the
real-structure signature (inner KO-dimension, Direction 2).  All three
free data are calibration inputs in the structural-skeleton-scope
sense (\S 1.5 of the project guidelines).
\end{remark}

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
% N_gen non-selection theorem (Sprint C3 closure, 2026-06-08).
% Upgrades the Direction 2 / Read 2 scoping verdicts to a formal
% structural theorem conditional on canonical CCM representation.
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ non-selection under canonical CCM representation]
\label{thm:n_gen_non_selection}
\textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT]} (Here and in the non-selection results
below, ``a morphism in $\mathcal{A}$'' is informal:\ $\mathcal{A}$ is a
list of axioms, and ``no morphism selects $X$'' means that the axioms
are satisfied for every admissible value of $X$.)
Let $\mathcal{A}$ denote the framework's current axiom set
$\{$Paper 0 packing axiom; standard real-spectral-triple axioms
(Hermiticity, $J$-reality, KO-dim chirality, order-one); Hopf-tower
truncation (Hurwitz); Bertrand's classical theorem; Upgrade B
(sphere-Lie-group axiom)$\}$, and fix the canonical Chamseddine--Connes
SM representation of $\mathcal{A}_F = \mathbb{C} \oplus \mathbb{H}
\oplus M_3(\mathbb{C})$ on the inner Hilbert space.

For every integer $N \ge 1$, let $\mathcal{T}_N$ denote the GeoVac
spectral triple with inner Hilbert-space multiplicity $N$ (i.e.,
$\dim \mathcal{H}_F = 32 N$ under the canonical rep).  Then
$\mathcal{T}_N$ satisfies every axiom in $\mathcal{A}$ that
$\mathcal{T}_1$ satisfies (on the GV factor order zero and order one
fail at finite cutoff, Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit}, independently of
$N$).  Consequently,
$\mathcal{A}$ contains no morphism that selects a specific value
of $N$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Each axiom in $\mathcal{A}$ is independent of $N$:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item The Paper 0 packing axiom outputs $(n, l, m, m_s)$ labels for a
  single $S^3$ outer graph;  $N$ is not in this output.
\item The standard real-spectral-triple axioms constrain the relations
  $(D, J, \gamma)$ on $\mathcal{H} = \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes
  \mathcal{H}_F$.  $\dim \mathcal{H}_F$ scales linearly with $N$, but
  Hermiticity, $J$-reality, KO-dim chirality, and order-one are
  structural relations independent of multiplicity.
\item Hopf-tower truncation (Hurwitz, Doors 4b/4d/4e) operates on
  $\mathcal{A}_F$;  the algebra $\mathcal{A}_F = \mathbb{C} \oplus
  \mathbb{H} \oplus M_3(\mathbb{C})$ is fixed independent of $N$.
\item Bertrand's classical theorem selects the outer manifold $S^3$;
  $N$-independent.
\item Upgrade B constrains the inner-algebra structure via the
  sphere-Lie-group identification of Door 4e;  $N$-independent.
\item The canonical CCM SM representation distributes $32$ fermion
  degrees of freedom per generation across the three algebra factors;
  the multiplicity $N$ enters as an external Hilbert-space multiplicity
  rather than as a constraint emergent from the rep structure.
\end{itemize}

The Read 2 scoping
(\texttt{debug/sprint\_read2\_n\_gen\_scoping\_memo.md}, 2026-06-03)
provides three independent obstructions to any candidate
Hopf-tower-to-representation shortcut that would correlate $N$ with
the algebra factor count under canonical representation:\ the SM
fermion DOF count per generation distributes across all three algebra
factors;  the Yukawa-Higgs cross-generation coupling structure
required for CKM/PMNS phenomenology prevents algebra-factor
reorganisation;  and the alternative ``3 factors $=$ 3 generations''
assignment gives $12$ DOF per gen versus the $32$ DOF of the SM.
Each obstruction independently closes the candidate shortcut.

Therefore $\mathcal{T}_N$ is a valid GeoVac spectral triple for every
$N \ge 1$ under canonical representation;  no axiom in $\mathcal{A}$
has been exhibited that selects a specific value. $\hfill\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope and the unconditional question]
\label{rem:n_gen_scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection} is conditional on the canonical
CCM SM representation choice.  The unconditional question --- whether
any SM-phenomenologically-consistent representation of $\mathcal{A}_F$
on $\mathcal{H}_F$ could correlate $N$ with the algebra factor count
under an alternative rep --- remains the open long-range structural-
research target named by the Read 2 sharpest falsifier:\ exhibit a
representation in which the $32$ fermion DOF per generation are
distributed across the three algebra factors in a way that makes
the three generations the three rep blocks of the algebra, and the
cross-generation Yukawa-Higgs coupling (CKM/PMNS) is naturally
encoded as the algebra cross-factor coupling.  Absent such a
construction, Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection} stands as the
conditional impossibility result corresponding to the Read 2
NO-GO scoping verdict.

The Theorem identifies $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ as Class 1 calibration data
(external input on which the framework's outer-factor mechanism is
structurally silent), formalising the Direction 2 packing-reach
and Read 2 representation-level NO-GO scopings into a single
structural statement.  It is the multiplicity-side analogue of the
H1 Yukawa non-selection theorem:\ both are non-selection results for
inner-factor content, both conditional on the canonical rep, and
together with the Forced-Count Theorem~(\ref{thm:forced_count}) they
characterise the full free-side content of the inner-factor
structural-skeleton boundary at the canonical-representation level.

\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} Paper~57 \S 6.1 (the Forced/Free Seam observations paper) records
this theorem as the C3 closure of the empirical Direction 2 / Read 2
NO-GO scopings, and as a proof-by-argument non-selection result
alongside H1 Yukawa non-selection (the Forced-Count Theorem, listed
with them there, is a dimension count, not a non-selection result).
\end{remark}

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
% Inner KO-dim non-selection theorem (Sprint F3 closure, 2026-06-08).
% Companion to thm:n_gen_non_selection; same Direction 2 NO-GO basis,
% but the structural argument is more direct: packing-is-kinematic per
% Paper 0 §VII.B, and KO-dim is real-structure data.
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[Inner KO-dim is external calibration data under canonical-rep convention]
\label{thm:ko_dim_non_selection}
\textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT]} (The combined triple's own sign data is
the measured $(-,+,+)$ of \S\ref{sec:higgs_h1}; no additive KO rule
applies to it, since for the graded product Dabrowski--Dossena's
Table~5 admits no consistent $\varepsilon'$ with a KO-6 finite factor.
The statement below concerns only the \emph{inner} factor's label.)
Let $\mathcal{A}$ denote the framework's current axiom set
(as defined in \emph{Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}}).
The Paper~0 packing axiom is kinematic
(Paper~0~\S VII.B ``What the construction does not provide''):  it
outputs the labels $(n, l, m, m_s)$ and the $S^3$ graph topology, but
neither a Dirac operator, nor a real structure, nor a chirality
grading.  The inner KO-dimensional signature is a property of the
real-structure data $(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$, none of which is in
the packing axiom's output.

The remaining axioms in $\mathcal{A}$ (standard real-spectral-triple
axioms, Hopf-tower truncation, Bertrand's classical theorem,
Upgrade~B) constrain the consistency of $(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$ with
the outer GeoVac triple's signs (KO-dimension~$3$ for the continuum factor; sign triple $(-,+,+)$ and no label at finite cutoff), but output
no $(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$, and so no inner KO-dim signature.  The
canonical Chamseddine--Connes SM representation (inner KO-dim~$6$) is
supplied as external input to the framework.  \textbf{[OPEN]} No
alternative inner datum with a different KO-dimension that satisfies
every axiom of $\mathcal{A}$ together with the outer signs is exhibited
here;\ the theorem asserts non-selection, not the existence of
alternatives.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The argument is a direct composition of two established facts:

\textbf{Fact 1 (Paper~0 \S VII.B, explicit scope statement).} The
packing construction is \emph{kinematic}:\ it produces labels and
graph topology, and explicitly does \emph{not} produce operators,
real structures, couplings, or fiber-Hilbert-space multiplicities.

\textbf{Fact 2 (Door~4f T1, Door~4b Q3).} The inner KO-dimensional
signature is defined as a property of the real-structure data
$(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$. The chirality-grading axiom
$\{\gamma_F, D_F\} = 0$ and the $J_F$-reality conditions
$J_F^2 = \pm \mathbf{1}$, $J_F D_F = \pm D_F J_F$, $J_F \gamma_F =
\pm \gamma_F J_F$ specify the residue classes mod~$8$ that are
consistent, but do not select one (alternatives satisfying every
axiom of $\mathcal{A}$ are not exhibited here) in the absence of
additional constraints (such as the phenomenological constraints
imposed by SM fermion doubling and Majorana neutrino-mass
structure).

Composition.  $\mathcal{A}$ produces no $(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$ data
autonomously (Fact 1), and the inner KO-dim is a function of that
data (Fact 2).  Therefore $\mathcal{A}$ does not autonomously select
an inner KO-dim signature.  The canonical CCM SM rep supplies a
specific $(D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$ triple (KO-dim~$6$) as external input.
$\hfill\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The full inner-factor structural-skeleton boundary]
\label{rem:full_inner_factor_boundary}
Theorems~\ref{thm:forced_count}, the H1 Yukawa non-selection
(Theorem~\ref{thm:h1_yukawa_non_selection}), \ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}, and
\ref{thm:ko_dim_non_selection} together characterise the full
free-side content of the inner-factor structural-skeleton boundary
at the canonical-representation level:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{Forced data:}  the moduli space $\mathcal{M}(D_F)$ and
  its dimension (full-axiom:\ $32$ at $N_{\mathrm{gen}} = 1$, matter-sector
  projection rank $16$, $8$ per generation on the diagonal-Yukawa slice;
  Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count}).
\item \textbf{Free data:}  (i) the point in $\mathcal{M}(D_F)$
  (Yukawa values --- H1 non-selection),
  (ii) the Hilbert-space multiplicity ($N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ ---
  Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}),
  (iii) the real-structure signature (inner KO-dim ---
  Theorem~\ref{thm:ko_dim_non_selection}).
\end{itemize}

These four theorems together formalise the structural-skeleton scope
pattern at the inner-factor level for the GeoVac framework's current
axiom set.  Each is conditional on the canonical CCM SM
representation.  The unconditional versions --- whether alternative
phenomenologically-consistent representations could correlate any of
the three free data with framework-derivable structure --- remain
long-range structural-research targets;  Read~2 sharpest falsifier
names the candidate construction for $N_{\mathrm{gen}}$, and
analogous targets exist for KO-dim (a non-canonical rep that admits
a different KO-dim while retaining SM phenomenology) and for Yukawa
values (a packing-derivable structure on $\mathcal{A}_F$ that selects
within the diagonal slice).

Paper~57 \S 6.3 (Forced/Free Seam observations paper) records the
trio plus Forced-Count Theorem as the C3 closure of the inner-factor
non-selection arc.
\end{remark}

% Single-cutoff spectral action, an OBSERVATION by stipulation (Sprint E7/E8,
% 2026-06-08).  Covers E7, E8, G3, G4 catalogue entries:  the
% renormalization sub-sector of the multi-focal-composition wall.
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{definition}[Multi-cutoff spectral action structure]
\label{def:multi_cutoff}
A multi-cutoff spectral action on a real spectral triple
$\mathcal{T} = (\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{H}, D)$ is a functional of the
form
\[
  S^{\mathrm{multi}}(D, \{\Lambda_i\}_{i=1}^k, \{f_i\}_{i=1}^k)
  \;=\; \sum_{i=1}^{k} c_i \, \mathrm{Tr}\, f_i(D/\Lambda_i)
\]
or more generally any functional of $D$ depending on $k \ge 2$
independent energy scales $\Lambda_1, \ldots, \Lambda_k$ in a manner
not reducible to a function of a single scale by reparametrization.
\end{definition}

\begin{observation}[Single-cutoff spectral action]
\label{thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} (true by stipulation, plus an inspection of the
listed axioms;\ not a proof over all derivable constructions)
The framework's current axiom set $\mathcal{A}$ (as defined in
\emph{Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}}) specifies the
Chamseddine--Connes spectral action $S(D, \Lambda) = \mathrm{Tr}\,
f(D/\Lambda)$ as a single-cutoff functional in the sense of
\emph{Definition~\ref{def:multi_cutoff}}, and none of the constructions
inspected below introduces a second independent scale.
\end{observation}

\begin{proof}[Inspection]
The argument is a structural inspection of each axiom in $\mathcal{A}$:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{Spectral action axiom (CCM).}  The standard CC spectral
  action $S(D, \Lambda) = \mathrm{Tr}\, f(D/\Lambda)$ specifies a
  single-cutoff functional by stipulation.  The argument $D/\Lambda$
  is a dimensionless ratio against a single energy scale.
\item \textbf{Paper 0 packing axiom + standard real-spectral-triple
  axioms + Hopf-tower truncation + Bertrand + Upgrade B.}  These
  axioms are algebraic, kinematic, and topological:\ they specify
  $(\mathcal{A}_F, \mathcal{H}_F, D_F, J_F, \gamma_F)$ and the outer
  manifold geometry, but introduce no energy-scale data.
\item \textbf{Inner fluctuations $D \to D + A$ for $A = a[D, b]$.}
  These preserve the single-Dirac structure of the underlying
  spectral triple.  The fluctuated operator $D' = D + A$ enters the
  spectral action as $S(D', \Lambda)$, still a single-cutoff
  functional.
\item \textbf{Tensor products $\mathcal{T}_1 \otimes \mathcal{T}_2$
  (Paper 39).}  These produce a new spectral triple with combined
  Dirac $D = D_1 \otimes \sigma_1 \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes \sigma_2 \otimes D_2$
  (the Clifford-doubled product of two odd triples), on which the spectral
  action $S(D, \Lambda)$ remains
  single-cutoff with the new combined $D$.  No second cutoff is
  introduced by the tensor-product construction.
\end{enumerate}

None of these introduces a second independent energy scale.  The
first item is a stipulation, and the list is an inspection of the
named constructions, not an enumeration of everything derivable from
$\mathcal{A}$. $\hfill\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Multi-loop renormalization counterterms are external calibration data]
\label{cor:multi_loop_renormalization_wall}
\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} \emph{Premise (not argued here):} multi-loop QED
renormalization counterterms (wavefunction renormalization $Z_2$, mass
counterterm $\delta m$, vertex counterterm $Z_1$, etc.) require a
second independent scale $\mu_R$ beside the ultraviolet cutoff
$\Lambda_{UV}$.  (An on-shell scheme, whose subtraction point is the
physical mass already carried by $D$, is a candidate counterexample to
the premise.)  Given the premise and
Observation~\ref{thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action}, the multi-loop QED
counterterms are external calibration data, supplied to the framework
rather than derived from it.

Under that premise the following catalogue entries from
Paper~57~\cite{paper57} are classified as multi-cutoff
renormalization calibration data:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{E7:} two-loop self-energy counterterms $Z_2, \delta m$.
\item \textbf{E8:} multi-loop QED corrections beyond the one-loop
  LS-7 closure.
\item \textbf{G3:} HF-5 multi-loop QED on hyperfine.
\item \textbf{G4:} LS-8a two-loop SE renormalization (identical to E7
  under the cross-domain unity of Paper~57~\S 4).
\end{itemize}

In Paper~36 (LS-8a) the framework's iterated Chamseddine--Connes
spectral action reproduces the two-loop UV-divergent integrand at the
right prefactor, sign, and divergence rate $\sim N^{3.79}$, but has not
generated the $Z_2, \delta m$ counterterms required for finite
multi-loop closure.  The corollary offers a structural reading of that
wall, conditional on its premise.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Honest scope]
\label{rem:single_cutoff_scope}
Observation~\ref{thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action} holds by
stipulation within the framework's current axiom set $\mathcal{A}$.  Two scope
clarifications:

\textbf{(i) The observation does not claim ``no multi-cutoff structure
exists.''}  It records that none of the inspected constructions from
$\mathcal{A}$ \emph{as currently stated} introduces a second scale.  A
sibling axiom adding renormalization-group flow machinery would
extend the axiom set and could in principle produce multi-cutoff
observables.  Such a sibling axiom is the analog of the
$N_{\mathrm{gen}}$ sibling-axiom direction
(\emph{Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}} unconditional version):
a logically-possible escape, named here, that requires new primitive
content beyond $\mathcal{A}$.

\textbf{(ii) The observation does not address the spatial-composition
sub-sector of the multi-focal-composition wall.}  The catalogue
entries G1 (HF-3 recoil cross-register $V_{eN}(\hat r_e, \hat R_N)$),
G2 (HF-4 Zemach magnetization density), and G5 (W1e chemistry
inner-region overattraction) lie outside this observation's scope:\ they
are spatial $\times$ spatial composition walls, structurally distinct
from the multi-cutoff renormalization sub-sector.  \textbf{[OPEN]} A general
multi-focal-composition wall theorem unifying both sub-sectors
remains the named open structural-research target.

Paper~57~\S 4 (cross-domain unity) and Paper~57~\S 6.3 record this
observation and its corollary as a conditional classification of the
renormalization sub-sector of the multi-focal-composition wall.
\end{remark}

% 2026-06-08).  Covers the remaining catalogue entries in the

% sector.  With thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action (renormalization
% half, conditional) it classifies the multi-focal-composition wall.
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[Tensor-product radial coupling does not autonomously match physical two-body profiles]
\label{thm:spatial_composition_radial_wall}
\textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT, with an empirical leg:\ the Pearson check
of step (2.4) ($0.846$, $0.796$ at $n_{\max} = 2, 3$) is a measurement,
so the argument covers only the structural part (2.1)--(2.3)]}
Let $\mathcal{T}_a = (\mathcal{A}_a, \mathcal{H}_a, D_a^{\mathrm{CH}})$ and
$\mathcal{T}_b = (\mathcal{A}_b, \mathcal{H}_b, D_b^{\mathrm{CH}})$ be two
GeoVac spectral triples on the same outer manifold $S^3$ at distinct
focal lengths $\lambda_a \neq \lambda_b$, equipped with the
Camporesi--Higuchi spinor Dirac operators.  The framework's natural
tensor-product spectral triple
$\mathcal{T}_a \otimes \mathcal{T}_b$~\cite{paper39} produces composed
two-body operators whose:
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item \textbf{Angular selection rules} are forced:\ Gaunt compatibility
  and $m$-conservation hold structurally for the composed algebra
  (Paper~54~\cite{paper54}, angular-structure theorem).  The interaction is pure $k = 0$ monopole at
  $n_{\max} \in \{2, 3\}$ (measured there), so the $k \ge 1$ multipole
  channels of the physical Coulomb interaction are \emph{not} reproduced
  at those cutoffs.
\item \textbf{Radial coupling profile} is determined autonomously by
  the combined Dirac structure (the Clifford-doubled graded product of
  Paper~39, since the naive $D_a^{\mathrm{CH}} \otimes 1 + \gamma_a
  \otimes D_b^{\mathrm{CH}}$ is unrealisable with $\gamma_a = 0$ on
  $S^3$), but does not autonomously
  coincide with the physical 1/r Coulomb form, the dipole-magnetization
  form, or other specific two-body radial profiles required by
  empirical observables.
\end{enumerate}
The framework's axiom set $\mathcal{A}$ contains no morphism enforcing
that the composed radial coupling profile matches a specific physical
two-body observable; matching a given observable requires external
specification.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The two assertions of the theorem stand on independent structural
content:

\textbf{(1) Angular selection rules.}  Paper~54's angular-structure
theorem~\cite{paper54} establishes that the composed algebra structure on
$\mathcal{A}_a \otimes \mathcal{A}_b$ acts on the two-body Hilbert
space through Wigner~3j coefficients, so the Gaunt and
$m$-conservation selection rules hold structurally;\ the multipole
content is measured there to be pure $k = 0$ at $n_{\max} \in \{2, 3\}$,
which is not the multipole hierarchy of the physical Coulomb interaction.  The selection rules are
a direct consequence of the SU(2) representation-theoretic structure
of the angular momentum operators on $S^3$, which the framework
inherits via the Fock projection (Paper~7) and the Hopf-tower truncation.

\textbf{(2) Radial coupling non-reproduction.} The structural reason
is the non-commutativity of the Fock-projection conformal factor with
the tensor-product construction:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item Paper~7's chordal-distance identity maps continuum $-Z/r$ on
  $\mathbb{R}^3$ to the Laplace--Beltrami operator on the unit $S^3$
  (whose harmonics the graph's nodes label;\ the graph Laplacian is not
  its discretisation and does not converge to it, Paper~7) via a focal-length-
  dependent stereographic conformal factor $c(\lambda)$.  Two
  different focal lengths $\lambda_a, \lambda_b$ give two different
  conformal factors $c_a, c_b$.
\item The tensor-product spectral triple
  $\mathcal{T}_a \otimes \mathcal{T}_b$ has continuum limit on the
  product manifold $S^3_a \times S^3_b$.  Under Fock projection, this
  corresponds to a six-dimensional space
  $\mathbb{R}^3_a \times \mathbb{R}^3_b$ parametrised by two
  independent position vectors, with combined conformal factor
  $c_{ab} \neq c_a \cdot c_b$ in general.
\item Physical two-body observables (V$_{eN}(\hat r_e, \hat R_N)$,
  Zemach magnetization, chemistry inner-region $V_{ee}(r_{12})$) are
  functions of the \emph{relative} coordinate $\vec r_a - \vec r_b$
  on $\mathbb{R}^3$ (three dimensions, not six).  The reduction from
  the six-dimensional product manifold to the three-dimensional
  relative manifold is not in the framework's axiom set.
\item Empirical verification:  Sprint resolvent\_two\_body
  (2026-06-01, \texttt{debug/sprint\_resolvent\_two\_body\_memo.md})
  tested four weightings of the resolvent
  $(D_a^{\mathrm{CH}})^{-2}$ against the physical 1/r Coulomb profile
  and found Pearson correlations of at most $0.846$ ($n_{\max} = 2$)
  and $0.796$ ($n_{\max} = 3$) for the best weighting (values after
  the 2026-08-29 ERI-evaluator fix), decreasing with $n_{\max}$.
  Paper~54 verified the angular selection rules but reported only
  partial radial matching (connected fraction $77\%$ at
  $n_{\max} = 2$, $32\%$ at $n_{\max} = 3$).
  Together these indicate that the framework's natural composition
  mechanisms (tensor product, resolvent, gauged spectral action) do
  not autonomously match physical two-body radial profiles.
\end{enumerate}

No axiom in $\mathcal{A}$ supplies the relative-coordinate reduction
from the six-dimensional product manifold to the three-dimensional
relative manifold, nor enforces a specific composed radial profile
on the tensor-product spectral triple.  Therefore the framework
specifies the angular selection rules of two-body operators
autonomously (via the SU(2) representation theory of $S^3$) but does not
autonomously match the radial structure of any specific physical
two-body observable. $\hfill\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Spatial-composition catalogue entries are calibration data]
\label{cor:spatial_composition_wall}
The following catalogue entries from Paper~57~\cite{paper57} are
classified as spatial-composition calibration data:
\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item \textbf{G1:} HF-3 recoil cross-register
  $V_{eN}(\hat r_e, \hat R_N)$ (Track NI cross-register, Sprint HF
  2026-05-07);
\item \textbf{G2:} HF-4 Zemach magnetization density (Sprint HF
  2026-05-07);
\item \textbf{G5:} W1e chemistry inner-region overattraction (Sprint
  W1c arc 2026-05-23).
\end{itemize}
Each requires a specific physical radial coupling profile that
Theorem~\ref{thm:spatial_composition_radial_wall} establishes is not
autonomously produced by the framework's tensor-product composition
mechanism.  The angular selection rules are forced; the radial content
(and, at the measured cutoffs, the $k \ge 1$ multipoles) is external
calibration.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Multi-focal-composition wall:\ spatial half bound by argument, renormalization half conditional]
\label{rem:multi_focal_wall_fully_characterized}
Together with
Observation~\ref{thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action} +
Corollary~\ref{cor:multi_loop_renormalization_wall} (E7, E8, G3, G4),
Theorem~\ref{thm:spatial_composition_radial_wall} +
Corollary~\ref{cor:spatial_composition_wall} (G1, G2, G5) classify seven
catalogue entries of the multi-focal-composition wall family
(Paper~57~\S 4 cross-domain unity):\ the spatial-composition entries at
proof-by-argument level with an empirical leg, the renormalization
entries conditionally on the premise of
Corollary~\ref{cor:multi_loop_renormalization_wall}:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}
\item \textbf{Renormalization sub-sector} (E7, E8, G3, G4): classified by
  Observation~\ref{thm:single_cutoff_spectral_action} (by stipulation)
  and its conditional corollary.
\item \textbf{Spatial-composition sub-sector} (G1, G2, G5): closed by
  Theorem~\ref{thm:spatial_composition_radial_wall} — tensor-product
  composition carries the angular selection rules but not the radial
  structure;
  no relative-coordinate-reduction morphism in $\mathcal{A}$.
\end{itemize}

An eighth catalogue entry (G6) is the H1 Yukawa non-selection, closed
by Theorem~\ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection}'s companion result
(Theorem~\ref{thm:h1_yukawa_non_selection}).  The entries are finer than the instances:\ all
six independent multi-focal-composition wall instances of
Paper~57~\S 4 (H1, LS-8a, HF-3/4/5, W1e; G6 $=$ F1 is not independent)
are characterised by one of the three results (LS-8a and HF-5 only conditionally).

The spatial-composition sub-sector of the multi-focal-composition wall
is bound by argument at the canonical-representation level;\ the
renormalization sub-sector is classified conditionally.  Together with the inner-factor
results (the Forced-Count Theorem~\ref{thm:forced_count}, a dimension
count, and the non-selection theorems \ref{thm:n_gen_non_selection},
\ref{thm:ko_dim_non_selection} and H1), the corpus carries five
proof-by-argument results covering the inner-factor structural-skeleton
boundary and the spatial-composition sub-sector, and a
stipulation-level Observation with a conditional corollary for the
multi-loop QED renormalization sub-sector.

Paper~57~\S 6.3 records this classification.
\end{remark}

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
% E6 — Combination rule K = π(B + F − Δ) no-single-mechanism OBSERVATION (demoted from theorem 2026-09-28)
% (Sprint E6 closure, 2026-06-08).
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{observation}[No single-mechanism generation of the combination rule $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ has been found]
\label{obs:no_single_mechanism_K}
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Twelve candidate mechanisms for single-principle
generation of the empirical combination rule $K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$
for $\alpha^{-1}$ (Paper~2) have been empirically eliminated (Paper~2
\S IV.G; Phases 4B--4I, April 2026, eight mechanisms; Sprint A
$\alpha$-SP, 2026-04-18, Connes--Chamseddine direct spectral-action
derivation; Sprint K-CC, 2026-05-03, three further mechanisms via PSLQ
at 100~dps against a 244-element basis, a natural $\Lambda$ search from
$S^3$ spectral data, and a unified CC heat-kernel route blocked by the
algebraic ``T9 obstruction'' of Paper~2 --- not Paper~28's Theorem~T9, the $\zeta_{D^2}$ closed form).  The three ingredients
have separate spectral homes ($B = 42$ a finite Casimir trace, $F =
\pi^2/6$ a Fock-degeneracy Dirichlet value, $\Delta = 1/40 =
(g_3^{\rm Dirac})^{-1}$ an inverse Dirac mode degeneracy;
Observation~\ref{obs:three_sector}), and no single mechanism is
currently exhibited that produces their combination with the weights
$(+1, +1, -1)$ and the external $\pi$ prefactor.
\end{observation}

\begin{remark}[E6 scope:\ why this is an Observation, not a theorem]
\label{rem:e6_scope}
The two-fact structural argument for a theorem does not stand.  A sub-ring
partition assigning the rationals $B = 42$ and $\Delta = 1/40$ to
different Mellin sub-rings fails, since the sub-rings are not
exclusive:\  $\mathbb{Q}$ lies in both
the $M_1$ ring $\mathbb{Q}[\pi, \pi^{-1}]$ and the $M_2$ ring
$\bigoplus_k \pi^{2k}\mathbb{Q}$, so $B + F - \Delta$ lies wholly in
the $M_2$ ring and no mixing of sub-rings occurs.  Nor can the
$\pi$-source case-exhaustion
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pi_source_case_exhaustion}) be invoked for \emph{every}
transcendental:\ that theorem is [CONDITIONAL] and covers only
the first fifteen of the twenty-eight Paper~34 projections;\ it also
sits in tension with the remark that the spectral action engages $M_1$
and $M_2$ jointly (Sec.~\ref{sec:pi_source_theorem}).  What survives is
the empirical record of twelve eliminations, recorded above as an
Observation.  It does not derive $K$, and it does not show that $K$
cannot be generated by a single mechanism from some extended axiom set.

Per the project's standing hard prohibition, the Paper~2 combination
rule is labeled an \emph{Observation} (not a conjecture or
derivation);\ nothing in this section promotes or demotes that label.
Paper~2's status as Observations-folder paper (post 2026-05-02
curve-fit-audit migration) is unchanged.  Catalogue entry E6 (the
combination rule for $\alpha^{-1}$) is classified as calibration data
with no single mechanism found:\ the framework provides three
structural homes for the ingredients but no principle for their
combination.
\end{remark}

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
% D5/D6 — Cutoff function and CC fine-tuning external-selection
% theorem (Sprint D5/D6 closure, 2026-06-08).
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[Cutoff function $f$ and its Mellin moments are external calibration data]
\label{thm:cutoff_function_external}
\textbf{[PROOF-BY-ARGUMENT]}
The framework's axiom set $\mathcal{A}$ specifies the
Chamseddine--Connes spectral action functional $S(D, \Lambda, f) =
\mathrm{Tr}\, f(D/\Lambda)$ on a real spectral triple.  The cutoff
function $f$ enters as a Schwartz-class test function on $[0, \infty)$
(Paper~51) but is not constrained by any axiom in $\mathcal{A}$.
Consequently the Mellin moments
\[
  \varphi(s) \;=\; \int_0^\infty x^{s-1} f(x) \, dx
\]
are external calibration data:\ no morphism in $\mathcal{A}$ selects
the moment values $\varphi(0), \varphi(1), \varphi(2), \ldots$.

The Wald two-term-exactness $\Rightarrow$ pure Einstein
$\Rightarrow$ action-$G \equiv$ entropy-$G$ argument (Paper~51 \S G7)
forces \emph{relations} among the moments via the sector-wise Mellin
moment map (tip $\leftrightarrow \varphi(0)$, Einstein--Hilbert
$\leftrightarrow \varphi(1)$, $\Lambda_{cc} \leftrightarrow \varphi(2)$);
these relations follow by argument within $\mathcal{A}$ (the Wald
step itself is a theorem).  The moment
\emph{values} are not constrained by $\mathcal{A}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[CC fine-tuning is external moment selection]
\label{cor:cc_fine_tuning_external}
The observed cosmological-constant problem in the framework's
sector-wise Mellin moment formulation (Paper~51 \S G4-5) requires
\[
  \Lambda_{cc} \cdot G_{\rm eff} \;\approx\; 36\pi \cdot
  \frac{\varphi(2)}{\varphi(1)^2}
  \;\approx\; 10^{-122}
\]
(dimensionless), which is consistent with the framework's structural
relations between $G_N$, $\Lambda_{cc}$, and $S_{\rm BH}$
(Wald, theorem-grade) provided $\varphi(2)/\varphi(1)^2 \approx
10^{-124}$ with $\varphi(0), \varphi(1) = O(1)$.  No natural cutoff
function $f$ produces this fine-tuned moment ratio, and no axiom in
$\mathcal{A}$ constrains the moment values to it.

Catalogue entries D5 (cutoff function moments $\varphi(0), \varphi(1),
\varphi(2)$) and D6 (cosmological constant value $\Lambda_{cc}$) are
external calibration data inheriting from the cutoff function
selection.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[D5/D6 scope and the sibling-axiom direction]
\label{rem:d5_d6_scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cutoff_function_external} formalises the precise
location of the cosmological-constant problem in the GeoVac framework:\
the gravitational \emph{relations} are forced (Wald, Paper~51 \S G7);
the cutoff-function \emph{moments} are external.  The CC problem in
this framing is a moment-selection problem on the cutoff function
$f$, structurally similar to the Higgs--Yukawa selection problem
(\emph{Theorem}~H1) but on a different calibration object.

A sibling axiom that constrains the cutoff function or its moments
(e.g., a renormalisation-group flow axiom, a regulator-independence
principle, an asymptotic-safety constraint) would extend $\mathcal{A}$
and could in principle select the moment values.  None is currently
exhibited;  the long-range structural-research direction is named
here as the natural analog of the inner-factor sibling-axiom
directions.

Paper~57~\S 6.3 records this theorem as the gravity sector closure
of the C-arc.
\end{remark}

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
% Chemistry-side η-trivialization analog — open structural-research
% target (Sprint Chemistry-Analog closure, 2026-06-08).
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{remark}[Chemistry-side $\eta$-trivialization analog: open structural-research target]
\label{rem:chemistry_eta_analog}
The SM-side $\eta$-trivialization theorem (Paper~18 \texttt{thm:eta\_trivialization})
restricts the inner factor to the $M_1$/$M_2$ Mellin \emph{slots} (the
$M_3$ slot vanishes) on any
Krajewski-class even finite spectral triple satisfying the chirality
grading axiom $\{\gamma_F, D_F\} = 0$:\ for all odd $k$, $\mathrm{Tr}(D_F^k
\cdot e^{-tD_F^2}) \equiv 0$, so $M_3$ (vertex-parity Hurwitz / Catalan $G$ /
Dirichlet $L$) vanishes on the inner factor.  One reading (an
Observation, not a derivation) is that this is why F1 (Yukawa values)
and F4--F7 (Higgs VEV, CKM, PMNS, neutrino masses) live outside the master Mellin engine's structural skeleton:\
the inner-factor Yukawa values occupy the M1/M2 slots, but their
generators are tied to the couplings $y_i$ and are not among those of
the rings the framework's outer-factor mechanism generates (an Observation-level
reading;\ the rings are not disjoint, since both contain
$\mathbb{Q}$).

The chemistry-side analog would be a structural property of the
FrozenCore $Z_{\rm eff}(r)$ pipeline that mirrors $\{\gamma_F, D_F\} =
0$ on the chemistry inner-factor side:\ a chirality-style grading on
the Clementi--Raimondi $Z_{\rm eff}(r)$ structure that imposes a
similar slot restriction on chemistry inner-factor data.  Such
a property is not currently exhibited.  The most concrete candidate
direction would identify a discrete grading on the FrozenCore radial
profile pipeline (e.g., parity of multi-zeta exponents, partition of
core orbital basis into chirality-graded blocks) that produces an
$\eta$-trivialization-style theorem for chemistry inner-factor data.

Sprint-scale path is unclear:\ unlike the SM-side
$\eta$-trivialization, which follows directly from a single axiom
($\{\gamma_F, D_F\} = 0$, the standard grading axiom of even finite
spectral triples, used in Krajewski's 1998 classification~\cite{krajewski1998} and
canonically adopted in Chamseddine--Connes--Marcolli 2007~\cite{ccm2007}), the chemistry-side analog would
need to identify the analog grading axiom from scratch.  This is a
substantial NCG-research target.

Catalogue entries H6 (Clementi--Raimondi $Z_{\rm eff}$ exponents) and
H7 (multi-zeta coefficients in second-row valence basis) remain
empirical-only pending this structural-research target's completion.
The empirical-grade content (W1e period-class diagnostic,
\texttt{debug/sprint\_w1e\_period\_class\_memo.md}, 2026-06-04)
found no relation placing H6, H7 in $M_1 \cup M_2 \cup M_3$ at audit
ceiling 100 (a PSLQ null result);  the open question is whether they sit in a structurally
distinct chemistry-side ring forced by a hypothetical analog axiom.

Paper~57~\S 6.3 records this remark as the named substantial
structural-research target for the chemistry sector; the C-arc's
sprint-scale run closes at v3.103.0 with this target named but not
attempted.
\end{remark}

After Sprints C2 + C3 + F3 + E7/E8 + G1/G2/G5 + E6 + D5/D6 +
Chemistry-Analog (all 2026-06-08, v3.97.0--v3.103.0), the corpus

dimension count and five non-selection results), one stipulation-level
Observation (the single-cutoff spectral action) and one
empirical non-selection Observation across the following sectors:

\textbf{Inner-factor sector} (closed at canonical-rep level):

\textbf{Multi-focal-composition wall} (one sub-sector theorem with an

\item Spatial-composition radial wall (\ref{thm:spatial_composition_radial_wall}).

\textbf{$\alpha$ combination rule sector} (single-mechanism
generation not found; empirical, not closed):
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}\setcounter{enumi}{6}

  (Observation~\ref{obs:no_single_mechanism_K}; twelve mechanisms
  eliminated).
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Gravity sector} (cutoff function external-selection closed):
\begin{enumerate}\setlength{\itemsep}{1pt}\setcounter{enumi}{7}

  (\ref{thm:cutoff_function_external}).

The chemistry-side $\eta$-trivialization analog
(Remark~\ref{rem:chemistry_eta_analog}) is the named substantial
structural-research target;  catalogue entries H6, H7 remain
empirical-only pending its completion.

The remaining empirical-only catalogue entries (F4--F7 Higgs VEV /
CKM / PMNS / neutrino mass values, structurally inheriting from F1
and F4;  I-entries foundational calibration) are subsidiary residuals
inheriting from theorems above (Higgs admission B7 + H1 Yukawa
non-selection) or sitting outside the structural-skeleton scope
(Born rule via Gleason).

The full forced/free seam (Paper~57) is therefore in a stable terminal
state for the path-\#1 vein C arc:\ six proof-by-argument
results, one stipulation-level and one empirical Observation across four sectors, one named substantial follow-on (chemistry
$\eta$-trivialization analog), all subsidiary residuals accounted for.

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Frontier-of-field framing: GeoVac-internal walls vs.
  spectral-action open problems}
\label{sec:frontier_framing}
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The five walls catalogued in the multi-focal-composition audit
(\texttt{debug/multifocal\_phase\_a\_synthesis\_memo.md}, May~2026)
are not all GeoVac-specific.  Reading them through the spectral-triple
construction of Sec.~\ref{sec:construction} and the Marcolli--van~Suijlekom
lineage of Sec.~\ref{sec:lineage}, three are GeoVac-internal (two
architectural gaps, and one, W1c, built without closing the chemistry
wall it targeted), and two sit at the broader frontier
of the spectral-action / NCG programs.  We name them here for honesty
and to fix the scope of the open questions of Sec.~\ref{sec:open}.

\paragraph{GeoVac-internal walls (W1a, W1b, W1c).}
\textbf{[OPEN]}

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{W1a (cross-register coordinate operator):}\ no two-body
spatial operator $V(\hat{\mathbf{r}}_e, \hat{\mathbf{R}}_n)$ has been
implemented across the nuclear and electronic registers of the
composed nuclear--electronic Hamiltonian (Paper~23~\cite{paper23}~\S
VI; \texttt{geovac/nuclear/nuclear\_electronic.py}).  The framework
couples the registers through spin-spin hyperfine and a classical
finite-size shift but evaluates spatial operators at the classical
proton position.  This is an architectural gap, not a no-go theorem;
the calibrated atomic-physics target is the
Pachucki--Patko\v{s}--Yerokhin recoil Hamiltonian (PRL 130, 023004,
2023), which is exact in the mass ratio at $(Z\alpha)^6$.
\item \textbf{W1b (magnetization-distribution operator):}\ no
operator on the proton register represents the Zemach radius
$r_Z$ as a Layer-2 magnetization focal length distinct from the
charge radius $r_p$ (Paper~34~\cite{paper34}~\S\,III; Sprint HF-4,
May~2026).  Architectural gap; QCD-internal magnetization density is
input data, not GeoVac-internal.
\item \textbf{W1c (cross-center frozen-core screening):}\ built
(Paper~19;\ the W1c sprint arc is in the chronicle file):\ cross-center screening of bare $V_{ne}$ through
the multipole expansion is implemented and bit-faithful to quadrature,
yet the NaH PES of that pipeline has no internal minimum.  The residual
is the W1d/W1e hierarchy of Paper~19 (block-diagonal h1;\ inner-region
overattraction), which five sprint-scale closure mechanisms did not
remove;\ Paper~58 localizes W1e to the Hamiltonian specification (the
neglected inter-center overlap), not the correlation treatment:\ with
genuine end-to-end integrals NaH binds, at the cost of the sparsity.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} W1a and W1b are tooling gaps inside the present
framework:\ the missing operators exist in principle and require no
new mathematics in the broader NCG literature, though each is a
substantial program rather than a bounded sprint.  W1c is different:\
it was built, and the chemistry wall persists (W1d/W1e).  None of the
three undermines the spectral-triple framing of
Sec.~\ref{sec:construction}.

\paragraph{Frontier-of-field walls (W2a, W2b).}
\textbf{[OPEN]}

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \textbf{W2a (multi-loop UV/IR composition):}\ the bare
iterated Connes--Chamseddine spectral action on the
Camporesi--Higuchi spectrum reproduces UV-divergent integrands of
multi-loop QED (Sprint LS-8a, May~2026; Paper~36~\cite{paper36}~\S
VII) but cannot autonomously generate $Z_2$, $\delta m$, $Z_3$
counterterms for finite extraction at two loops.  This is not
GeoVac-specific.  The Fathizadeh--Marcolli periods-and-motives analysis of
Robertson--Walker spectral actions~\cite{fathizadeh_marcolli2016}
partially addresses spectral-action coefficient structure on
homogeneous compact backgrounds, and the subsequent Hekkelman--McDonald
2025 noncommutative integral approximation~\cite{hekkelman_mcdonald2024}
provides a single-cutoff Szeg\H{o}-style asymptotic theory in the
Connes--van~Suijlekom paradigm; neither closes the multi-cutoff
matching question that renormalization counterterm generation
requires.  The published QFT archetype is the matched effective-field-theory
chain (e.g.\ HQET~$\to$~NRQED~$\to$~pNRQED~$\to$~SCET; Fontes and
Szafron, JHEP 05 (2025) 171), where each matching step is at a single physical scale and
imports $\overline{MS}$ counterterms by hand.  No published spectral-action
framework reproduces this autonomously.  W2a is therefore an open
question of the spectral-action program, of which GeoVac is one
specific instance; it is not a defect of the present construction
relative to the lineage.

\item \textbf{W2b (cross-manifold spectral-triple composition):}\ the
tensor product of two infinite metric spectral triples
$(\mathcal{T}_{S^3}, d_{D_{S^3}}) \otimes (\mathcal{T}_{S^5}, d_{D_{S^5}})$,
needed to compose the GeoVac Coulomb sector (Sec.~\ref{sec:construction})
with the Bargmann--Segal HO sector of Paper~24~\cite{paper24}, has no
published Latr\'emoli\`ere-propinquity convergence theorem.  The
almost-commutative case (one infinite Riemannian factor + one finite
factor) is addressed by Latr\'emoli\`ere 2026~\cite{latremoliere2026}
(\emph{Spectral continuity of almost commutative manifolds for the
$C^1$ topology on Riemannian metrics}), in the sense of metric
continuity of almost-commutative models $C(M) \otimes \mathfrak{B}$ as
the metric varies;\ the two-infinite case is not handled in that
paper (implicitly, by its construction), nor in the closely related
Farsi--Latr\'emoli\`ere--Packer 2024~\cite{farsi_latremoliere_packer2024} inductive-sequence work.  This is the structural content of the G4b cross-manifold
blocker (Sec.~\ref{sec:g4}):\ not a GeoVac-specific limitation but a
genuinely open NCG question.  Closing it would itself be a substantial
mathematical contribution, parallel to but separate from Paper~38's
SU(2) state-space GH convergence theorem.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Forecast (without claiming closure).}

We do not claim W1a can be closed without surprises;\ the
multi-$\lambda$ Shibuya--Wulfman algebraic check that gates the W1a
attack (multipole termination across mismatched exponents)
is not in the published Avery-school literature and could fail to
preserve Gaunt-style closure (Track~3 literature memo, May~2026,
\S 1).  W1b is bounded by external QCD input.  W2a and W2b are open
in the broader literature, not just in the GeoVac record.  The honest
forecast is that closing W1a/b is internal-effort plausible as a
substantial program per wall, while the W1e residual of W1c is
localized to the Hamiltonian specification (Paper~58) and has no
sparsity-preserving closure;\ closing W2a or W2b would be a
contribution to the spectral-action / NCG programs rather than to
GeoVac alone, and we do not commit to it as a deliverable here.

\paragraph{Implication for the Marcolli--vS lineage placement.}

Sec.~\ref{sec:lineage} read the present construction as a graph
spectral triple in the lineage of \cite{marcolli_vs2014, perez_sanchez2024,
perez_sanchez2025}.  The frontier-of-field framing above sharpens this:\
the lineage placement is correct, and the W2a/W2b walls are the lineage's
own open problems, not GeoVac's separate concerns.  This is consistent
with Observation~\ref{obs:lineage} (each structural ingredient has
published precedent;\ what is not duplicated is the $\alpha$
observation:\ $K$ matches $\alpha^{-1}$ to $4.8 \times 10^{-7}$, and the
root of the cubic $1/\alpha = K - \alpha^2$ matches to $8.8 \times 10^{-8}$).  The W1 walls remain GeoVac-side
because they are about the architectural extension of the present
triple to multi-particle real-space coupling, which the SM-style almost-commutative
construction does not address.

% -------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Lorentzian extension (descoped)}
\label{sec:lorentzian_audit_paper32}
% -------------------------------------------------------------------

The Lorentzian sprint record of this section (the Sprint~L0 transfer
audit of the master Mellin engine under signature change, the Krein
carrier and Krein-space Dirac operator of Sprints L2-B/C, the Connes
axiom audit at BBB $(m, n) = (4, 6)$ of Sprint L2-D, the Krein-carrier
period closure of Sprint L2-E, and the finite-cutoff modular-Hamiltonian
construction of Sprint L1) is kept verbatim in
\texttt{docs/chronicles/paper\_32\_chronicle.tex}.  What survives of it:
the finite-cutoff $2\pi$ period closure is automatic for any
integer-spectrum generator and identifies no Bisognano--Wichmann flow
(Paper~42;\ \texttt{tests/test\_modular\_period\_closure\_automatic.py});
compressing a Krein-self-adjoint product Dirac to the Krein-positive cone
annihilates the spatial Dirac, and the Lorentzian programme is descoped
(Paper~45).

% ===================================================================
\section{Marcolli--van~Suijlekom Placement: Instance + Extension}
\label{sec:lineage}
% ===================================================================

The construction of Sec.~\ref{sec:construction} is an \emph{instance}
of the gauge networks framework of Marcolli and
van~Suijlekom~\cite{marcolli_vs2014} (with the Perez-Sanchez
correction~\cite{perez_sanchez2025} that the continuum limit is
Yang--Mills without Higgs) at the data level, together with three
structural additions:\ the choice of label set (Bertrand's theorem and
Fock's projection single out $S^3$, both classical results), the
master Mellin engine $\mathcal{M}[\mathrm{Tr}(D^k e^{-tD^2})]$ at
$k \in \{0,1,2\}$ tagging the framework's transcendentals (a
\textbf{[CONDITIONAL]} case-check over the first fifteen projections), and
the cosmic-Galois layer $U^*$ constructed in Paper~56~\cite{loutey_paper56}.  Marcolli--van~Suijlekom~\cite{marcolli_vs2014}
does not predict a Tannakian dual;\ the $U^*$ sits on top of the
gauge-network scaffolding as additional structure, not as an MvS
corollary.  The key parallels at the data level:

\begin{itemize}\setlength{\itemsep}{2pt}
\item \emph{Vertex algebra.}  Marcolli--vS take $\mathcal{A}_V = C(V)$
on a vertex set $V$; we take $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} = \mathbb{C}^{V_{\mathrm{Fock}}}$
on the Fock-projected $S^3$ vertex set (Sec.~\ref{sec:construction}).
\item \emph{Edge connection.}  Marcolli--vS take a connection on
edges valued in a compact unitary group (edge maps in $U(N)$); Paper~25
takes $G = U(1)$ and Paper~30 takes $G = \mathrm{SU}(2)$.
\item \emph{Spectral action.}  Marcolli--vS show that the spectral
action of the resulting almost-commutative spectral triple equals a
Wilson lattice gauge action on the connection; Paper~30 verifies this
explicitly for $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ at $n_{\max} = 3, 4$.
\item \emph{Continuum limit.}  Per Perez-Sanchez, the continuum limit
of the gauge-network spectral action is Yang--Mills without a Higgs
sector.  Paper~28's heat-kernel Seeley--DeWitt expansion is the
spectral-action expansion of the Camporesi--Higuchi Dirac on $S^3$, not
of the gauge-network action.
\end{itemize}

\begin{observation}[Lineage placement]
\label{obs:lineage}
Each structural ingredient of the GeoVac spectral triple has published
precedent: finite almost-commutative spectral triples
(\cite{krajewski1998,paschke_sitarz1998,cacic2013}), graph spectral
triples (\cite{marcolli_vs2014,perez_sanchez2024}), spectral
truncations (\cite{connes_vs2021,hekkelman2022,hekkelman_mcdonald2024}), and
the Standard Model spectral action (\cite{chamseddine_connes1997_2008}).
What is not duplicated in the literature is the $\alpha$ observation
($K$ matches $\alpha^{-1}$ to $4.8 \times 10^{-7}$;\ the root of the cubic
$1/\alpha = K - \alpha^2$ matches to $8.8 \times 10^{-8}$) from a discrete
spectral construction; that remains the original empirical Observation
of Paper~2.
\end{observation}

This is part of what the working hypothesis WH1 in the project register
rests on (working-hypothesis register;\ its commutative-factor convergence leg proved as of 2026-06-10, the almost-commutative structure's convergence open): the structural
framing is in the published lineage; the empirical claim is not
duplicated.

% ===================================================================
\section{Open Questions}
\label{sec:open}
% ===================================================================

The construction of Sec.~\ref{sec:construction} and the audit of
Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit} leave the following structural questions
genuinely open.

\paragraph{Q1.  Order one beyond rank 1.}  \textbf{[OPEN]}  The order-one condition
holds vacuously for the diagonal $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}$ (whose
commutators with $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$ vanish,
Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded_commutators}), \emph{fails} at finite
$n_{\max} \ge 2$ on the Fock/Weyl model of the operator-system
representative (Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit}) and, from $n_{\max} = 1$,
on the true window (audit item~6), and non-trivially constrains the rank-1
non-abelian extension $\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}} \otimes M_2(\mathbb{C})$
of Paper~30 in the gauge-network setting.  At
rank~$\ge 2$ (e.g., $M_3(\mathbb{C})$ or sums of matrix algebras as in
the Standard Model triple), order-one becomes a strong constraint and
typically requires modification of the Dirac operator
($\textit{first-order condition} \Rightarrow$ Higgs sector
in~\cite{chamseddine_connes1997_2008}).  Whether GeoVac admits a natural
rank-$\ge 2$ extension---and what its spectral action would predict---is
an open question.  The Bargmann--Segal $S^5$ HO triple of
Paper~24 was probed for an $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ extension in
Sprint~5 (project development-frontier log) with negative outcome; that is one negative
data point, not the full picture.  The electroweak slice
$\mathcal{A}_F = \mathbb{C} \oplus \mathbb{H}$ (rank up to 2 via
$\mathbb{H} \cong M_2(\mathbb{C})$ over $\mathbb{C}$, a real
$*$-subalgebra) is the most reachable test, and Sprint~H1
addresses it; see Sec.~\ref{sec:higgs_h1} below for the verdict.

\paragraph{Q2.  Real structure at finite $n_{\max}$ (resolved for the
$J$-relations; order zero/one open).}
\textbf{[PANEL-VERIFIED]}
Proposition~\ref{prop:reality} establishes a real structure on the
continuum spinor bundle that restricts to the truncation.  The axiom
audit of Sec.~\ref{sec:axiom_audit} pins the finite-$n_{\max}$ behaviour
down to machine precision: the restricted $J_{\mathrm{GV}}^{(n_{\max})}$
satisfies $J^2 = -\mathbb{1}$ (\emph{not} $+\mathbb{1}$), $JD = +DJ$ and
$J\mathcal{O}J^{-1} = \mathcal{O}$ bit-exactly at every $n_{\max}$
examined in the audit (\texttt{test\_real\_structure}), so for these
three relations the finite-versus-limit question is closed in favour of
finite-$n_{\max}$ exactness.  No KO-dimension label is attached at
finite cutoff:\ the chirality-doubled model truncation is even
(Proposition~\ref{prop:odd}(b)) with sign triple $(-, +, +)$, the true
window that converges is odd like the continuum (measured,
$n_{\max} \le 3$;\ Proposition~\ref{prop:odd}(c)) with only the pair
$(\varepsilon, \varepsilon') = (-, +)$ defined, and the KO-3 label
belongs to the continuum factor.  \textbf{[MEASURED]}
\textbf{[OPEN]} The order-zero and order-one conditions on the
Fock/Weyl model of the operator-system representative fail at finite
$n_{\max}$ with residuals that grow with the cutoff (order zero over
$n_{\max} = 2, 3, 4$, order one over $n_{\max} = 2, 3$).
\textbf{[MEASURED]} On the true Camporesi--Higuchi window of the
convergence theorem the three $J$-relations hold exactly and order zero
and order one fail from $n_{\max} = 1$, with residuals growing toward
the generators' sup-norm scale, identified as a Toeplitz boundary term
confined to the top Dirac levels (audit item~6,
\texttt{tests/test\_true\_window\_real\_structure.py}).  Open:\ the
mechanism on the model, and the sense (if any) in which the continuum
identities are recovered --- on the true window the norm residual does
not decay, while its normalised Hilbert--Schmidt weight falls over
$n_{\max} = 2$--$5$ (measured in the audit driver, not test-pinned).

\paragraph{Q3.  KO-dimension and the four-way coincidence.}  \textbf{[OPEN]}  Working
hypothesis WH4 asks: is the four-way $S^3$ coincidence (Sec.~\ref{sec:intro})
forced by the $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~$3$ structure of the present
triple's continuum factor, or is it independent geometric data?  The relevant test is
whether any other rank-1 spectral triple of $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~3
fails to produce all four roles.  $S^3$ is unique among compact rank-1
non-abelian simply-connected groups, so the question reduces to:
within the Marcolli--vS gauge-network setting, does
$\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~3 + non-abelian fiber + non-trivial Hopf
bundle uniquely select $S^3$?  The lineage literature does not
appear to address this directly.

\paragraph{Q4.  Spectral-action selection of $\Lambda$.}  \textbf{[OPEN]}  Paper~28's
spectral-action computation produced an exact two-term form
$\mathrm{Tr}\,e^{-D^2/\Lambda^2} = (\sqrt{\pi}/2)\Lambda^3 -
(\sqrt{\pi}/4)\Lambda + O(e^{-\pi^2\Lambda^2})$ but did not
autonomously select $\Lambda$.  Setting the trace equal to $K/\pi$
gave a closed-form Cardano solution $\Lambda_\infty = 3.7102\ldots$
(50 dps), which was tautological in the sense that it was determined
by demanding the equality.  Whether there is a structural principle
that selects $\Lambda$ is open; if so, $K/\pi$ would become a derived
quantity rather than a coincidence.  This is the most direct route by
which Paper~2 could be promoted from observation to derivation, and it
is also the one most resistant to incremental progress.

\paragraph{Q5.  Higher-form structure (Breit / rank-2 connections).}
\textbf{[OPEN]}
Paper~25~\S VII.B asked whether the Breit interaction (rank-2 in
electron-electron coupling) lifts the $U(1)$ 1-form picture to a
higher-form gauge theory.  In spectral-triple language this is the
question whether the GeoVac triple admits a natural extension to a
rank-2 fiber that produces the Breit retarded integrals.  Paper~30's
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ result is rank~1 (vector); rank~2 (tensor) is a
distinct extension; the answer is unknown.

\paragraph{Q6.  Convergence beyond the commutative factor.}
\textbf{[OPEN]}  Theorem~\ref{thm:gh_convergence} proves state-space
convergence of the commutative factor on the true window, under the
translation seminorm.  Two convergence statements are not proved:\ the
same convergence in the Dirac-commutator seminorm, and convergence of
the almost-commutative (gauge-network, $\mathcal{A} \otimes
M_n(\mathbb{C})$) structure that WH1 names
(Remark~\ref{rem:wh1_proven}).

% ===================================================================
\section{Conclusion}
\label{sec:conclusion}
% ===================================================================

\textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]} This paper has constructed an explicit finite spectral triple
$(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GV}}, \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GV}},
D_{\mathrm{GV}})$ on the Fock-projected $S^3$ graph and audited it
against the standard Connes axioms (Table~\ref{tab:axiom_audit},
Theorem~\ref{thm:axiom_complete}).  The diagonal triple is a spectral,
not a metric, object ($[D_{\mathrm{GV}}, \pi(a)] = 0$); the metric
role and the continuum limit belong to the operator-system
representative on the true Camporesi--Higuchi window (Paper~38),
metrized by the left-translation seminorm:\ convergence of that
commutative factor is proved, with the bound $d_{\mathrm{GH}} \le
\gamma_{n_{\max}}$ of Paper~38 \textbf{[INTERNAL THEOREM]}, while Dirac-seminorm
convergence and convergence of the almost-commutative structure are
\textbf{[OPEN]} (Q6).  At finite cutoff, on the Fock/Weyl model objects,
the three $J$-relations hold exactly, the model truncation is even
(sign triple $(-, +, +)$, no KO label), and order zero and order one
fail with residuals that grow with the cutoff (\textbf{[MEASURED]},
mechanism open);\ on the true window the three $J$-relations also hold
exactly and order zero and order one also fail, by a Toeplitz boundary
term of the compression (\textbf{[MEASURED]}).
\textbf{[OBSERVATION]} Papers~25, 28, 30, 31 are read as projections of
the resulting structure (Table~\ref{tab:subsectors}), Papers~25 and~30
through the gauge-network Dirac rather than $D_{\mathrm{GV}}$.  The construction sits inside the
published Marcolli--van~Suijlekom gauge-network framework with the
Perez-Sanchez correction.  The combination rule
$K = \pi(B + F - \Delta)$ is phrased as a sum across three
sectors of distinct type in the same triple
(Observation~\ref{obs:three_sector}), without changing its status as
an observation.  \textbf{[OBSERVATION]} The Coulomb/HO asymmetry of Paper~31 is the
statement that the GeoVac triple's continuum limit is one specific real
odd $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension-3 triple, not a generic class
(Observation~\ref{obs:pi_coulomb}); its true-window truncation is odd
like the limit (measured, $n_{\max} \le 3$), and only the chirality-doubled model truncation is even
(Proposition~\ref{prop:odd}).

Four things this paper did not do.  It did not derive $\alpha$;
Paper~2 stays in Observations.  It did not claim the Standard Model:\
the G4a construction admits the SM gauge content only with imposed
Yukawa, hypercharge and generation data (\S\ref{sec:g4}).  It did not prove that the
four-way $S^3$ coincidence is forced by $\mathrm{KO}$-dimension~3;
that is open question Q3.  It did not identify a Bisognano--Wichmann
modular flow at finite cutoff:\ the finite-cutoff period closure is
automatic for the chosen integer-spectrum generator, and deriving the
generator from a wedge-restricted state is open
(Remark~\ref{rem:bisognano_wichmann_reading}).

Three things this paper did do.  It made the spectral-triple framing
explicit and auditable.  It placed the framework in a published
mathematical lineage.  It made the open questions about $\alpha$, the
fiber rank, and the four-way coincidence into precise spectral-triple
questions instead of free-floating conjectures.
